"project:tpcc-mysql", "project:tpcc-postgres", "project:tpcds-kit", "project:tpch-kit"]}, "lines": {"paper:abrich2026blindspots": "- **[Finding Side-Effect Loopholes in Verifiable Rewards](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.09142 \"Finding Side-Effect Loopholes in Verifiable Rewards (2026)\")** (NeurIPS 2026 workshop \"Who Verifies the Agents?\"). Audits whether the shipped verifiers of two agent benchmarks, whose verdicts become RL rewards, notice a run that does the task and also makes an unrequested change: AppWorld's unit tests over the app database and WorkArena's form and catalog verifiers on a hosted ServiceNow instance (abstract; §1; §4.1). No model is called; AppWorld replays the shipped solutions and WorkArena runs the shipped drivers' `cheat` (§3; §7; App. H). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/abrich2026blindspots \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.09142) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09142)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:agarwal2021rliable": "- **[Deep Reinforcement Learning at the Edge of the Statistical Precipice](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2108.13264 \"Deep Reinforcement Learning at the Edge of the Statistical Precipice (2021)\")** ((rliable), NeurIPS 2021). Argues that comparisons of deep RL methods from a handful of runs per task must report uncertainty: stratified bootstrap confidence intervals, performance profiles, and the interquartile mean (IQM) as aggregate (Tab. 1; §4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/agarwal2021rliable \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2108.13264) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.13264) · [Code](https://github.com/google-research/rliable)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:agarwal2026fire": "- **[FIRE: Failure-Informed Runtime Engineering for Reliable Language-Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.26048 \"FIRE: Failure-Informed Runtime Engineering for Reliable Language-Model Agents (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Runtime policies for a terminal coding agent (the Codex CLI on Terminal-Bench 2.1): short natural-language instructions or action denials that the harness applies when a trajectory reaches a state that preceded observed failures, with model weights and the user prompt unchanged (abstract; §3; §4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/agarwal2026fire \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.26048) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26048)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a></span>", "paper:bertran2026compression": "- **[What Fits (Into Few Tokens) Doesn't Overfit: Compression and Generalization in ML Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.11045 \"What Fits (Into Few Tokens) Doesn't Overfit: Compression and Generalization in ML Research Agents (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Asks why reusing a validation set adaptively causes little overfitting, and tests the hypothesis that successful strategies are compressible on LLM research agents (Claude Opus in Claude Code) that edit and train models on 8 tasks, from tabular classification to language and diffusion modeling, scored on a separate holdout (abstract; §4, Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/bertran2026compression \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.11045) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11045)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:bhat2026interwhen": "- **[interwhen](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.11202 \"interwhen: A Generalizable Framework for Steering Reasoning Models with Test-time Verification (2026)\")**: A Generalizable Framework for Steering Reasoning Models with Test-time Verification (preprint 2026). Steers a single reasoning or agent trajectory with verifiers that run alongside generation: a forked copy of the model (on τ²-bench and VitaBench, a separate Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct, §5) periodically extracts verifiable states from the partial trace, and a violation goes back to the model as feedback (§1; §4.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/bhat2026interwhen \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.11202) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11202) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/interwhen)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:chang2026npo": "- **[Naive Prompt Optimization (NPO)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.27266 \"Naive Prompt Optimization: Rethinking the Need for Complex Prompt Search (2026)\")**: Naive Prompt Optimization: Rethinking the Need for Complex Prompt Search (preprint 2026). A single lineage of prompts revised by a teacher model from rollout feedback. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chang2026npo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.27266) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27266)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:chen2026noisefloor": "- **[Noise Floor Audit for Agent Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.22331 \"Noise Floor Audit for Agent Benchmarks (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Measures how much the score of a tool-calling endpoint moves between identical reruns at temperature 0 and under semantics-preserving surface changes to the user message (collapsed whitespace, or an added prefix, wrapper or suffix), on a frozen set of 150 matched instances from BFCL's AST-graded `multiple` and `parallel` categories (abstract; §3; Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026noisefloor \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.22331) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22331)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:cheng2024trace": "- **[Trace / OptoPrime](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.16218 \"Trace is the Next AutoDiff: Generative Optimization with Rich Feedback, Execution Traces, and LLMs (2024)\")**: Trace is the Next AutoDiff: Generative Optimization with Rich Feedback, Execution Traces, and LLMs (NeurIPS 2024). Optimizes general workflows (prompts, code, hyperparameters) from rich feedback such as console output or a user's responses (abstract) and execution errors (§1.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/cheng2024trace \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.16218) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16218) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/Trace)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:dang2026skiller": "- **[SKILLER](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.10538 \"SKILLER: Language-Level Reinforcement Learning for Reusable Skill Extraction in Small Language Models (2026)\")**: Language-Level Reinforcement Learning for Reusable Skill Extraction in Small Language Models (preprint 2026). A frontier model (GPT-5.4) acts as critic and actor to write and revise a task-specific agent skill for a frozen small model (Qwen3.5-9B or Qwen3.5-4B) running in the OpenCode agent loop, rewarded by each benchmark's official verifier; the authors call it reinforcement learning in natural language, since every signal is text and no weights change (abstract; § \"Method\", § \"Experimental Setup\"). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/dang2026skiller \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.10538) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10538) · [Code](https://github.com/DANG-ai/SKILLER)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:edy2026daedalus": "- **[DAEDALUS](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.08048 \"DAEDALUS: Bootstrapping Agent Memory from Self-Generated Tasks (2026)\")**: Bootstrapping Agent Memory from Self-Generated Tasks (preprint 2026). Builds an agent's memory with no training tasks and no oracle verifier: an Explorer proposes tasks with success conditions and tunes their difficulty, a Solver attempts them, and a heuristic written from each failure is kept only after the Solver then succeeds with it several times in a row; the kept heuristics are merged into one bank given to the agent at test time (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/edy2026daedalus \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.08048) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.08048) · [Code](https://github.com/illuin-tech/daedalus)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:gemmellgorriz2026externalising": "- **[Externalising Reasoning by Teaching Language Models to Use Programs as Tools](https://theses.gla.ac.uk/86006/3/2026GemmellG%C3%B3rrizPhD.pdf \"Externalising Reasoning by Teaching Language Models to Use Programs as Tools (2026)\")** ((ToolWriter, GRILLBot), PhD thesis, University of Glasgow, 2026). A PhD thesis (work from 2019–2023, §1.4) arguing that language models can write programs and use them as tools \"to externalise reasoning at scale\" on semi-structured data (§1.5, PDF p. 23): English-to-Python generation (Ch. 4–5), question-specific row-filter programs for question answering over tables (ToolWriter, Ch. 6, from EMNLP 2023), and one Python call per turn as the policy of the GRILLBot voice assistant (Ch. 7) (§1.7, PDF p. 27). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/gemmellgorriz2026externalising \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://theses.gla.ac.uk/86006/3/2026GemmellG%C3%B3rrizPhD.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.5525/gla.thesis.86006)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a></span>", "paper:guan2026continualskill": "- **[ContinualSkillBench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.03874 \"ContinualSkillBench: Can LLM Agents Truly Evolve Their Capabilities? (2026)\")**: Can LLM Agents Truly Evolve Their Capabilities? (preprint 2026). A benchmark of \"in-context continual skill learning\": five domains (Law, Finance, Healthcare, Mathematics, Office), each a stream of 100 tasks taken from existing benchmarks and ordered by difficulty and by LLM-judged skill-transfer dependencies, in which the agent may create or revise skills after each task's evaluator feedback (abstract; §3, §3.2, §3.5). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/guan2026continualskill \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.03874) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03874) · [Code](https://github.com/gtynnn060110-hash/continual-skill-bench-final)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:guo2026skilldisco": "- **[SKILL-DISCO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.26669 \"SKILL-DISCO: Distilling and Compiling Agent Traces into Reusable Procedural Skills (2026)\")**: Distilling and Compiling Agent Traces into Reusable Procedural Skills (preprint 2026). Distills the procedure shared by an agent's successful traces and compiles it into callable, executable skills (abstract): Python procedures, not prompts (§3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/guo2026skilldisco \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.26669) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26669)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:hajimiri2026worthtokens": "- **[Are Online Skill and Memory Modules Always Worth Their Tokens? A Budget-Constrained Study of Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.15017 \"Are Online Skill and Memory Modules Always Worth Their Tokens? A Budget-Constrained Study of Web Agents (2026)\")** (EMNLP 2026). Re-evaluates three online memory, workflow and skill modules for web agents (AWM, ASI, ReasoningBank) against a vanilla actor that spends a comparable token budget on more interaction steps instead (Vanilla-IB), counting the tokens of every module (abstract; §1; §3.2–3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/hajimiri2026worthtokens \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.15017) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15017)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:harrington2026continual": "- **[When Does Continual Learning Require Learning](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.07847 \"When Does Continual Learning Require Learning (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Compares ways to keep improving an LLM as its tasks change, under one staged protocol on Qwen3-8B: prompt optimization (GEPA, ACE), supervised updates (SFT, SDFT), reinforcement learning (GRPO, SDPO) and context compression (Cartridges, In-place TTT) (abstract; §3.2, Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/harrington2026continual \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.07847) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07847) · [Code](https://github.com/anneharrington/studying-cl)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:hsu2026grace": "- **[GRACE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.09175 \"Scoped Verification for Reliable Long-Horizon Agentic Context Evolution under Distribution Shift (2026)\")**: Scoped Verification for Reliable Long-Horizon Agentic Context Evolution under Distribution Shift (preprint 2026). Keeps an agent's persistent system instruction as a typed semantic graph, updated from operational experience while the model, tools and harness stay fixed (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/hsu2026grace \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.09175) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09175) · [Code](https://github.com/RedMind-Research/GRACE)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:hu2026toolrecovery": "- **[Quantization Effects on Tool-Failure Recovery Vary Across Prompts and Evaluation Designs](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.07781 \"Quantization Effects on Tool-Failure Recovery Vary Across Prompts and Evaluation Designs (2026)\")** (NeurIPS 2026 SLM-Agents workshop). Compares 8-bit and 4-bit GGUF variants of Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct as tool-calling agents recovering from one injected transient tool failure, on twenty deterministic order-management tasks with verified answers and five prompts (abstract; §4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/hu2026toolrecovery \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.07781) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07781)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:huang2026skillconsumption": "- **[From Raw Experience to Skill Consumption: A Systematic Study of Model-Generated Agent Skills](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23899 \"From Raw Experience to Skill Consumption: A Systematic Study of Model-Generated Agent Skills (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Measures whether one domain-level skill that an extractor model distills from a target model's own trajectories helps that target on held-out tasks, for five extractors, six targets and five agentic domains (ALFWorld, SpreadsheetBench, SWE-bench-Verified, SEAL-0, BFCL-v4 multi-turn) (abstract; §4.1, Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/huang2026skillconsumption \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23899) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23899) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/SkillLens)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:jang2026flatscore": "- **[Flat Score, Amplified Failures](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.27275 \"Flat Score, Amplified Failures: How the Error Budget Masks Damage in Quantized LLM Agents (2026)\")**: How the Error Budget Masks Damage in Quantized LLM Agents (preprint 2026). Multi-turn tool-calling agents on τ²-bench at 16-, 8- and 4-bit weights (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/jang2026flatscore \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.27275) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27275)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:kim2026skillsandbox": "- **[SkillSandbox](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.10088 \"SkillSandbox: Skill Verification via Dynamic Scenario Synthesis (2026)\")**: Skill Verification via Dynamic Scenario Synthesis (preprint 2026 (PDF p. 1 \"Preprint\")). Decides whether a skill an agent distilled from one task (a \"condition\" for when it applies and a \"principle\" to follow) enters the agent's library, by synthesizing new tasks and environments in which that condition arises and comparing runs with and without the skill (abstract; §3.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/kim2026skillsandbox \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.10088) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10088) · [Code](https://anonymous.4open.science/r/skillsandbox-647C/)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:lee2026metaharness": "- **[Meta-Harness](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.28052 \"Meta-Harness: End-to-End Optimization of Model Harnesses (2026)\")**: End-to-End Optimization of Model Harnesses (COLM 2026 style). Searches over harness code (what to store, retrieve and show the model). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lee2026metaharness \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.28052) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28052) · [Code](https://github.com/stanford-iris-lab/meta-harness-tbench2-artifact)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:li2026combee": "- **[Combee](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.04247 \"Combee: Scaling Parallel Prompt Learning for Self-Improving LLM Agents (2026)\")**: Scaling Parallel Prompt Learning for Self-Improving LLM Agents (COLM 2026). Runs ACE/GEPA-style prompt learning in parallel over many agent traces. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2026combee \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.04247) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04247)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:li2026progagent": "- **[ProgAgent: Learning Tool Orchestration through Programmatic Training](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.14774 \"ProgAgent: Learning Tool Orchestration through Programmatic Training (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Trains Qwen3-8B and 14B to call tools by writing Python programs that run in a persistent sandbox, keeping tool results in variables and printing only what they choose (programmatic tool calling, PTC), and compares this under one recipe with direct tool calling (DTC), where every tool result enters the context, and with a Hybrid of the two (abstract; §2.2; §3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2026progagent \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.14774) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14774) · [Code](https://github.com/hkust-nlp/ProgAgent)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/gym\">gym</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "paper:li2026skillsbench": "- **[SkillsBench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.12670 \"SkillsBench: Benchmarking How Well Agent Skills Work Across Diverse Tasks (2026)\")**: Benchmarking How Well Agent Skills Work Across Diverse Tasks (preprint 2026). A paired benchmark of agent skills: tasks across domains with curated skills and deterministic verifiers, each run with and without skills on many model–harness configurations (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2026skillsbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.12670) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.12670)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:lin2026ahe": "- **[Agentic Harness Engineering (AHE)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.25850 \"Agentic Harness Engineering: Observability-Driven Automatic Evolution of Coding-Agent Harnesses (2026)\")**: Agentic Harness Engineering: Observability-Driven Automatic Evolution of Coding-Agent Harnesses (preprint 2026). A closed loop that evolves coding-agent harnesses. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lin2026ahe \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.25850) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25850) · [Code](https://github.com/china-qijizhifeng/agentic-harness-engineering)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:luo2026unearned": "- **[unearned passes in agentic benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.34262 \"Maintaining Benchmarks Against Increasingly Capable Agents: Detection and Remediation of Unearned Passes (2026)\")**: Maintaining Benchmarks Against Increasingly Capable Agents: Detection and Remediation of Unearned Passes (preprint 2026). Audits passing agent trajectories to separate reward hacking from verifier weakness (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/luo2026unearned \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.34262) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34262)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:menaged2026automata": "- **[Can Agents Infer Environment from Interaction? Evidence from Agentic Automata Learning](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16576 \"Can Agents Infer Environment from Interaction? Evidence from Agentic Automata Learning (2026)\")** (preprint 2026 (under review at ICLR 2027, per the PDF header)). Tests whether tool-calling LLM agents can identify a hidden deterministic finite automaton (DFA) by asking an oracle membership queries (is this word accepted?) and equivalence queries (is this the target? if not, a counterexample word), within a query budget of twice what the better of the classic learners L* and TTT needs (abstract; §3; §4 \"Query budget\"). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/menaged2026automata \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16576) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16576) · [Code](https://github.com/reefmenaged/Agentic_Automata_Learning)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/gym\">gym</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a></span>", "paper:mittal2026rot": "- **[How Fast Do Agents Rot?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.01660 \"How Fast Do Agents Rot? An Empirical Study of Long-Horizon Degradation in LLM Agents for Production Decision-Making (2026)\")**: How Fast Do Agents Rot? An Empirical Study of Long-Horizon Degradation in LLM Agents for Production Decision-Making (preprint 2026). Measures agent success against task horizon on four task families, one a tool-use loop, five horizons and three context regimes (abstract), with success checked exactly by a ground-truth simulator (§3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/mittal2026rot \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.01660) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01660) · [Code](https://github.com/shubmittal/agent-horizon-degradation)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:moll2026grasp": "- **[GRASP: Gated Regression-Aware Skill Proposer for Self-Improving LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.29668 \"GRASP: Gated Regression-Aware Skill Proposer for Self-Improving LLM Agents (2026)\")** (EMNLP 2026). Improves an LLM agent through edits to a bounded library of skills (Markdown instructions injected into its context), with no weight updates: an LLM labels each failed trace with a failure mode, and a skill writer proposes add, modify or remove edits for the most frequent modes (abstract; §2.1–2.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/moll2026grasp \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.29668) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29668) · [Code](https://github.com/jomoll/GRASP)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:pai2026md5": "- **[Long-Horizon State Tracking in LLMs: Executing MD5 through a Deep Sequence of Dependent Tool Calls](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.00012 \"Long-Horizon State Tracking in LLMs: Executing MD5 through a Deep Sequence of Dependent Tool Calls (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Has gpt-oss-120b, an open mixture-of-experts model, compute an MD5 hash step by step through tools, carrying the four 32-bit state words in its own context across a canonical 196 dependent calls; every call is aligned to a from-scratch reference trace and the digest is checked to the bit (abstract; §4, §4.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/pai2026md5 \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.00012) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00012)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:pan2024feedbackloops": "- **[Feedback Loops With Language Models Drive In-Context Reward Hacking](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.06627 \"Feedback Loops With Language Models Drive In-Context Reward Hacking (2024)\")** (ICML 2024). When an LLM's outputs change the world it later reads, the feedback loop optimizes a proxy objective at test time and can raise harmful side effects with it, which the authors call in-context reward hacking (ICRH) (abstract; §1). Two mechanisms: output-refinement, where the LLM improves its own earlier outputs, and policy-refinement, where it changes how it acts after feedback such as API errors (§3.2, §4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/pan2024feedbackloops \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.06627) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06627)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:sarukkai2025selfgenerated": "- **[Self-Generated In-Context Examples Improve LLM Agents for Sequential Decision-Making Tasks](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00234 \"Self-Generated In-Context Examples Improve LLM Agents for Sequential Decision-Making Tasks (2025)\")** (preprint 2025). A ReAct-style agent keeps every trajectory that succeeds on a training task in a database and retrieves its nearest stored trajectories as in-context examples at each plan, reasoning and action step, starting from a few human-written ones (§2.2, §5.1); no weights change (App. C fine-tunes only as a side test). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sarukkai2025selfgenerated \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00234) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00234)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:satheesha2026rlmopt": "- **[RLMOpt](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.10471 \"RLMOpt: Adaptive Prompt Optimization via Recursive Language Models (2026)\")**: Adaptive Prompt Optimization via Recursive Language Models (preprint 2026). A recursive language model runs the search policy itself: inspects failures, allocates budget, decides when to stop. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/satheesha2026rlmopt \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.10471) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10471)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:shang2026vag": "- **[VaG (\"When Self-Evolution Backfires\")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.05810 \"When Self-Evolution Backfires: Pre-Commit Gating against Skill Contamination in LLM Agents (2026)\")**: When Self-Evolution Backfires: Pre-Commit Gating against Skill Contamination in LLM Agents (preprint 2026). Finds that a self-evolving agent that admits every skill it distills from its own trajectories improves, peaks and then degrades as the pool grows, and argues that removing a defective skill afterwards cannot undo the skills later distilled with it in context (abstract; Introduction; Methodology). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/shang2026vag \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.05810) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05810)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:shi2026skillkd": "- **[SKILL-KD: Contrastive Skill Distillation for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.28048 \"SKILL-KD: Contrastive Skill Distillation for LLM Agents (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Improves a frozen weaker student agent through a textual skill library written from a stronger teacher's runs: when the student fails a training task, the teacher runs the same task and a consolidation agent turns the gap between the two trajectories into a rule for the student's prompt (abstract; §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/shi2026skillkd \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.28048) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28048)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:shinn2023reflexion": "- **[Reflexion](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.11366 \"Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning (2023)\")**: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning (preprint 2023). The agent writes a verbal reflection after a failed attempt and keeps it in memory for the next attempt at the same task. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/shinn2023reflexion \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.11366) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.11366) · [Code](https://github.com/noahshinn/reflexion)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a></span>", "paper:singh2026casd": "- **[Coding Agents are Strong Prompt Optimizers](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.26261 \"Coding Agents are Strong Prompt Optimizers (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A coding agent analyzes a static corpus of trajectories with its own code and writes behavioral rules into the prompt. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/singh2026casd \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.26261) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26261)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:sundararaman2026flare": "- **[FLARE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.02919 \"FLARE: Few-shot Learning-based Adaptive Reflective Engine (2026)\")**: Few-shot Learning-based Adaptive Reflective Engine (preprint 2026). Reflective instruction optimization combined with few-shot reference examples. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sundararaman2026flare \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.02919) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02919) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/FLARE---Few-shot-Learning-based-Adaptive-Reflective-Engine)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:tan2025langprobe": "- **[LangProBe: a Language Program Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.20315 \"LangProBe: a Language Program Benchmark (2025)\")** (Findings of EMNLP 2025). A benchmark of language programs: DSPy programs from a single call and chain of thought to RAG, multi-hop retrieval, ReAct agents and generator–critic–ranker or –fuser pipelines (§3.2), crossed with four prompt optimizers (BootstrapFewShot, BootstrapFewShotRandomSearch, MIPROv2, and the authors' RuleInfer, §3.3) and six OpenAI and Llama models (§4), on 16 tasks in six categories, among them AppWorld, HumanEval, MATH, GSM8K, classification and question answering (§3.1; Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/tan2025langprobe \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.20315) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20315) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.findings-emnlp.1172) · [Code](https://github.com/Shangyint/langProBe)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a></span>", "paper:wan2026apiflowbench": "- **[APIFlow-Bench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.29128 \"APIFlow-Bench: Measuring Whether Agents Survive Long, Dependent API Workflows (2026)\")**: Measuring Whether Agents Survive Long, Dependent API Workflows (preprint 2026). A benchmark of long, dependent REST-API workflows in procedurally generated mock worlds, graded deterministically on both the backend state (a mock-minted canary traced through the call path) and a typed answer card (abstract; §3; §5). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wan2026apiflowbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.29128) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29128) · [Code](https://github.com/postmanlabs/APIFlow-Bench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a></span>", "paper:wang2023voyager": "- **[Voyager](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.16291 \"Voyager: An Open-Ended Embodied Agent with Large Language Models (2024)\")**: An Open-Ended Embodied Agent with Large Language Models (TMLR 2024). A GPT-4 agent in Minecraft that writes its actions as code and keeps the programs that work in an ever-growing skill library, indexed by the embedding of their description and retrieved for new tasks; an automatic curriculum proposes the next task (abstract; §2.1–2.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2023voyager \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.16291) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16291) · [Code](https://github.com/MineDojo/Voyager)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:wang2026assay": "- **[Not All Skills Help: Measuring and Repairing Agent Knowledge](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.15390 \"Not All Skills Help: Measuring and Repairing Agent Knowledge (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Curates an agent's natural-language skill library by measurement rather than LLM judgment: randomized masking on a small development set gives each skill a causal score per development task, from which it splits skills whose effect varies across tasks into conditional variants (an LLM rewrites them, behind a development-set gate), retires inert ones, merges near-duplicates, and at inference suppresses the skills predicted to hurt each test task, using its nearest development tasks (abstract; §2.1–2.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026assay \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.15390) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15390)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:wang2026compound": "- **[Do Agent Optimizers Compound?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.14004 \"Do Agent Optimizers Compound? A Continual-Learning Evaluation on Terminal-Bench 2.0 (2026)\")**: Do Agent Optimizers Compound? A Continual-Learning Evaluation on Terminal-Bench 2.0 (technical report 2026 (RELAI)). A two-phase continual-learning test on hard Terminal-Bench 2.0 tasks: optimize, then optimize again after new tasks arrive (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026compound \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.14004) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14004) · [Code](https://github.com/relai-ai/Continual-Learning-Terminal-Bench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:wang2026harnesseval": "- **[Rethinking the Evaluation of Harness Evolution for Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.12227 \"Rethinking the Evaluation of Harness Evolution for Agents (2026)\")** (preprint 2026 (v4)). Re-evaluates automatic harness evolution, where an LLM agent rewrites an agent's prompts, tools, memory and control code from task feedback (as Meta-Harness, AHE and AEVO do), against parallel sampling and sequential refinement at a matched budget, with and without unit-test feedback, on Terminal-Bench 2.1 with Claude Opus 4.6, GPT-5.4 and GPT-5.4 mini (abstract; §3; §4.1). It adds harness scaling, its own per-task variant that rewrites the harness for each task (§3.5). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026harnesseval \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.12227) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12227) · [Code](https://github.com/rethinking-harness-evolution/code)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:wang2026sage": "- **[SAGE (Statistical Acceptance Gate)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.36043 \"SAGE: A Statistical Acceptance Gate for Self-Evolving Agents (2026)\")**: SAGE: A Statistical Acceptance Gate for Self-Evolving Agents (preprint 2026). An acceptance gate for agents that evolve a skill document: instead of keeping any edit that raises the aggregate validation score, it compares the current and the edited skill item by item, weighs a regression more than a repair, caps the share of solved items an edit may break, and commits only when a one-sided exact binomial test on the discordant items rejects, abstaining otherwise (abstract; §3.2–3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026sage \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.36043) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36043)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:wu2026lowbit": "- **[Which Decisions Low-Bit Quantization Breaks, and How to Predict Them](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.06564 \"Which Decisions Low-Bit Quantization Breaks, and How to Predict Them (2026)\")** (preprint 2026 (v5)). Reads decisions of 16 open models at the first token, as the logit margin between two options (call a tool or reply in text, which tool, which answer, refuse or comply), before and after post-training quantization to 4, 3 and 2 bits under round-to-nearest, activation-aware scaling, GPTQ and GGUF, on BFCL, MetaTool, XSTest, MMLU, BoolQ, BBQ and authored items (abstract; §2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wu2026lowbit \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.06564) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06564)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:xie2026memo": "- **[MEMO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.09022 \"MEMO: Memory-Augmented Model Context Optimization for Robust Multi-Turn Multi-Agent LLM Games (2026)\")**: Memory-Augmented Model Context Optimization for Robust Multi-Turn Multi-Agent LLM Games (preprint 2026). Self-play that optimizes the inference-time context of agents in multi-turn, multi-agent text games (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/xie2026memo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.09022) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09022) · [Code](https://github.com/openverse-ai/MEMO)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:xu2026harnesslens": "- **[HarnessLens (\"Verify Smarter](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.27311 \"Verify Smarter, Evolve Further: Efficient Harness Evolution through Behavior-Aware Verification (2026)\")**: Verify Smarter, Evolve Further: Efficient Harness Evolution through Behavior-Aware Verification (Evolve Further\"), preprint 2026). Budget-aware evolution of agent harnesses (instructions, tools, runtime components) (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/xu2026harnesslens \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.27311) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27311) · [Code](https://github.com/jhxu5214/HarnessLens)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:yan2026skillglow": "- **[SkillGLoW (Global–Local Weave)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.02217 \"SkillGLoW: Procedural-Family Skill Consolidation for Self-Improving Agents on Long-Horizon Task Streams (2026)\")**: SkillGLoW: Procedural-Family Skill Consolidation for Self-Improving Agents on Long-Horizon Task Streams (preprint 2026). Organizes the skills a self-improving agent writes for itself by procedural family: the local skill each task writes from its own runs is clustered with others by how the task is solved, each cluster is compressed into one de-instantiated prior, and at solving time a recalled prior is combined with a local skill regenerated from the current task's own feedback (abstract; §3.1–3.5). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yan2026skillglow \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.02217) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02217)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:yang2026harnessadapt": "- **[Better Harnesses, Smaller Models: Building 90\\% Cheaper Agents via Automated Harness Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.08938 \"Better Harnesses, Smaller Models: Building 90\\% Cheaper Agents via Automated Harness Adaptation (2026)\")** (Smaller Models\"), preprint 2026). A frontier meta-agent (`gemini-3.1-pro-preview`) adapts the harness of a small open-weight model per task, so that it approaches a frontier model with a generic harness at a fraction of the cost; seven business-style agentic tasks, three small models (abstract; §IV-A; §IV-B). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026harnessadapt \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.08938) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08938) · [Code](https://github.com/malusamayo/slm-harness-adaptation)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:yang2026selfpot": "- **[How Much Can Language Models Gain from Test-Time Computation?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.01110 \"How Much Can Language Models Gain from Test-Time Computation? (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A benchmark and evaluation framework for test-time scaling across competition mathematics, AtCoder programming, AppWorld workflows and Terminal-Bench tasks, on a test set sealed before any test-time call (abstract; §2.4, Tab. 1). Each task is graded by a rule-based answer grader, hidden tests or the benchmark's own final-state evaluator (§2.5). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026selfpot \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.01110) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.01110)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:yang2026skillopt": "- **[SkillOpt](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23904 \"SkillOpt: Executive Strategy for Self-Evolving Agent Skills (2026)\")**: Executive Strategy for Self-Evolving Agent Skills (preprint 2026). Trains an agent's skill document like weights: bounded add/delete/replace edits accepted only on validation gains. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026skillopt \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23904) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23904)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:yao2022react": "- **[ReAct](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2210.03629 \"ReAct: Synergizing Reasoning and Acting in Language Models (2023)\")**: Synergizing Reasoning and Acting in Language Models (ICLR 2023). Interleaves reasoning traces with tool actions and observations. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yao2022react \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2210.03629) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.03629) · [Code](https://github.com/ysymyth/ReAct)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:yao2024taubench": "- **[τ-bench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.12045 \"$\\tau$-bench: A Benchmark for Tool-Agent-User Interaction in Real-World Domains (2025)\")**: $\\tau$-bench: A Benchmark for Tool-Agent-User Interaction in Real-World Domains (ICLR 2025). A benchmark of customer-service agents (retail and airline domains) that use database API tools, follow a written domain policy and talk to an LLM-simulated user; each task's hidden user instruction is written so that only one final database state is possible (abstract; §3; §4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yao2024taubench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.12045) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12045) · [Code](https://github.com/sierra-research/tau-bench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a></span>", "paper:yu2026rsebench": "- **[When Non-Adversarial Noise Hurts Learning: Benchmarking Skill Self-Evolution in Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19910 \"When Non-Adversarial Noise Hurts Learning: Benchmarking Skill Self-Evolution in Agents (2026)\")** (preprint 2026 (JD Future Academy template)). A benchmark of agents that distill experience into reusable skills, under ordinary noise injected at one of four points of the learning loop: the task instruction, the evidence the agent searches, the execution record the learner reads, and the pairing of each outcome with its record; tasks, answers and native scoring stay unchanged (abstract; §3.2, §3.4). Six benchmarks in four domains: spreadsheets, document question answering, ALFWorld and ordered task streams (§3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yu2026rsebench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19910) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19910)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/placebo\">placebo</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:yuan2026forge": "- **[FORGE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.15209 \"Failure-Guided Co-Evolution of Prompts and Training Data (2026)\")**: Failure-Guided Co-Evolution of Prompts and Training Data (preprint 2026). Co-evolves the prompts and the training data of an LM program whose model weights are frozen (§ \"Problem Formulation\"; the paper numbers no main-text sections). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yuan2026forge \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.15209) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15209)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:zeng2026hardening": "- **[Scalable Weak-to-Strong Verifier Hardening for Agent Environments](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.15924 \"Scalable Weak-to-Strong Verifier Hardening for Agent Environments (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Weaker LLMs automatically find and repair exploitable verifiers in agent environments from Terminal Bench, KernelBench and SETA, and stronger held-out models attack the result (abstract; §1; §4.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zeng2026hardening \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.15924) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15924)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a></span>", "paper:zeng2026locabench": "- **[LOCA-bench: Benchmarking Language Agents Under Controllable and Extreme Context Growth](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.07962 \"LOCA-bench: Benchmarking Language Agents Under Controllable and Extreme Context Growth (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A benchmark of tool-using agents under a context-length dial: code generators enlarge the environment state behind 15 seed tasks adapted from Toolathlon, served by local database-backed mock servers (Canvas, email, BigQuery, Snowflake, WooCommerce and others), from 8K to 256K tokens of \"environment description length\", \"while keeping the underlying task semantics fixed\" (abstract; §2.2–2.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zeng2026locabench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.07962) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07962) · [Code](https://github.com/hkust-nlp/LOCA-bench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/gym\">gym</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a></span>", "paper:zhang2025ace": "- **[ACE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.04618 \"Agentic Context Engineering: Evolving Contexts for Self-Improving Language Models (2026)\")**: Agentic Context Engineering: Evolving Contexts for Self-Improving Language Models (ICLR 2026). Contexts as evolving playbooks, built by a generator, reflector and curator. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2025ace \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.04618) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04618) · [Code](https://github.com/ace-agent/ace)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:zhao2023expel": "- **[ExpeL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.10144 \"ExpeL: LLM Agents Are Experiential Learners (2024)\")**: LLM Agents Are Experiential Learners (AAAI 2024). An agent gathers experience on training tasks by retrying them with Reflexion, then at test time adds natural-language insights drawn from that experience, and retrieved successful trajectories, to its ReAct prompt, without weight updates (§1, §4.1–§4.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhao2023expel \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.10144) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.10144) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v38i17.29936) · [Code](https://github.com/LeapLabTHU/ExpeL)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:zheng2026vpcontrol": "- **[Engineering Reliable Commit Gates for Agentic AI: Cost-Aware Verification Portfolios under Common-Mode Data Failures](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.10969 \"Engineering Reliable Commit Gates for Agentic AI: Cost-Aware Verification Portfolios under Common-Mode Data Failures (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A gate between an agent's proposed state-changing action and its execution chooses a verification plan (re-check on the same source, another verifier model, an independent read, a transactional guard, deferral to a person) from metadata a deployment can observe, calibrated to a risk target (abstract; §III). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zheng2026vpcontrol \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.10969) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10969) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33511441.v1) · [Code](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33511441.v1)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:zhong2026skilllearnbench": "- **[SkillLearnBench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.20087 \"SkillLearnBench: Benchmarking Continual Learning Methods for Agent Skill Generation on Real-World Tasks (2026)\")**: Benchmarking Continual Learning Methods for Agent Skill Generation on Real-World Tasks (preprint 2026). A benchmark for methods that have an LLM write agent skills from one seed instance of a task: 20 tasks, each with a deterministic pass/fail verifier, several instances that vary its parameters, and human-authored reference skills; every instance must be passed in at most half of 10 runs without skills and at least once by the same or a stronger LLM with the human-authored ones (abstract; §3.1, Eq. 1; §3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhong2026skilllearnbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.20087) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20087) · [Code](https://github.com/cxcscmu/SkillLearnBench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:zhu2026socratic": "- **[SSL (\"Socratic Skill Learning\")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17860 \"Socratic Skill Learning: Interleaving Teaching and Practice for Transferable Agent Skills (2026)\")**: Socratic Skill Learning: Interleaving Teaching and Practice for Transferable Agent Skills (preprint 2026). A Student model practises on one anchor task and writes an agent skill package itself while an Educator model reviews its work and the live package read-only and explains, challenges, diagnoses or stays silent; fresh solver sessions then use the frozen package on held-out members of the task's family, in the authors' SkillTransferBench (35 task families, 408 executable cases with private tests, six domains) (abstract; §3–4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhu2026socratic \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17860) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17860) · [Code](https://github.com/NJU-LINK/Socratic-Skill-Learning)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/agent\">agent</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "project:ace": "- **[ace](https://github.com/ace-agent/ace)**: implements [ACE](#/papers/zhang2025ace \"Agentic Context Engineering: Evolving Contexts for Self-Improving Language Models (2026)\")", "project:agent-horizon-degradation": "- **[agent-horizon-degradation](https://github.com/shubmittal/agent-horizon-degradation)**: implements [How Fast Do Agents Rot?](#/papers/mittal2026rot \"How Fast Do Agents Rot? **[vpcontrol](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33511441.v1)**: implements [Engineering Reliable Commit Gates…](#/papers/zheng2026vpcontrol \"Engineering Reliable Commit Gates for Agentic AI: Cost-Aware Verification Portfolios under Common-Mode Data Failures (2026)\")", "paper:alnegheimish2026prolong": "- **[Probing for Long-Horizon Deductive Reasoning Capabilities in Language Models with Prolog](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.11592 \"Probing for Long-Horizon Deductive Reasoning Capabilities in Language Models with Prolog (2026)\")** (Findings of EMNLP 2026). A synthetic benchmark of Prolog query answering, \"can the query be proven given the program, or not?\" (§1), whose reasoning depth runs from 0 to 22 while the context grows with it, since every rule and fact is needed in the proof (abstract; §2; §3.3); 8 reasoning models across 5 families, with output budgets of 64k tokens (82k for the Qwen models) (abstract; §4.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/alnegheimish2026prolong \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.11592) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11592) · [Code](https://github.com/halnegheimish/ProloNg)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/gym\">gym</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/longctx\">longctx</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a></span>", "paper:alrashed2026verification": "- **[Developing and Benchmarking Verification Algorithms to Improve Text-to-SQL Generation](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol19/p3732-alrashed.pdf \"Developing and Benchmarking Verification Algorithms to Improve Text-to-SQL Generation (2026)\")** (PVLDB 19(11) 2026). Develops and benchmarks verification algorithms that decide whether generated SQL answers the question. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/alrashed2026verification \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol19/p3732-alrashed.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3836663.3836721) · [Code](https://figshare.com/s/c88a751bc29d9751816b)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-data\">nl2dsl-data</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-select\">nl2dsl-select</a></span>", "paper:amster2026claimcheck": "- **[claimcheck: Measuring and Improving Specification Faithfulness](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17358 \"claimcheck: Measuring and Improving Specification Faithfulness (2026)\")** (preprint 2026 (PDF p. 1 footer: NeurIPS 2026 Workshop on AI for Verifiable Coding)). Checks whether a formal artifact means what its English requirement asks, a question no verifier asks: one model translates the artifact into English without seeing the requirement, and a second model, the judge, compares that text with the requirement (abstract; §3, Fig. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/amster2026claimcheck \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17358) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17358) · [Code](https://github.com/metareflection/claimcheck)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a></span>", "paper:bertsch2025oolong": "- **[Oolong](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.02817 \"Oolong: Evaluating Long Context Reasoning and Aggregation Capabilities (2026)\")**: Evaluating Long Context Reasoning and Aggregation Capabilities (COLM 2026). A long-context benchmark of aggregation questions with short, automatically scored answers: Oolong-synth fills inputs from 1K to 4M tokens with items from ten text-classification datasets, each given a user ID and a date, and asks for label counts, frequencies, and user and timeline relations (§2, Tab. 4); Oolong-real asks about dice rolls and spells in transcripts of one to 24 Dungeons & Dragons episodes, with fan-compiled statistics as gold answers (§3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/bertsch2025oolong \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.02817) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.02817) · [Code](https://github.com/abertsch72/oolong)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/gym\">gym</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/longctx\">longctx</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:bhaskar2023ambiqt": "- **[AmbiQT](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.13659 \"Benchmarking and Improving Text-to-SQL Generation under Ambiguity (2023)\")**: Benchmarking and Improving Text-to-SQL Generation under Ambiguity (EMNLP 2023). A text-to-SQL benchmark where each question has two plausible SQL readings, from lexical or structural ambiguity (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/bhaskar2023ambiqt \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.13659) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.13659) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.emnlp-main.436) · [Code](https://github.com/testzer0/AmbiQT)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:chen2026dbabench": "- **[DBA-Bench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.22165 \"DBA-Bench: A Production-Fidelity Benchmark for LLM-Based Database Operations Agents (2026)\")**: A Production-Fidelity Benchmark for LLM-Based Database Operations Agents (preprint 2026). A benchmark of LLM database-operations agents on live PostgreSQL and distributed TiDB deployments with active workloads, in single-task and persistent multi-event scenarios (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026dbabench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.22165) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22165)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:cheng2026scope": "- **[Scope Before You Persist](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.29144 \"Scope Before You Persist: Preventing Cross-Family Interference in Agent Memory (2026)\")**: Preventing Cross-Family Interference in Agent Memory (preprint 2026). A frozen model edits a 360-character skill policy for a frozen actor round by round, and a gate (ORC: public examples, private tests and metamorphic checks, each with a paired-bootstrap bound, no regression on earlier families) decides each edit; the authors then retrieve each accepted skill only for the task family that produced it (§1; §3; §4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/cheng2026scope \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.29144) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29144)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:cheng2026vera": "- **[VeRA: Renewing Reasoning Benchmarks with Executable Specifications](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.13217 \"VeRA: Renewing Reasoning Benchmarks with Executable Specifications (2026)\")** (preprint 2026 (v2, a rewrite of v1)).
