Turns each item of a reasoning benchmark into an executable task family, a question template, an input generator and a deterministic answer program, so that new labelled instances come from running code; VeRA-E draws fresh instances of the same family, VeRA-H modifies the family toward harder tasks, and VeRA-H Pro keeps the judge-ranked best of up to five validated proposals per seed (abstract; §2.1–2.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/cheng2026vera \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.13217) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13217) · [Code](https://github.com/Marco-Cheng/VeRA)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a></span>", "paper:cobbe2021verifiers": "- **[GSM8K and trained verifiers](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2110.14168 \"Training Verifiers to Solve Math Word Problems (2021)\")**: Training Verifiers to Solve Math Word Problems (preprint 2021). Introduces GSM8K and trains a verifier on final-answer correctness labels to rank sampled solutions (§4.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/cobbe2021verifiers \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2110.14168) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.14168) · [Code](https://github.com/openai/grade-school-math)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:ding2021dsb": "- **[DSB](#/papers/ding2021dsb \"DSB: a decision support benchmark for workload-driven and traditional database systems (2021)\")**: a decision support benchmark for workload-driven and traditional database systems (PVLDB 14(13) 2021). TPC-DS adapted for workload-driven systems: skewed, correlated data and new join-heavy templates. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/ding2021dsb \"Summary\") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3484224.3484234) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/dsb)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/workload\">workload</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:gao2026uniql": "- **[UniQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.08018 \"UniQL: Towards Dialect-Universal Benchmarking for Text-to-SQL (2026)\")**: Towards Dialect-Universal Benchmarking for Text-to-SQL (preprint 2026). Cross-dialect text-to-SQL: questions aligned with executable SQL in many dialects over shared schemas and data (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/gao2026uniql \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.08018) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08018) · [Code](https://github.com/JerryGao818/UniQL)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/difftest\">difftest</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/pairs\">pairs</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/rules-discover\">rules-discover</a></span>", "paper:golikov2026arbigraph": "- **[ArbiGraph: Composing Arbitrary Verifiable Tasks for Agent Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.20764 \"ArbiGraph: Composing Arbitrary Verifiable Tasks for Agent Evaluation (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A framework that composes problems with verifiable answers (math, predicting Python output, RLVE algorithmic tasks, HotpotQA questions) into graphs of dependent tasks: to get a task's input the agent reports its prerequisites' answers to a tool, which returns the intended input if they are correct and otherwise an alternative input whose verified answer differs, so no conversion between task types is needed (abstract; §2.1–2.2; §2.4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/golikov2026arbigraph \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.20764) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20764)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/gym\">gym</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/longctx\">longctx</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a></span>", "paper:hahn2026query4regex": "- **[Query4Regex](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-eacl.331.pdf \"Query4Regex: Verifiable Regex Transformation through Formal Operations from NL and DSL Queries (2026)\")**: Verifiable Regex Transformation through Formal Operations from NL and DSL Queries (Findings of EACL 2026). A benchmark of regex transformation: given source regexes and a query naming one to three classical operations (union, intersection, concatenation, complement, Kleene star, bounded repetition), in English or in a small operation DSL, an LLM writes the resulting regex, which counts as correct only if a DFA equivalence test finds it equivalent to the target (abstract; §1, §3.1–3.3, PDF pp. 1–3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/hahn2026query4regex \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-eacl.331.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.findings-eacl.331) · [Code](https://github.com/peer0/Query4Regex)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl\">nl2dsl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-regex\">dsl-regex</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:hogan2025qlang": "- **[Technical Report: Full-Stack Fine-Tuning for the Q Programming Language](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.06813 \"Technical Report: Full-Stack Fine-Tuning for the Q Programming Language (2025)\")** (preprint 2025 (Morgan Stanley and Prime Intellect)). A LeetCode-style benchmark for Q, the language of the kdb+ time-series database, which the authors call \"much less present on the Internet\" than mainstream languages (abstract; §2); frontier and base Qwen-2.5 models benchmarked on it (§4); Qwen-2.5 models from 1.5B to 32B adapted to Q by pretraining, supervised fine-tuning and RL (§5–7); models, code and data released (§1, §11). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/hogan2025qlang \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.06813) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.06813) · [Code](https://github.com/morganstanley/MSML)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rarelang\">rarelang</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:huo2025birdinteract": "- **[BIRD-INTERACT](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.05318 \"BIRD-INTERACT: Re-imagining Text-to-SQL Evaluation for Large Language Models via Lens of Dynamic Interactions (2026)\")**: Re-imagining Text-to-SQL Evaluation for Large Language Models via Lens of Dynamic Interactions (ICLR 2026). Multi-turn text-to-SQL: each database comes with a knowledge base, metadata files and a function-driven user simulator, so a model can ask for clarification, retrieve knowledge and recover from errors (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/huo2025birdinteract \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.05318) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05318) · [Code](https://github.com/bird-bench/BIRD-Interact)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl\">nl2dsl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:jiang2026higherorder": "- **[Language Models as Higher-Order Planning Formalizers](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.23844 \"Language Models as Higher-Order Planning Formalizers (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Asks whether LLM formalizers, which translate a natural-language planning problem into PDDL for a classical planner, keep scaling when a short description denotes a very large planning instance (\"unraveling problems\"), and proposes the higher-order formalizer: the LLM writes a program (Python in the experiments) that, when run, emits the PDDL problem file, which then goes to the planner (abstract; §1; §3.1; §3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/jiang2026higherorder \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.23844) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.23844) · [Code](https://github.com/oj79/formalizer-scaling)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:jin2025eltbench": "- **[ELT-Bench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.04808 \"ELT-Bench: An End-to-End Benchmark for Evaluating AI Agents on ELT Pipelines (2025)\")**: An End-to-End Benchmark for Evaluating AI Agents on ELT Pipelines (preprint 2025). An end-to-end benchmark of AI agents building Extract-Load-Transform pipelines (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/jin2025eltbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.04808) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04808) · [Code](https://github.com/uiuc-kang-lab/ELT-Bench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:jin2026annotation": "- **[Pervasive Annotation Errors Break Text-to-SQL Benchmarks and Leaderboards](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.08778 \"Pervasive Annotation Errors Break Text-to-SQL Benchmarks and Leaderboards (2026)\")** (PVLDB 19(5), 2026). Measures annotation error rates in BIRD Mini-Dev and Spider 2.0-Snow, with an LLM reviewer (SAR-Agent) whose flags SQL experts adjudicate, and re-ranks 16 open-source BIRD-leaderboard agents on a corrected BIRD Dev subset (abstract; §1, §4.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/jin2026annotation \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.08778) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08778) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3796195.3796206) · [Code](https://github.com/uiuc-kang-lab/text_to_sql_benchmarks)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-data\">nl2dsl-data</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:koh2026dnbench": "- **[Can LLMs Normalize Databases? A Benchmark and Multi-Agent Framework for Schema Normalization](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.11141 \"Can LLMs Normalize Databases? A Benchmark and Multi-Agent Framework for Schema Normalization (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A benchmark of LLM database normalization from 1NF to BCNF; it reports recurring failures in dependency inference, schema decomposition and inter-table constraint reconstruction (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/koh2026dnbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.11141) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11141)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:kothari2026arrivalbench": "- **[ArrivalBench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.02363 \"ArrivalBench: Agent-Generated Data Pipelines Are Correct Once and Wrong Under Time (2026)\")**: Agent-Generated Data Pipelines Are Correct Once and Wrong Under Time (NeurIPS 2026 workshop \"Who Verifies the Agents?\"). Re-runs the SQL pipelines LLM agents write, after the agent has stopped, under replayable delivery schedules (late, duplicated, out-of-order, retried and backfilled records), on 40 tasks and eleven models (abstract, §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/kothari2026arrivalbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.02363) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02363)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:lambert2024tulu": "- **[Tülu 3](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.15124 \"Tulu 3: Pushing Frontiers in Open Language Model Post-Training (2024)\")**: Tulu 3: Pushing Frontiers in Open Language Model Post-Training (preprint 2024). An open post-training recipe: SFT, preference tuning, then RL with verifiable rewards. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lambert2024tulu \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.15124) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.15124) · [Code](https://github.com/allenai/open-instruct)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:lei2023s3eval": "- **[S3Eval](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.15147 \"S3Eval: A Synthetic, Scalable, Systematic Evaluation Suite for Large Language Models (2024)\")**: A Synthetic, Scalable, Systematic Evaluation Suite for Large Language Models (NAACL 2024). Evaluates LLMs by SQL execution: given a randomly generated table and a random SQL query, the model must return the execution result (PDF §1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lei2023s3eval \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.15147) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.15147) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.naacl-long.69) · [Code](https://github.com/lfy79001/S3Eval)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/workload\">workload</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:lei2024spider2": "- **[Spider 2.0](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.07763 \"Spider 2.0: Evaluating Language Models on Real-World Enterprise Text-to-SQL Workflows (2025)\")**: Evaluating Language Models on Real-World Enterprise Text-to-SQL Workflows (ICLR 2025). Enterprise text-to-SQL workflows over real databases in local and cloud systems such as BigQuery and Snowflake (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lei2024spider2 \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.07763) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.07763) · [Code](https://github.com/xlang-ai/Spider2)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:lesort2026chess": "- **[Benchmarking Prompt Optimization of Large Language Models With Chess](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.00416 \"Benchmarking Prompt Optimization of Large Language Models With Chess (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A benchmark of 1,118 Lichess puzzles for prompt optimization of frozen LLMs (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lesort2026chess \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.00416) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00416)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a></span>", "paper:li2022alphacode": "- **[AlphaCode](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.07814 \"Competition-Level Code Generation with AlphaCode (2022)\")**: Competition-Level Code Generation with AlphaCode (Science 2022). Competitive programming: an encoder-decoder transformer, pre-trained on GitHub code and fine-tuned on the authors' CodeContests dataset, samples up to millions of C++ and Python programs per problem; programs that fail the problem statement's example tests are dropped, and the rest are clustered by their outputs on inputs written by a separate test-input model, choosing at most 10 submissions (abstract; §4.4–4.6). Evaluated on CodeContests and in simulated Codeforces contests (§5.1–5.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2022alphacode \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.07814) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.07814) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.abq1158) · [Code](https://github.com/google-deepmind/code_contests)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/cex-search\">cex-search</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:li2023bird": "- **[BIRD](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.03111 \"Can LLM Already Serve as A Database Interface? A BIg Bench for Large-Scale Database Grounded Text-to-SQLs (2023)\")**: Can LLM Already Serve as A Database Interface? A BIg Bench for Large-Scale Database Grounded Text-to-SQLs (NeurIPS 2023 (Datasets and Benchmarks)). A large text-to-SQL benchmark over big, dirty real-world databases, with external-knowledge evidence. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2023bird \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.03111) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.03111) · [Code](https://github.com/AlibabaResearch/DAMO-ConvAI)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:li2025swesql": "- **[SWE-SQL (BIRD-CRITIC)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.18951 \"SWE-SQL: Illuminating LLM Pathways to Solve User SQL Issues in Real-World Applications (2025)\")**: SWE-SQL: Illuminating LLM Pathways to Solve User SQL Issues in Real-World Applications (NeurIPS 2025). BIRD-CRITIC: a benchmark of debugging SQL issues, distilled from real user issues and replayed in new environments, with a PostgreSQL set and a multi-dialect set (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2025swesql \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.18951) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.18951) · [Code](https://github.com/bird-bench/BIRD-CRITIC-1)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:li2025tokdrift": "- **[TokDrift: When LLM Speaks in Subwords but Code Speaks in Grammar](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.14972 \"TokDrift: When LLM Speaks in Subwords but Code Speaks in Grammar (2025)\")** (preprint 2025). Measures whether code LLMs give the same test-checked result when the input program is rewritten in ways that keep its meaning but change how the tokenizer cuts it: six naming rules that change identifier casing and eighteen spacing rules that add one space next to an operator, applied one at a time to eight Java and Python benchmarks for bug fixing, code summarization and code translation, with nine open models of three series and sizes (§3; Tab. 1–3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2025tokdrift \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.14972) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14972) · [Code](https://github.com/uw-swag/tokdrift)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:li2026datalogbench": "- **[DatalogBench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.37233 \"DatalogBench: Evaluating Large Language Models on Text-to-Datalog Synthesis (2026)\")**: Evaluating Large Language Models on Text-to-Datalog Synthesis (preprint 2026). A benchmark of text-to-Datalog synthesis in the Soufflé dialect: each task gives a natural-language question and the input and output relation signatures, and a candidate program counts only if it compiles and reproduces the expected output on every held-out evaluation variant (abstract; §3; §5.2). Evaluates LLMs prompted directly, with and without relation descriptions and an input–output example, and coding agents that get execution feedback (abstract; §5.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2026datalogbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.37233) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37233) · [Code](https://github.com/ZJU-PL/DatalogBench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-datalog\">dsl-datalog</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:lin2025dlbench": "- **[DLBench](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/stampPDF/getPDF.jsp?tp=&arnumber=11334627 \"DLBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for SQL Translation with Large Language Models (2025)\")**: A Comprehensive Benchmark for SQL Translation with Large Language Models (ASE 2025). A benchmark for LLM SQL dialect translation from SQLite and other sources into six engines (MySQL, PostgreSQL, MariaDB, MonetDB, DuckDB, ClickHouse) (§I, §IV). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lin2025dlbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/stampPDF/getPDF.jsp?tp=&arnumber=11334627) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/ase63991.2025.00076) · [Code](https://github.com/dlbenchll/DLBench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/pairs\">pairs</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:lin2025goedelproverv2": "- **[Goedel-Prover-V2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.03613 \"Goedel-Prover-V2: Scaling Formal Theorem Proving with Scaffolded Data Synthesis and Self-Correction (2025)\")**: Scaling Formal Theorem Proving with Scaffolded Data Synthesis and Self-Correction (preprint 2025). Open Lean 4 prover models trained by expert iteration and RL (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lin2025goedelproverv2 \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.03613) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.03613) · [Code](https://github.com/Goedel-LM/Goedel-Prover-V2)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:liu2023evalplus": "- **[EvalPlus](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.01210 \"Is Your Code Generated by ChatGPT Really Correct? Rigorous Evaluation of Large Language Models for Code Generation (2023)\")**: Is Your Code Generated by ChatGPT Really Correct? Rigorous Evaluation of Large Language Models for Code Generation (NeurIPS 2023). Augments code benchmarks with generated tests (HumanEval+: 80× the tests of HumanEval) (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liu2023evalplus \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.01210) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.01210) · [Code](https://github.com/evalplus/evalplus)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/cex-search\">cex-search</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:liu2026plsqlbench": "- **[PLSQLBench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.15931 \"PLSQLBench: Benchmarking LLM Systems for Executable Procedural Database Programming (2026)\")**: Benchmarking LLM Systems for Executable Procedural Database Programming (preprint 2026). A benchmark of writing executable PL/SQL, scored by execution-based tests (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liu2026plsqlbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.15931) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15931) · [Code](https://github.com/oracle-samples/plsqlbench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl\">nl2dsl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:long2026sqlrewritebench": "- **[SQL-RewriteBench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.09251 \"SQL-RewriteBench: A Correctness-Gated, Full-Denominator Benchmark for Statement-Level SQL Rewriting (2026)\")**: A Correctness-Gated, Full-Denominator Benchmark for Statement-Level SQL Rewriting (preprint Jul 2026). Executable rewrite instances in EQUIV / PERF / ROBUST / PG-aware pools, with *correctness gating*. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/long2026sqlrewritebench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.09251) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09251) · [Code](https://github.com/SQL-RewriteBench/benchmark)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/pairs\">pairs</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/rewrite-eval\">rewrite-eval</a></span>", "paper:lyu2026text2graphquery": "- **[Text2GraphQuery-Bench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.11745 \"Text2GraphQuery-Bench: A Text to Graph Query Benchmark (2026)\")**: A Text to Graph Query Benchmark (preprint 2026). A text-to-graph-query benchmark with parallel gold queries in Cypher, GQL and SQL/PGQ, each question written at three abstraction levels and, in one test, with schema names replaced by LLM-made aliases; it scores frontier and open-weight LLMs, zero-shot, three-shot and (the open-weight ones) LoRA-fine-tuned, on parse validity, GLEU, Jaro-Winkler similarity and execution accuracy (abstract; §4.3; §6; §7.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lyu2026text2graphquery \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.11745) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11745) · [Code](https://github.com/ldbc/Text2GraphQuery-DataGen)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rarelang\">rarelang</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-graph\">dsl-graph</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-data\">nl2dsl-data</a></span>", "paper:mahassadi2026redos": "- **[An Empirical Analysis of ReDoS Vulnerabilities and ReDoS Detection Tools](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.10294 \"An Empirical Analysis of ReDoS Vulnerabilities and ReDoS Detection Tools (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). An empirical study of ReDoS (regular-expression denial of service) in two parts: CVEs from the NVD that report ReDoS, compared with all other CVEs on severity attributes, evidence of exploitation, exploitability scores, vendors and weakness labels (§4); and five ReDoS detectors (Saferegex, Rescue, ReDoSHunter, Revealer, RAT) and one repair tool (RegexScalpel) run on regexes from RegexLib and from the vulnerable and patched code of ReDoS CVEs in CVEfixes (§3, §5). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/mahassadi2026redos \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.10294) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10294) · [Code: CVE-ReDoS-Analysis](https://github.com/JustinVallee/CVE-ReDoS-Analysis) · [Code: ReDoS-Evaluation-Pipeline](https://github.com/ISMAELYVANN/ReDoS-Evaluation-Pipeline)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-regex\">dsl-regex</a></span>", "paper:mandarapu2026divergence": "- **[Same Pattern](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.23032 \"Same Pattern, Different Answer: A Reference Semantics and Divergence Map for GQL and SQL/PGQ Path Patterns (2026)\")**: Same Pattern, Different Answer: A Reference Semantics and Divergence Map for GQL and SQL/PGQ Path Patterns (Different Answer (GQL and SQL/PGQ path patterns), preprint 2026 (a PVLDB-format submission copy is in the artifact repository)). Measures what graph engines return for path patterns whose meaning the two ISO standards GQL and SQL/PGQ fix in their shared core, GPML: a 17-construct suite on six releases of five products (Kùzu, DuckPGQ, two Neo4j releases, Memgraph, Apache AGE), each cell scored as conforming, diverging, rejected or inexpressible (abstract; §4–5). The authors develop one of the engines they measure (Samyama-Graph) and keep its row out of every statistic (§8). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/mandarapu2026divergence \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.23032) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23032) · [Code](https://github.com/samyama-ai/gpml-conformance)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dialect\">dialect</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/difftest\">difftest</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-graph\">dsl-graph</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nondet-eval\">nondet-eval</a></span>", "paper:northcutt2021labelerrors": "- **[Pervasive Label Errors in Test Sets Destabilize Machine Learning Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2103.14749 \"Pervasive Label Errors in Test Sets Destabilize Machine Learning Benchmarks (2021)\")** (NeurIPS 2021 (Datasets and Benchmarks)). Finds label errors in the test sets of 10 common image, text and audio datasets, among them ImageNet, CIFAR-10, IMDB and AudioSet: confident learning flags likely errors from a model's out-of-sample predicted probabilities, and five Mechanical Turk workers judge each flagged example, or a random sample of them for QuickDraw and Amazon Reviews (abstract; §3; §4; Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/northcutt2021labelerrors \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2103.14749) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.14749)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:opsahlong2024mipro": "- **[MIPRO (shipped in DSPy as MIPROv2)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.11695 \"Optimizing Instructions and Demonstrations for Multi-Stage Language Model Programs (2024)\")**: Optimizing Instructions and Demonstrations for Multi-Stage Language Model Programs (EMNLP 2024). Optimizes instructions and few-shot demonstrations of every module in a multi-stage LM program. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/opsahlong2024mipro \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.11695) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11695) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.emnlp-main.525)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a></span>", "paper:pei2026rose": "- **[ROSE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.12988 \"ROSE: An Intent-Centered Evaluation Metric for NL2SQL (2026)\")**: An Intent-Centered Evaluation Metric for NL2SQL (ACL 2026). An intent-centered NL2SQL metric meant to replace execution accuracy; an execution match still decides which LLM stage judges the prediction (§4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/pei2026rose \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.12988) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12988) · [Code](https://github.com/CedricPei/ROSE)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:perlitz2026polysql": "- **[PolySQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.07796 \"PolySQL: Scaling Text-to-SQL Evaluation Across SQL Dialects via Automated Backend Isomorphism (2026)\")**: Scaling Text-to-SQL Evaluation Across SQL Dialects via Automated Backend Isomorphism (preprint 2026). Evaluates text-to-SQL across SQL dialects by running each side on its own engine and comparing normalized results. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/perlitz2026polysql \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.07796) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07796) · [Code](https://github.com/IBM/polysql)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:preslermarshall2021sqlrepair": "- **[SQLRepair](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2102.05729 \"SQLRepair: Identifying and Repairing Mistakes in Student-Authored SQL Queries (2021)\")**: Identifying and Repairing Mistakes in Student-Authored SQL Queries (ICSE-SEET 2021). Collects the SQL queries that undergraduates wrote for programming-by-example problems, each given as (source, destination) table pairs that a query must turn one into the other, and classifies their syntax and semantic errors (abstract; §II; §IV-A). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/preslermarshall2021sqlrepair \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2102.05729) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.05729) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICSE-SEET52601.2021.00030) · [Code](https://github.com/kpresler/SQLRepair)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/smt\">smt</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:ren2025deepseekproverv2": "- **[DeepSeek-Prover-V2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.21801 \"DeepSeek-Prover-V2: Advancing Formal Mathematical Reasoning via Reinforcement Learning for Subgoal Decomposition (2025)\")**: Advancing Formal Mathematical Reasoning via Reinforcement Learning for Subgoal Decomposition (preprint 2025). Lean 4 provers (671B, abstract; 7B, §2.3) trained with RL after a cold start (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/ren2025deepseekproverv2 \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.21801) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21801) · [Code](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-Prover-V2)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:saparina2024ambrosia": "- **[AMBROSIA](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.19073 \"AMBROSIA: A Benchmark for Parsing Ambiguous Questions into Database Queries (2024)\")**: A Benchmark for Parsing Ambiguous Questions into Database Queries (NeurIPS 2024 (Datasets and Benchmarks)). A benchmark of ambiguous questions (scope, attachment, vagueness) with their interpretations and a SQL query for each (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/saparina2024ambrosia \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.19073) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.19073) · [Code](https://github.com/saparina/ambrosia)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:sharma2026esolang": "- **[EsoLang-Bench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.09678 \"EsoLang-Bench: Evaluating Genuine Reasoning in Large Language Models via Esoteric Programming Languages (2026)\")**: Evaluating Genuine Reasoning in Large Language Models via Esoteric Programming Languages (preprint 2026). A benchmark of 80 programming problems in four difficulty tiers, each to be solved in five Turing-complete esoteric languages (Brainfuck, Befunge-98, Whitespace, Unlambda, Shakespeare) that the authors choose for their scarcity on GitHub, with the same problems run in Python and JavaScript as an in-distribution baseline (abstract; §3; §5.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sharma2026esolang \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.09678) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09678) · [Code](https://github.com/Lossfunk/EsolangBench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rarelang\">rarelang</a></span>", "paper:singh2024sqlequiquest": "- **[Can the Rookies Cut the Tough Cookie? Exploring the Use of LLMs for SQL Equivalence Checking](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.05561 \"Can the Rookies Cut the Tough Cookie? Exploring the Use of LLMs for SQL Equivalence Checking (2024)\")** (preprint, 2024). A harder, realistic equivalence benchmark; most LLMs are biased towards \"equivalent\", GPT-4 excepted (§5). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/singh2024sqlequiquest \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.05561) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05561) · [Code](https://github.com/rajatb115/LLMs-for-SQL-Equivalence-Checking)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/pairs-check\">pairs-check</a></span>", "paper:singhal2026ctcbench": "- **[No More Free Lunch: Corpus Task Complexity Matters as Corpora Grow](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.29245 \"No More Free Lunch: Corpus Task Complexity Matters as Corpora Grow (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Defines corpus task complexity (CTC), how the number of calls to a small-context oracle needed to solve a task grows with corpus size; classes 12 retrieval and long-context tasks as growing linearly (\"low CTC\") and adds 10 that grow quadratically or faster (\"high CTC\", such as finding all contradicting claim pairs, matching questions to documents, rarest-topic outliers, reordering), the 22-task CTC-Bench (abstract; §2, Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/singhal2026ctcbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.29245) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29245) · [Code](https://github.com/PrasannS/corpustaskcomplexity)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/gym\">gym</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/longctx\">longctx</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:sinha2025longhorizon": "- **[The Illusion of Diminishing Returns](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.09677 \"The Illusion of Diminishing Returns: Measuring Long Horizon Execution in LLMs (2026)\")**: Measuring Long Horizon Execution in LLMs (ICLR 2026). Measures long-horizon execution with the knowledge and plan given, so only execution can fail (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sinha2025longhorizon \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.09677) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09677) · [Code](https://github.com/long-horizon-execution/measuring-execution)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:sinha2025refute": "- **[REFUTE (\"Can Language Models Falsify?\")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.19414 \"Can Language Models Falsify? Evaluating Algorithmic Reasoning with Counterexample Creation (2025)\")**: Can Language Models Falsify? Evaluating Algorithmic Reasoning with Counterexample Creation (preprint 2025). A benchmark of LLMs creating counterexamples for subtly incorrect algorithmic solutions, checked by running code (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sinha2025refute \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.19414) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19414) · [Code](https://github.com/falsifiers/REFUTE)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/pairs-prog\">pairs-prog</a></span>", "paper:sobal2026staminabench": "- **[StaminaBench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.19613 \"StaminaBench: Stress-Testing Coding Agents over 100 Interaction Turns (2026)\")**: Stress-Testing Coding Agents over 100 Interaction Turns (preprint 2026). A benchmark of how many consecutive change requests a coding agent survives: the agent implements a REST API server and then modifies it through procedurally generated schema changes, up to 100 turns (abstract; §3.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sobal2026staminabench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.19613) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19613) · [Code](https://github.com/amazon-science/StaminaBench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a></span>", "paper:su2026gqlbench": "- **[GQLBench](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1476.pdf, \"GQLBench: A Large-Scale Cross-Domain, Cross-Dialect Benchmark for NL2GQL (2026)\")**: A Large-Scale Cross-Domain, Cross-Dialect Benchmark for NL2GQL (ACL 2026 (Volume 1: Long Papers)). A benchmark of writing graph-database queries from English questions (NL2GQL) in two dialects, Cypher and NebulaGraph's GQL, which the authors use \"as the implementation of ISO GQL\", scored by execution over hundreds of graph databases; it evaluates eight LLMs zero-shot and four open models after LoRA fine-tuning, with breakdowns by graph pattern, return form and advanced function (abstract, PDF p. 1; footnote 2, PDF p. 2; §6.5–6.7, PDF pp. 7–9). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/su2026gqlbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1476.pdf,) · [Code](https://github.com/qxssadf/GQLBench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/difftest\">difftest</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rarelang\">rarelang</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-graph\">dsl-graph</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-data\">nl2dsl-data</a></span>", "paper:sun2025dysqlbench": "- **[DySQL-Bench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.26495 \"Rethinking Text-to-SQL: Dynamic Multi-turn SQL Interaction for Real-world Database Exploration (2025)\")**: Rethinking Text-to-SQL: Dynamic Multi-turn SQL Interaction for Real-world Database Exploration (preprint 2025). A benchmark of dynamic, multi-turn text-to-SQL over 13 SQLite databases from BIRD and Spider 2: an LLM-simulated user (Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct) follows a task instruction over several turns, and the model under test talks to it and runs SQL, including INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE (abstract; §IV-D; §V). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sun2025dysqlbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.26495) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26495) · [Code](https://github.com/Aurora-slz/Real-World-SQL-Bench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:tan2024judgebench": "- **[JudgeBench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.12784 \"JudgeBench: A Benchmark for Evaluating LLM-based Judges (2025)\")**: A Benchmark for Evaluating LLM-based Judges (ICLR 2025). A benchmark of LLM judges on response pairs whose correctness is objectively checkable. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/tan2024judgebench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.12784) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12784) · [Code](https://github.com/ScalerLab/JudgeBench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a></span>", "paper:thompson2024rango": "- **[Rango](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.14063 \"Rango: Adaptive Retrieval-Augmented Proving for Automated Software Verification (2025)\")**: Adaptive Retrieval-Augmented Proving for Automated Software Verification (ICSE 2025). Coq proof synthesis for software verification with a fine-tuned LLM that retrieves relevant premises and similar proofs from the current project at every step (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/thompson2024rango \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.14063) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14063) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICSE55347.2025.00161) · [Code](https://github.com/rkthomps/coq-modeling)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/prove\">prove</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:vejvar2025spider4ssc": "- **[Spider4SSC & S2CLite](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.09354 \"Spider4SSC \\& S2CLite: A text-to-multi-query-language dataset using lightweight ontology-agnostic SPARQL to Cypher parser (2025)\")**: Spider4SSC \\& S2CLite: A text-to-multi-query-language dataset using lightweight ontology-agnostic SPARQL to Cypher parser (preprint 2025). S2CLite, a purely rule-based SPARQL-to-Cypher translator that needs neither the RDF graph nor external tools, compared with S2CTrans on BSBM42 and Spider4SPARQL; and Spider4SSC, a dataset pairing natural-language questions with SQL, SPARQL and Cypher queries over Spider's databases (abstract; §2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/vejvar2025spider4ssc \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.09354) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09354) · [Code](https://github.com/vejvarm/S2CLite)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/difftest\">difftest</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl\">nl2dsl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/pairs\">pairs</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-graph\">dsl-graph</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sparql\">dsl-sparql</a></span>", "paper:verbruggen2026jqbench": "- **[jqBench](https://openreview.net/pdf?id=VStKtgGXUc \"jqBench: A Benchmark for Reading and Editing JSON from Natural Language and/or Examples (2026)\")**: A Benchmark for Reading and Editing JSON from Natural Language and/or Examples (ICLR 2026). A benchmark of writing jq expressions (jq: a command-line language that queries and transforms JSON) from English and/or input–output examples, in two parts: jqStack, distilled from Stack Overflow questions tagged jq, and jqSpider, adapted from Spider's text-to-SQL tasks with JSON databases and JSON Schemas; LLMs are scored with automatic compile, run and test feedback, with a code-running tool instead, with documentation tools, writing Python instead of jq, and from examples alone (abstract; §1, PDF pp. 1–2; §3.2–3.3, PDF pp. 6–9). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/verbruggen2026jqbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://openreview.net/pdf?id=VStKtgGXUc) · [Code](https://github.com/PidgeyUsedGust/jqBench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl\">nl2dsl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rarelang\">rarelang</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-path\">dsl-path</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:wang2026catch": "- **[CATCH](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.39533 \"CATCH: A Controllable Analysis Testbed for Reward Hacking in Coding RL (2026)\")**: A Controllable Analysis Testbed for Reward Hacking in Coding RL (preprint 2026). A testbed for reward hacking in coding RL: it deliberately exposes loopholes in its own environment and labels hacking by comparing success under a vulnerable evaluator with task correctness under an independent audit (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026catch \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.39533) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39533) · [Code](https://github.com/THUAIS-Lab/CATCH)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:wang2026contratable": "- **[ContraTable (\"The Table Says Otherwise\")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.23667 \"The Table Says Otherwise: Testing LLMs with Counterfactual Relational Data (2026)\")**: The Table Says Otherwise: Testing LLMs with Counterfactual Relational Data (VLDB 2026 Workshop (NOVAS)). Tests whether LLMs answer from the relational tables they are given or from facts learned in pretraining (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026contratable \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.23667) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23667) · [Code](https://github.com/AuroraWXZ/LLM_understand_table)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a></span>", "paper:wang2026heuristictrap": "- **[Formalize, Don't Optimize](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.12421 \"Formalize, Don't Optimize: The Heuristic Trap in LLM-Generated Combinatorial Solvers (2026)\")**: The Heuristic Trap in LLM-Generated Combinatorial Solvers ((the heuristic trap in LLM-generated combinatorial solvers; CP-SynC-XL), preprint 2026). Asks how an LLM should write a solver for a combinatorial problem given in natural language, and whether it should also try to speed up the search: three frontier LLMs write a reusable solver as plain Python search, as a model for the OR-Tools CP-SAT solver through its Python API, or as a MiniZinc model run on the same CP-SAT, from a baseline prompt that asks for correctness and a heuristic prompt that also asks for efficiency (abstract; §1; §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026heuristictrap \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.12421) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12421)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:wei2025codearc": "- **[CodeARC: Benchmarking Reasoning Capabilities of LLM Agents for Inductive Program Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.23145 \"CodeARC: Benchmarking Reasoning Capabilities of LLM Agents for Inductive Program Synthesis (2025)\")** (COLM 2025 (per the arXiv v2 PDF's running header)). A benchmark of inductive program synthesis (programming by example) for LLM agents: hidden Python functions taken from HumanEval+, MBPP+ and APPS, shown only through input-output examples, with their original names kept or replaced by a generic one (abstract; §3.3; §4, Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wei2025codearc \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.23145) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23145) · [Code](https://github.com/Anjiang-Wei/CodeARC)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a></span>", "paper:wei2025equibench": "- **[EquiBench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.12466 \"EquiBench: Benchmarking Large Language Models' Reasoning about Program Semantics via Equivalence Checking (2025)\")**: Benchmarking Large Language Models' Reasoning about Program Semantics via Equivalence Checking (EMNLP 2025). Program-pair benchmark across 4 languages testing LLM reasoning about equivalence. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wei2025equibench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.12466) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12466) · [Code](https://github.com/Anjiang-Wei/EquiBench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/pairs-prog\">pairs-prog</a></span>", "paper:xu2026uojbench": "- **[UOJ-Bench (\"Beyond Problem Solving\")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.12864 \"Beyond Problem Solving: UOJ-Bench for Evaluating Code Generation, Hacking, and Repair in Competitive Programming (2026)\")**: Beyond Problem Solving: UOJ-Bench for Evaluating Code Generation, Hacking, and Repair in Competitive Programming (ICML 2026 (PMLR 306, per the PDF's first-page footer)). A benchmark of three competitive-programming tasks built from real submissions to the Universal Online Judge (UOJ): code generation, code hacking (write an input that breaks a buggy solution) and code repair, all graded by UOJ's own judge through its API (abstract; §1; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/xu2026uojbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.12864) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12864) · [Code](https://github.com/hehezhou/UOJ-Bench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:yang2023leandojo": "- **[LeanDojo](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.15626 \"LeanDojo: Theorem Proving with Retrieval-Augmented Language Models (2023)\")**: Theorem Proving with Retrieval-Augmented Language Models (NeurIPS 2023 (Datasets and Benchmarks)). An open toolkit that extracts proof data from Lean and lets programs interact with it, plus ReProver, a retrieval-augmented prover (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yang2023leandojo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.15626) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.15626) · [Code: LeanDojo](https://github.com/lean-dojo/LeanDojo) · [Code: ReProver](https://github.com/lean-dojo/ReProver)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:yang2025reducefix": "- **[Input Reduction Enhanced LLM-based Program Repair](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.15251 \"Input Reduction Enhanced LLM-based Program Repair (2025)\")** (ICSE 2026 (per the PDF's running header), 2025). LLM program repair when the failing test input is long: an LLM writes, once per task, a delta-debugging (ddmin) script that shrinks the failure-inducing input while the buggy program and a correct reference still print different outputs, and the repair prompt carries the reduced input instead of the full one (abstract; §3.1–3.4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yang2025reducefix \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.15251) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15251)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/longctx\">longctx</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/reduce\">reduce</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:yang2026codewithimage": "- **[Self-Evolving Code-with-Image Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.11292 \"Self-Evolving Code-with-Image Reasoning (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Visual questions whose exact answers need a multi-step computation over the pixels, solved by a frozen multimodal model given only a Python interpreter (\"Code-with-Image\"), on the authors' CwI-Bench of generated task families with labels correct by construction and disjoint train, validation and test splits (abstract; §3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026codewithimage \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.11292) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11292)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:ye2025longproc": "- **[LongProc (\"Long Procedural Generation\")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.05414 \"LongProc: Benchmarking Long-Context Language Models on Long Procedural Generation (2025)\")**: LongProc: Benchmarking Long-Context Language Models on Long Procedural Generation (COLM 2025). A benchmark of six procedural generation tasks (HTML to TSV, pseudocode to C++, path traversal, theory-of-mind tracking, Countdown, travel planning): the model follows a deterministic procedure described in the prompt and writes a structured output of about 500, 2K or 8K tokens, the three difficulty levels (abstract; §3, Tab. 2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/ye2025longproc \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.05414) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.05414) · [Code](https://github.com/princeton-pli/LongProc)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/longctx\">longctx</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a></span>", "paper:yin2026verifierbench": "- **[VerifierBench: Benchmarking the Generation of Executable Verifiers for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28679 \"VerifierBench: Benchmarking the Generation of Executable Verifiers for LLM Agents (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A benchmark of verifier generation: from the instruction and environment a solver would get, an agent writes an executable verifier that must return PASS for every valid solution and FAIL for invalid ones, scored on 222 tasks from six agent benchmarks (tool-call, state and code tasks) with 4,993 hidden test cases: reference solutions, valid variants, ordinary errors and attacks on verification (abstract; §2.1–2.2, Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yin2026verifierbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28679) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28679)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:yu2018spider": "- **[Spider](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1809.08887 \"Spider: A Large-Scale Human-Labeled Dataset for Complex and Cross-Domain Semantic Parsing and Text-to-SQL Task (2018)\")**: A Large-Scale Human-Labeled Dataset for Complex and Cross-Domain Semantic Parsing and Text-to-SQL Task (EMNLP 2018). Cross-domain text-to-SQL: complex queries over many databases, with unseen databases at test time. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yu2018spider \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1809.08887) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1809.08887) · [Code](https://github.com/taoyds/spider)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:yu2026sweabs": "- **[SWE-ABS: Adversarial Benchmark Strengthening Exposes Inflated Success Rates on Test-based Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.00520 \"SWE-ABS: Adversarial Benchmark Strengthening Exposes Inflated Success Rates on Test-based Benchmark (2026)\")** (ICML 2026 (per the authors' repo)). Strengthens SWE-Bench test suites in two stages: tests aimed at patch-relevant lines found by program slicing, then LLM-made mutants of the gold patch that pass the existing tests, with new tests written to reject them (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yu2026sweabs \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.00520) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00520) · [Code](https://github.com/OpenAgentEval/SWE-ABS)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:zanoli2026eltverified": "- **[ELT-Bench-Verified](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.29399 \"ELT-Bench-Verified: Benchmark Quality Issues Underestimate AI Agent Capabilities (2026)\")**: Benchmark Quality Issues Underestimate AI Agent Capabilities (PVLDB 20(1) (reference-format block; not yet published), 2026). Re-evaluates ELT-Bench with a newer model (\"a single model upgrade\", §2.1) and audits its quality (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zanoli2026eltverified \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.29399) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29399) · [Code](https://github.com/uiuc-kang-lab/ELT-Bench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:zhang2025kgqagen": "- **[Diagnosing and Addressing Pitfalls in KG-RAG Datasets: Toward More Reliable Benchmarking](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.23495 \"Diagnosing and Addressing Pitfalls in KG-RAG Datasets: Toward More Reliable Benchmarking (2025)\")** (NeurIPS 2025 Datasets and Benchmarks Track). A manual audit of sampled question–answer pairs from 16 knowledge-graph question-answering (KGQA) benchmarks, WebQSP and CWQ among them, for wrong, outdated or incomplete answers and ambiguous, trivial or unanswerable questions (§3.1, Tab. 1; App. B.3); then KGQAGen, a pipeline that writes questions over Wikidata, the 10,787-question KGQAGen-10k built with it (§5), and an evaluation of LLMs and KG-RAG systems on it (§6, Tab. 2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2025kgqagen \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.23495) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23495) · [Code](https://github.com/liangliang6v6/KGQAGen)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sparql\">dsl-sparql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:zhang2026procarena": "- **[ProcArena](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.06527 \"ProcArena: A Multi-Scenario Benchmark for LLMs on Direct and Interactive PL/SQL Development from Natural Language (2026)\")**: A Multi-Scenario Benchmark for LLMs on Direct and Interactive PL/SQL Development from Natural Language (PVLDB 20(1) (reference-format block; not yet published), 2026). An execution-based benchmark of developing PL/SQL from natural language on PostgreSQL and Oracle, in direct and interactive modes (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026procarena \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.06527) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06527) · [Code](https://github.com/ZhanGHanG9991/ProcArena)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl\">nl2dsl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:zhang2026quarry": "- **[Quarry (\"Planning to Hammer\")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.17981 \"Planning to Hammer: Difficulty-Aware Decomposition for Automating Rocq Proofs (2026)\")**: Planning to Hammer: Difficulty-Aware Decomposition for Automating Rocq Proofs (preprint 2026 (submitted to OOPSLA 2026)). The LLM proposes decompositions of a Rocq goal into sublemmas, and CoqHammer discharges the pieces (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026quarry \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.17981) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17981) · [Code](https://github.com/ningZhang-cs/QUARRY)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/prove\">prove</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:zheng2026dblifebench": "- **[DBLifeBench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.03794 \"Evaluating LLMs in Database Scenarios: A Lifecycle Benchmark for Assessing Their Potential in Core Database Tasks (2026)\")**: Evaluating LLMs in Database Scenarios: A Lifecycle Benchmark for Assessing Their Potential in Core Database Tasks (preprint 2026). Evaluates LLMs across five phases of the database lifecycle: design, implementation, operation, debugging and maintenance (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zheng2026dblifebench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.03794) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03794)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl\">nl2dsl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:zhong2020testsuite": "- **[Semantic Evaluation for Text-to-SQL with Distilled Test Suites](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2010.02840 \"Semantic Evaluation for Text-to-SQL with Distilled Test Suites (2020)\")** (EMNLP 2020). \"Test-suite accuracy\": a small suite of databases distilled to separate the gold query from near-miss neighbours. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhong2020testsuite \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2010.02840) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.02840) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2020.emnlp-main.29) · [Code](https://github.com/taoyds/test-suite-sql-eval)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/cex-search\">cex-search</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:zhong2025impossiblebench": "- **[ImpossibleBench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.20270 \"ImpossibleBench: Measuring LLMs' Propensity of Exploiting Test Cases (2025)\")**: Measuring LLMs' Propensity of Exploiting Test Cases (preprint 2025). Variants of LiveCodeBench and SWE-bench tasks whose tests conflict with the spec; the authors say any pass implies a specification-violating shortcut (abstract), and validate the LLM-made mutations on SWE-bench only (§2.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhong2025impossiblebench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.20270) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.20270) · [Code](https://github.com/safety-research/impossiblebench)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a></span>", "paper:zhou2025parrot": "- **[PARROT](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.23338 \"PARROT: A Benchmark for Evaluating LLMs in Cross-System SQL Translation (2025)\")**: A Benchmark for Evaluating LLMs in Cross-System SQL Translation (NeurIPS 2025). A cross-system SQL translation benchmark from open-source benchmarks and real services, across many systems. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2025parrot \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.23338) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23338) · [Code](https://github.com/OpenDataBox/PARROT)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/benchmark\">benchmark</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dialect\">dialect</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/difftest\">difftest</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/pairs\">pairs</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:zhou2026salus": "- **[SALUS](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05540 \"SALUS: Automated Auditing of NL-to-SQL Benchmarks through Weak Supervision of Multi-Agent Output (2026)\")**: Automated Auditing of NL-to-SQL Benchmarks through Weak Supervision of Multi-Agent Output (SIGMOD 2027 (PACMMOD 4(6)), 2026). Flags wrong gold SQL in a text-to-SQL benchmark without human labels: four LLM agents (in the primary configuration GPT-5, GPT-4o, Claude Opus 4.6 and Gemini 2.5 Pro; §5.1) each write one query per task; 44 labeling functions over execution results, ASTs and question cues vote on the gold query; a Snorkel label model turns the votes into weak labels, and per-agent reliability models over 12 features of the gold SQL weight each agent's verdict (abstract; §3; §5.1, Tab. 3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2026salus \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05540) · Computable bounds B such that agreement on all databases with ≤ B tuples per relation implies equivalence, for conjunctive queries mixing set and bag-set semantics (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/cohen2026fewrows \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.09978) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09978)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/bounded\">bounded</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/theory-bag\">theory-bag</a></span>", "paper:he2024verieql": "- **[VeriEQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.03193 \"VeriEQL: Bounded Equivalence Verification for Complex SQL Queries with Integrity Constraints (2024)\")**: Bounded Equivalence Verification for Complex SQL Queries with Integrity Constraints (OOPSLA 2024). Checks whether a Cypher (graph database) query and a SQL query are equivalent modulo a user-written database transformer, rules that say which relational database holds the same data as a given graph (abstract; §4, Defs. 4.3–4.6). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/he2025graphiti \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.03182) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03182) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3729319)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/bounded\">bounded</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/prove\">prove</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-graph\">dsl-graph</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:klopfenstein2025spotit": "- **[SpotIt](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.26840 \"SpotIt: Evaluating Text-to-SQL Evaluation with Formal Verification (2026)\")**: Evaluating Text-to-SQL Evaluation with Formal Verification (ICLR 2026). ACM 35(1) 1988). CQ containment with <, ≤, = over dense orders needs a finite family of representative databases, one per ordering. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/klug1988inequalities \"Summary\") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/42267.42273)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/bounded\">bounded</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/theory-set\">theory-set</a></span>", "paper:sagiv1980union": "- **[Equivalences Among Relational Expressions with the Union and Difference Operators](#/papers/sagiv1980union \"Equivalences Among Relational Expressions with the Union and Difference Operators (1980)\")** (J.
