Unions of tableaux with the same target relation scheme: a union is contained in another iff each member is contained in some member of the other (Theorem 3, PDF p. 9). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sagiv1980union \"Summary\") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/322217.322221)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/bounded\">bounded</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/theory-set\">theory-set</a></span>", "paper:sternbach2026shrink": "- **[How Can We Shrink the Family of Test Databases? Query Containment with Nulls and Comparisons (2026)\")** (preprint Sep 2026). Containment of CQs over databases with NULLs (SQL 3-valued logic) and of CQs with order comparisons; both Π₂ᵖ-complete by prior work (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sternbach2026shrink \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.16218) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16218)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/bounded\">bounded</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/theory-null\">theory-null</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/theory-set\">theory-set</a></span>", "paper:tremante2026spotitplus": "- **[SpotIt+](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.04334 \"SpotIt+: Verification-based Text-to-SQL Evaluation with Database Constraints (2026)\")**: Verification-based Text-to-SQL Evaluation with Database Constraints (preprint, 2026). SpotIt plus a best-effort constraint-mining pipeline, so counterexample databases are more realistic (abstract; §1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/tremante2026spotitplus \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.04334) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04334) · [Code](https://github.com/ai-ar-research/SpotIt-plus)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/bounded\">bounded</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/smt\">smt</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:vandermeyden1992indefinite": "- **[querying indefinite order data](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/137097.137902 \"The complexity of querying indefinite data about linearly ordered domains (1992)\")**: The complexity of querying indefinite data about linearly ordered domains (PODS 1992). Complexity of evaluating positive existential (conjunctive and disjunctive) queries over databases that state only some order facts between points (abstract, §1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/vandermeyden1992indefinite \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/137097.137902) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/137097.137902)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/bounded\">bounded</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/theory-set\">theory-set</a></span>", "paper:wang2018symbolic": "- **[Speeding up symbolic reasoning for relational queries](#/papers/wang2018symbolic \"Speeding up symbolic reasoning for relational queries (2018)\")** (OOPSLA 2018). Refines a bounded check's symbolic search space by backward symbolic provenance analysis before SMT solving. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2018symbolic \"Summary\") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3276527)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/bounded\">bounded</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/smt\">smt</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:zhao2025polygon": "- **[Polygon](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.06542 \"Polygon: Symbolic Reasoning for SQL using Conflict-Driven Under-Approximation Search (2025)\")**: Symbolic Reasoning for SQL using Conflict-Driven Under-Approximation Search (PLDI 2025). Generates an input database on which several queries' outputs satisfy a property, e.g. two queries differ. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhao2025polygon \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.06542) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06542) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3729303) · [Code: polygon-artifact-evaluation](https://zenodo.org/records/15059866) · [Code: polygon-sql](https://github.com/polygon-sql/polygon)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/bounded\">bounded</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/smt\">smt</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "project:Cosette": "- Evaluating Text-to-SQL Evaluation with Formal Verification (2026)\")", "project:VeriEQL": "- **[VeriEQL](https://github.com/VeriEQL/VeriEQL)**: implements [VeriEQL](#/papers/he2024verieql \"VeriEQL: Constraints (2024)\")", "paper:allamanis2025disproving": "- **[Disproving Program Equivalence with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.18473 \"Disproving Program Equivalence with LLMs (2025)\")** (preprint 2025). A white-box search for counterexamples to the equivalence of two or more executable programs, by an LLM with execution feedback (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/allamanis2025disproving \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.18473) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18473)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:cassano2023multiplt": "- **[MultiPL-T](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.09895 \"Knowledge Transfer from High-Resource to Low-Resource Programming Languages for Code LLMs (2024)\")**: Knowledge Transfer from High-Resource to Low-Resource Programming Languages for Code LLMs (OOPSLA 2024 (PACMPL 8, OOPSLA2)). Makes fine-tuning data for programming languages code LLMs are weak in (Julia, Lua, OCaml, R, Racket): a code LLM translates documented Python functions from The Stack, and only translations that pass the function's LLM-written, execution-checked Python tests, compiled into the target language without an LLM, are kept (abstract; §4, Fig. 6). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/cassano2023multiplt \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.09895) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.09895) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3689735) · [Code](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.12453932)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:castelein2018evosql": "- **[EvoSQL](#/papers/castelein2018evosql \"Search-Based Test Data Generation for SQL Queries (2018)\")**: Search-Based Test Data Generation for SQL Queries (ICSE 2018). Generates test data covering each SQLFpc coverage target of a query. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/castelein2018evosql \"Summary\") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3180155.3180202) · [Code: evosql](https://github.com/SERG-Delft/evosql) · [Code: evosql-appendix](https://zenodo.org/records/1166023)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/cex-search\">cex-search</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:chandra2014xdata": "- **[XData](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1411.6704 \"Data generation for testing and grading SQL queries (2015)\")**: Data generation for testing and grading SQL queries (VLDB J. 24(6) 2015). Generates small datasets that \"kill\" query mutants (join-type, comparison, aggregation, …). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chandra2014xdata \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1411.6704) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1411.6704) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00778-015-0395-0)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/smt\">smt</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:chandra2019grading": "- **[Edit Based Grading of SQL Queries](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1912.09019 \"Edit Based Grading of SQL Queries (2019)\")** (arXiv 2019, published at CODS-COMAD 2021; a short version is ICDE 2019 (\"Automated Grading of SQL Queries\")). Grades student SQL: XData's generated test databases decide correctness, and partial marks come from an edit distance to the nearest correct query. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chandra2019grading \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1912.09019) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.09019)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:chen2022codet": "- **[CodeT](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2207.10397 \"CodeT: Code Generation with Generated Tests (2023)\")**: Code Generation with Generated Tests (ICLR 2023). The same code model that samples candidate programs is prompted to write test cases (input and expected output) for the problem; every program runs on every test, programs that pass the same tests form a consensus set, and the set scored by its numbers of programs and tests supplies the answer (\"dual execution agreement\", RANSAC-inspired) (abstract; §2.1–2.2). Zero-shot on HumanEval, MBPP, APPS and CodeContests with three Codex models, InCoder-6B and CodeGen-16B (§3); APPS and CodeContests with code-davinci-002 only (§4.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2022codet \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2207.10397) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.10397) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/CodeT)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:chen2025parseval": "- **[ParSEval](https://dbgroup.cs.tsinghua.edu.cn/jnwang/papers/vldb2025-parseval.pdf \"ParSEval: Plan-aware Test Database Generation for SQL Equivalence Evaluation (2025)\")**: Plan-aware Test Database Generation for SQL Equivalence Evaluation (PVLDB 18(11) 2025). Generates test databases that aim to cover every branch of the queries' logical plans (abstract; a greedy heuristic, §4.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2025parseval \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://dbgroup.cs.tsinghua.edu.cn/jnwang/papers/vldb2025-parseval.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3749646.3749727) · [Code](https://github.com/sfu-db/ParSEval)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/smt\">smt</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:chen2026cyclebench": "- **[Compilation Is Not Correctness: Scan-by-Scan Evaluation of LLM-Generated PLC Programs](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28675 \"Compilation Is Not Correctness: Scan-by-Scan Evaluation of LLM-Generated PLC Programs (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A benchmark of 301 tasks for LLMs writing Structured Text (ST: a high-level textual language defined in IEC 61131-3 for programmable logic controllers, which run their logic in repeated scans), each with a reviewed public specification and a hidden reference program and input scenarios; a program passes only if it keeps the public interface, compiles, completes every scenario and matches the reference's outputs after every scan (abstract; §3). The authors call it \"To our knowledge, the first benchmark for LLM-based Structured Text generation with comprehensive per-scan behavioral evaluation against hidden references\" (§1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026cyclebench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28675) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28675)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:dong2023slabcity": "- **[SlabCity](#/papers/dong2023slabcity \"SlabCity: Whole-Query Optimization Using Program Synthesis (2023)\")**: Whole-Query Optimization Using Program Synthesis (PVLDB 16(11) 2023). Rule-free whole-query rewriting by program synthesis, with a new LeetCode benchmark with constraints. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/dong2023slabcity \"Summary\") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3611479.3611515) · [Code](https://github.com/eidos06/SlabCity)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/smt\">smt</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/rewrite-classic\">rewrite-classic</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/rewrite-eval\">rewrite-eval</a></span>", "paper:geneves2008analyzer": "- **[XML Static Analyzer User Manual](https://arxiv.org/pdf/0812.3550 \"XML Static Analyzer User Manual (2008)\")** (INRIA research report RR-6726 (arXiv 2008)). User manual of a satisfiability solver for a logic of finite trees that decides XPath emptiness, containment and equivalence, optionally under a DTD, XML Schema or Relax NG type, and outputs a satisfying or counterexample XML tree (§2, Examples 1–3; §3.2 \"Supported XML Types\"; §4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/geneves2008analyzer \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/0812.3550) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/0812.3550)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/prove\">prove</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-path\">dsl-path</a></span>", "paper:habibollah2026synsql": "- **[SynSQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.27261 \"SynSQL: Synthesizing Relational Databases for Robust Evaluation of Text-to-SQL Systems (2026)\")**: Synthesizing Relational Databases for Robust Evaluation of Text-to-SQL Systems (preprint, 2026). LLM-synthesized test databases conditioned on the NL question and schema, not the gold SQL. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/habibollah2026synsql \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.27261) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27261)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:helff2026gaming": "- **[LLMs Gaming Verifiers](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.15149 \"LLMs Gaming Verifiers: RLVR can Lead to Reward Hacking (2026)\")**: RLVR can Lead to Reward Hacking (LLM Reasoning Workshop @ ICLR 2026 (PDF header)). RLVR-trained models on inductive rule tasks enumerate instance labels instead of inducing a rule, which passes a verifier that checks only extensional correctness (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/helff2026gaming \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.15149) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15149) · [Code](https://github.com/ml-research/llms-gaming-verifiers)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:ke2026logos": "- **[Logos](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.15709 \"Logos: Certified Order-Sensitive SQL Rewrites with Mechanized Semantics and LLM Guidance (2026)\")**: Certified Order-Sensitive SQL Rewrites with Mechanized Semantics and LLM Guidance (preprint Aug 2026). Order-sensitive SQL semantics mechanized in Rocq (FormalSQL); an LLM proof agent proves equivalence or proposes a counter-database. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/ke2026logos \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.15709) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15709) · [Code: FormalSQL](https://github.com/WindOctober/FormalSQL) · [Code: Logos](https://github.com/WindOctober/Logos)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/prove\">prove</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nondet-semantics\">nondet-semantics</a></span>", "paper:kikot2026separating": "- **[data-aware NL2SQL candidate selection](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.12319 \"Data-aware candidate selection in NL2SQL translation via small separating instances (2026)\")**: Data-aware candidate selection in NL2SQL translation via small separating instances (preprint 2026). Picks among text-to-SQL candidates using small separating database instances. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/kikot2026separating \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.12319) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12319) · [Code](https://github.com/staskikotx/SISelection)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/reduce\">reduce</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/cex-search\">cex-search</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-data\">nl2dsl-data</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-select\">nl2dsl-select</a></span>", "paper:li2025arg": "- **[ARG](#/papers/li2025arg \"ARG: Testing Query Rewriters via Abstract Rule Guided Fuzzing (2025)\")**: Testing Query Rewriters via Abstract Rule Guided Fuzzing (ASE 2025). Fuzzes query rewriters with queries steered toward untested \"abstract rules\". <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2025arg \"Summary\") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/ase63991.2025.00151)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/cex-search\">cex-search</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/rewrite-eval\">rewrite-eval</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/rules-verify\">rules-verify</a></span>", "paper:li2026advermcts": "- **[AdverMCTS](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.10449 \"AdverMCTS: Combating Pseudo-Correctness in Code Generation via Adversarial Monte Carlo Tree Search (2026)\")**: Combating Pseudo-Correctness in Code Generation via Adversarial Monte Carlo Tree Search (preprint 2026). Code generation as a game between two MCTS agents on one LLM: a Solver searches over reasoning steps and writes programs, and an Attacker searches for test inputs on which the programs that pass the public tests disagree; an LLM arbiter labels each such input's expected output, and the labelled tests then screen new programs, penalize failing branches and re-rank the final candidates (abstract; §3.3–3.6). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2026advermcts \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.10449) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.10449)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:li2026dpc": "- **[DPC (Dual-Paradigm Consistency)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.15163 \"DPC: Training-Free Text-to-SQL Candidate Selection via Dual-Paradigm Consistency (2026)\")**: DPC: Training-Free Text-to-SQL Candidate Selection via Dual-Paradigm Consistency (ACL 2026 per the arXiv comment). Picks among text-to-SQL candidates without a gold query: LLM agents build a \"Minimal Distinguishing Database\" on which the two leading candidates return different results (abstract, §3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2026dpc \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.15163) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15163) · [Code](https://github.com/HKUSTDial/DPC)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-select\">nl2dsl-select</a></span>", "paper:lian2026admitor": "- **[ADMITOR (\"Admission Without Answers\")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.15565 \"Admission Without Answers: Label-Free Certification and Experience Learning for LLM-Based Optimization Modeling (2026)\")**: Admission Without Answers: Label-Free Certification and Experience Learning for LLM-Based Optimization Modeling (preprint 2026). A label-free admission gate for an agent that learns skills for optimization modeling (LLM-written LP and MILP models): three model families each write a model, every model is solved on the stated instance and on instances with resampled parameters, the largest group whose optimal values agree on every instance across families gives the accepted value, and a threshold fitted on solver-verified synthetic problems turns the agreement into accept, abstain or escalate, with a Clopper–Pearson bound on false certificates (abstract; §3, Prop. 2). Only the host's own trajectories whose answer equals the accepted value enter its skill library (§3, Step 5). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lian2026admitor \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.15565) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15565) · [Code](https://github.com/junbolian/AdmitOR)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/cex-search\">cex-search</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:liang2026testgen": "- **[Auditing and Decomposing Feedback-Driven Evolution in LLM Test Generation under the Oracle Problem](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.19626 \"Auditing and Decomposing Feedback-Driven Evolution in LLM Test Generation under the Oracle Problem (2026)\")** (preprint 2026 (the source's comment calls it an FSE submission)). Audits LLM generation of input–output tests from a problem statement when one accepted program supplies the expected outputs, and asks whether the gains of mutation-based test evolution and of a three-round execution-feedback loop survive an audit by the task's other accepted programs (abstract; §1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liang2026testgen \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.19626) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19626)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/placebo\">placebo</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:liu2023onlinejudge": "- **[Who Judges the Judge: An Empirical Study on Online Judge Tests](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3597926.3598060 \"Who Judges the Judge: An Empirical Study on Online Judge Tests (2023)\")** (ISSTA 2023). Audits AtCoder's hidden test suites on 939 problems and 541,552 accepted Python, Java and C++ solutions (from CodeContests): their line and branch coverage and mutation scores, and whether accepted solutions are still wrong (abstract; §3.1–3.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liu2023onlinejudge \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3597926.3598060) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3597926.3598060) · [Code](https://zenodo.org/records/7977256)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/cex-search\">cex-search</a></span>", "paper:liu2024trickcatcher": "- **[TrickCatcher](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.10304 \"LLM-Powered Test Case Generation for Detecting Bugs in Plausible Programs (2025)\")**: LLM-Powered Test Case Generation for Detecting Bugs in Plausible Programs (ACL 2025). Generates tests that expose bugs in \"plausible programs\", those that pass an existing test suite yet are wrong (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liu2024trickcatcher \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.10304) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10304) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.acl-long.20) · [Code](https://github.com/RinCloud/TrickCatcher)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:liu2026cegl": "- **[Counterexample Guided Learning in the Large using Reasoning Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.11521 \"Counterexample Guided Learning in the Large using Reasoning Agents (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). An LLM learner infers a regular expression from labeled strings; a teacher builds automata for the candidate and the target, decides whether they are equivalent and, if not, returns strings on which they disagree as positive or negative counterexamples, and the loop repeats until equivalence or a round budget (abstract; §3; §4 \"Teacher-Learner Iterative Framework\"). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liu2026cegl \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.11521) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11521) · [Code](https://github.com/Lhtie/CEGML)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/cex-search\">cex-search</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-regex\">dsl-regex</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:ma2026sweproof": "- **[SWE-Proof: Can Language Models Resolve Real-World Issues with Machine-Checked Proofs?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21190 \"SWE-Proof: Can Language Models Resolve Real-World Issues with Machine-Checked Proofs? (2026)\")** (preprint 2026 (v2)). A benchmark that gives each of the 500 SWE-bench Verified issues a formal specification, a verifying reference implementation and its proof under three backends (Nagini for Python, Velvet in Lean, and pure Lean with agent-written proofs), extended to the Python instances of SWE-bench Pro (abstract; §1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/ma2026sweproof \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21190) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21190)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:miao2019ratest": "- **[RATest](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1904.04467 \"Explaining Wrong Queries Using Small Examples (2019)\")**: Explaining Wrong Queries Using Small Examples (SIGMOD 2019). Given a separating database, looks for the smallest sub-instance that still separates a wrong query from the right one (abstract); its faster algorithm, used for the rest of the experiments (§7.1), minimizes the witness of one differing tuple (§4.2, Alg. 2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/miao2019ratest \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1904.04467) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.04467) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3299869.3319866)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/reduce\">reduce</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/smt\">smt</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:micelibarone2025sinq": "- **[Program Semantic Inequivalence Game with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.03818 \"Program Semantic Inequivalence Game with Large Language Models (2025)\")** (NeSy 2026 (PMLR vol. 284, per the v3 PDF's first-page header), 2025). A generator makes semantically different variants of real programs and an evaluator must find inputs on which they differ; the two train each other (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/micelibarone2025sinq \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.03818) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03818) · [Code](https://github.com/Avmb/semantic_neq_game)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:mohamed2024cvc5sql": "- **[Verifying SQL Queries using Theories of Tables and Relations](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.03057 \"Verifying SQL Queries using Theories of Tables and Relations (2024)\")** (LPAR 2024). Extends cvc5's SMT theories of bags (as a theory of tables) and of relations, and adds a theory of nullable sorts, for SQL queries with join, projection and selection (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/mohamed2024cvc5sql \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.03057) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03057) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.29007/rlt7) · [Code](https://github.com/cvc5/cvc5)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/prove\">prove</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/smt\">smt</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:poon2026selfplay": "- **[Semantic Equivalence Self-Play (SEQ/SINQ](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.17010 \"Improving LLM Code Reasoning via Semantic Equivalence Self-Play with Formal Verification (2026)\")**: Improving LLM Code Reasoning via Semantic Equivalence Self-Play with Formal Verification (Haskell), Findings of ACL 2026). A generator (Alice) writes a variant of a reference Haskell program, either equivalent with a Liquid Haskell proof (SEQ) or inequivalent with a diverging input that is executed (SINQ); an evaluator (Bob) judges whether the pair is equivalent, and its sampled success rate sets each instance's difficulty (abstract; §3.1, §3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/poon2026selfplay \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.17010) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17010) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.findings-acl.1615) · [Code](https://github.com/Baki-0501/llm-self-play-liquidhaskell)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:singh2026ruleflow": "- **[RuleFlow](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.09051 \"RuleFlow: Generating Reusable Program Optimizations with LLMs (2026)\")**: Generating Reusable Program Optimizations with LLMs (preprint 2026). Optimizes pandas notebooks (not SQL) by turning LLM-found rewrites of single code cells into general rewrite rules with runtime preconditions, which a deterministic compiler then applies to unseen notebooks with no LLM calls (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/singh2026ruleflow \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.09051) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09051) · [Code](https://github.com/ADAPT-uiuc/RuleFlow)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/cex-search\">cex-search</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/rules-discover\">rules-discover</a></span>", "paper:somwase2024complex": "- **[Test Data Generation for Complex SQL Queries](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.18821 \"Test Data Generation for Complex SQL Queries (2025)\")** (PACMMOD 3(6) 2025 (SIGMOD 2026)). XData's successor for multi-block, multi-level nested queries (§6; benchmark, §9). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/somwase2024complex \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.18821) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18821) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3769832)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/smt\">smt</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:wang2026lara": "- **[LARA (Lookahead Automata ReDoS Analyzer)](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3832783.3837475 \"LARA: Static ReDoS Detection for Regular Expressions with Lookahead via Matching Automata (2026)\")**: LARA: Static ReDoS Detection for Regular Expressions with Lookahead via Matching Automata (ASE 2026). A static detector of ReDoS (regexes whose backtracking match time grows super-linearly with the input) for regexes with lookahead assertions: it compiles the regex into an automaton with lookahead transitions, replaces each lookahead by ε-transitions into the lookahead's own automaton (the \"matching NFA\"), and runs classical ambiguity analysis on the result (abstract, PDF p. 1; §3.1, PDF p. 4). The authors state the matching NFA is not language-equivalent to the regex, and prove that on every input its degree of match, a count of the (state, position) pairs backtracking visits, equals the regex's lookahead-aware count (Thm. 3.3, PDF p. 5; the proof is in supplementary material the PDF does not link). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026lara \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3832783.3837475) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3832783.3837475)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-regex\">dsl-regex</a></span>", "paper:xie2026testauditors": "- **[Coding Agents as Test-Suite Auditors](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.01715 \"Coding Agents as Test-Suite Auditors: Finding What Official Suites Miss While Approaching What They Catch (2026)\")**: Finding What Official Suites Miss While Approaching What They Catch (preprint 2026). Off-the-shelf coding agents (Codex CLI, Claude Code, Antigravity, OpenCode, mini-SWE-agent) build adversarial test suites from a problem's statement and one reference solution, without seeing the official tests or the submissions audited (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/xie2026testauditors \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.01715) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01715) · [Code](https://github.com/xieTwim/test-suite-auditors)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:yang2025sqldriller": "- **[SQLDriller](#/papers/yang2025sqldriller \"Automated Validating and Fixing of Text-to-SQL Translation with Execution Consistency (2025)\")**: Automated Validating and Fixing of Text-to-SQL Translation with Execution Consistency (PACMMOD / SIGMOD 2025). Finds and fixes wrong NL→SQL mappings in training data via counterexample databases and \"execution consistency\". <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yang2025sqldriller \"Summary\") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3725271)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/cex-search\">cex-search</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-data\">nl2dsl-data</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-select\">nl2dsl-select</a></span>", "paper:yuviler2025expairt": "- **[ExPairT-LLM](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.10855 \"ExPairT-LLM: Exact Learning for LLM Code Selection by Pairwise Queries (2026)\")**: Exact Learning for LLM Code Selection by Pairwise Queries (AAAI 2026 (per the PDF's copyright line, p. 1)). Code selection as exact learning with an LLM as the oracle: candidate programs are clustered by their outputs on a few inputs, a round-robin tournament of \"pairwise membership\" queries (which of two output lists better fits the task) picks a cluster, and \"pairwise equivalence\" queries ask the LLM for an input on which two programs of that cluster differ; an input confirmed by running both programs splits the cluster and the loop repeats (abstract; § \"Introduction\"; § \"Exact Learning by Pairwise Queries to LLMs\", Alg. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yuviler2025expairt \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.10855) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10855) · [Code](https://github.com/TomYuviler/ExPairT-LLM)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:zhu2026modelequivbench": "- **[ModelEquivBench: Certifying Multi-Relational Evaluation of LLM-Generated Optimization Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.29431 \"ModelEquivBench: Certifying Multi-Relational Evaluation of LLM-Generated Optimization Models (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Grades an LLM-generated optimization model against a reference not with one equivalent/not-equivalent verdict but with a profile of seven relations, E0–E6: it builds and ingests, a verified variable map, feasible sets (same space or under an affine lift), objective order, optimal value, and optimizer sets (abstract; §1; Tab. 1). The authors say it is an evaluator, \"not a new benchmark dataset\": the problems come from Bench4Opt (§1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhu2026modelequivbench \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.29431) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29431)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/cex\">cex</a><a class=\"tag\"
