"paper:allamanis2024roundtrip": "- **[Round-Trip Correctness (RTC)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.08699 \"Unsupervised Evaluation of Code LLMs with Round-Trip Correctness (2024)\")**: Unsupervised Evaluation of Code LLMs with Round-Trip Correctness (ICML 2024). Evaluates code LLMs without human labels: describe code in natural language, synthesize code back, and check whether the result is semantically equivalent to the original (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/allamanis2024roundtrip \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.08699) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08699) · [Code](https://github.com/google-deepmind/icml2024-roundtrip-correctness)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a></span>", "paper:alrabah2026chipmem": "- **[ChipMEM](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.27067 \"ChipMEM: Verification-Grounded Memory for EDA Agents (2026)\")**: Verification-Grounded Memory for EDA Agents (preprint 2026). A memory layer for LLM agents that optimize RTL hardware designs or write testbenches: skills retrieved by task similarity, plus Bayesian (Beta) estimates of tool-call success that advise on retries (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/alrabah2026chipmem \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.27067) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27067) · [Code](https://github.com/aalrabah/ChipMEM)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:brown2024monkeys": "- **[Large Language Monkeys](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2407.21787 \"Large Language Monkeys: Scaling Inference Compute with Repeated Sampling (2024)\")**: Scaling Inference Compute with Repeated Sampling (preprint 2024). Coverage (any sample correct) often grows log-linearly with the number of samples and can be modelled as an exponentiated power law (abstract; §1, §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/brown2024monkeys \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2407.21787) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21787) · [Code](https://github.com/ScalingIntelligence/large_language_monkeys)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:chen2023selfdebug": "- **[Self-Debugging](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2304.05128 \"Teaching Large Language Models to Self-Debug (2023)\")**: Teaching Large Language Models to Self-Debug (preprint 2023 (published at ICLR 2024)). Few-shot demonstrations teach an LLM to debug its own program from execution results and explanations (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2023selfdebug \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2304.05128) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.05128)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-select\">nl2dsl-select</a></span>", "paper:chen2026cofailure": "- **[When Does Combining Language Models Help? A Co-Failure Ceiling on Routing, Voting, and Mixture-of-Agents Across 67 Frontier Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.27288 \"When Does Combining Language Models Help? A Co-Failure Ceiling on Routing, Voting, and Mixture-of-Agents Across 67 Frontier Models (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Any policy whose output is one member model's answer (a router, a vote, a cascade) has accuracy at most 1 − β, where β is the rate at which every model in the pool is wrong on the same query. The average pairwise error correlation ρ, the diagnostic practice reports, cannot identify β (abstract; §5, Props. 1 and 3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026cofailure \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.27288) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27288)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:cheng2025guru": "- **[Revisiting Reinforcement Learning for LLM Reasoning from A Cross-Domain Perspective](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.14965 \"Revisiting Reinforcement Learning for LLM Reasoning from A Cross-Domain Perspective (2025)\")** (NeurIPS 2025). A 92K-example RL corpus over six domains (Math, Code, Science, Logic, Simulation, Tabular), each with its own verifiable reward (abstract); the authors report that Math, Code and Science \"easily benefit from cross-domain RL training\", while Logic, Simulation and Tabular, which they describe as having \"limited pretraining exposure\", \"require in-domain training to achieve meaningful performance gains\" (abstract; §3.1, Fig. 3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/cheng2025guru \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.14965) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14965) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.52202/085713-4479) · [Code](https://github.com/LLM360/Reasoning360)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "paper:cieslak2026audits": "- **[Better Policies, Worse Verifier Audits](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29029 \"Better Policies, Worse Verifier Audits (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Shows that scoring a component verifier on fresh policy outputs against the full success label can stop measuring that component as the policy masters it (\"on-policy audit collapse\"), can prefer a worse verifier, and, when used to trigger a refresh, can make the verifier worse (abstract; §1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/cieslak2026audits \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29029) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29029)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:deepseek2025reasoning": "- **[DeepSeek-R1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.12948 \"DeepSeek-R1 incentivizes reasoning in LLMs through reinforcement learning (2025)\")**: DeepSeek-R1 incentivizes reasoning in LLMs through reinforcement learning (Nature 645 (2025)). Reasoning learned by RL with rule-based rewards (accuracy and format), starting without SFT (R1-Zero). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/deepseek2025reasoning \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.12948) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12948) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-09422-z) · [Code](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-R1)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:delgado2026constraintdsl": "- **[A framework for assessing the capabilities of code generation of constraint domain-specific languages with large language models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.05278 \"A framework for assessing the capabilities of code generation of constraint domain-specific languages with large language models (2026)\")** (Alloy) with LLMs, Journal of Systems and Software 238 (2026), 112871). A configurable pipeline that turns natural-language constraint descriptions, plus a text description of the domain, a domain model or both, into code in the constraint languages OCL and Alloy, or in Python as a high-resource baseline, then checks each snippet for well-formedness and correctness, with an optional single round of LLM repair after each check (abstract; §3, Fig. 1–3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/delgado2026constraintdsl \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.05278) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05278) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jss.2026.112871) · [Code](https://github.com/david-xander/code-generation-framework-for-dsls)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:ding2024semcoder": "- **[SemCoder](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.01006 \"SemCoder: Training Code Language Models with Comprehensive Semantics Reasoning (2024)\")**: Training Code Language Models with Comprehensive Semantics Reasoning (NeurIPS 2024). Fine-tunes DeepSeekCoder-6.7B jointly on writing Python from English and on \"monologue reasoning\", natural-language explanations of a function's purpose, the properties and constraints that always hold, and its execution, forward to the output and backward to a possible input; the authors report \"competitive performance with GPT-3.5-turbo on code generation and execution reasoning tasks\" (abstract; §6.1, Tab. 1), and that SemCoder-S, the variant also trained on extra instruction data, outperforms four open instruction-tuned models at iterative debugging and self-refinement (§6.3, Tab. 3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/ding2024semcoder \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.01006) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01006) · [Code](https://github.com/ARiSE-Lab/SemCoder)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:du2025contextlength": "- **[Context Length Alone Hurts LLM Performance Despite Perfect Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.05381 \"Context Length Alone Hurts LLM Performance Despite Perfect Retrieval (2025)\")** (Findings of EMNLP 2025). Measures whether an LLM that can still recite all the evidence of a problem word for word solves it as well over a long input as over a short one: GSM8K, MMLU, HumanEval and a synthetic variable-summation task, each split into evidence and question with filler tokens inserted between them (§3.1, Tab. 1), on Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct and Mistral-v0.3-7B-Instruct, and on three closed models in one setting (§3.2, §4.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/du2025contextlength \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.05381) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05381)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/longctx\">longctx</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:first2023baldur": "- **[Baldur](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.04910 \"Baldur: Whole-Proof Generation and Repair with Large Language Models (2023)\")**: Whole-Proof Generation and Repair with Large Language Models (ESEC/FSE 2023). Fine-tuned LLMs generate whole Isabelle/HOL proofs at once, instead of one step at a time inside a search (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/first2023baldur \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.04910) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.04910) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3611643.3616243)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/prove\">prove</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:gozel2026commitfirst": "- **[Commit-first LLM judging inherits the judge's own errors](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.00088 \"Commit-first LLM judging inherits the judge's own errors (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Audits the default LLM-judge configurations of eight evaluation frameworks for commit-first judging (in the author's definition, the judge commits its own answer before it sees the candidate and accepts only a match (abstract); in [More Convincing, Not More Correct](#/papers/zhou2026convincing \"More Convincing, Not More Correct: Self-Play Reward Hacking of Reference-Free LLM Judges (2026)\"), commit-first keeps the candidate in view and blind-solve withholds it (App. A)): the author reports that none of the 24 in-scope configurations implements it and nine use what it calls the \"recompute\" form, which that work reports ineffective (abstract; §2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/gozel2026commitfirst \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.00088) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00088) · [Code](https://github.com/idilgozel/evaluator-integrity)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:haluptzok2022teach": "- **[Language Models Can Teach Themselves to Program Better](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2207.14502 \"Language Models Can Teach Themselves to Program Better (2022)\")** (ICLR 2023 (the PDF's running header, p. 1), 2022). An LM writes programming puzzles and solutions, a Python interpreter keeps the correct ones, and the LM is fine-tuned on them (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/haluptzok2022teach \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2207.14502) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.14502)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:he2026verificationtrap": "- **[Verification Trap](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05170 \"Verification Trap: Understanding Test-Time Selection Failures under False Premises in Code Generation (2026)\")**: Understanding Test-Time Selection Failures under False Premises in Code Generation (EMNLP 2026). Inserts a false premise into coding task descriptions, with hidden tests unchanged, samples 64 candidate programs, and selects one by default with unit tests a verifier writes from the same prompt; a task is trapped when the pool held a hidden-test-correct program but the selector returned a wrong one (Trap@64) (abstract; §3.1; §4.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/he2026verificationtrap \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05170) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05170)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:iscan2026falsification": "- **[Selection Without Signal, Recovery Through Expression: A Measurement Study of Post-Hoc Falsification Operators for Frozen Small Code Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16999 \"Selection Without Signal, Recovery Through Expression: A Measurement Study of Post-Hoc Falsification Operators for Frozen Small Code Models (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Measures whether post-hoc operators over a frozen small code model's own samples (selection, verification, repair, elimination, portfolios, sound vetoes, generation conditioning) beat Best-of-N, which deploys the first candidate that passes the visible tests; correctness is the hidden tests' verdict (abstract; §2). Models of at most 1.7B run locally (Qwen2.5-Coder 1.5B and 0.5B, DeepSeek-Coder 1.3B, Qwen3 probes) on a self-authored benchmark, an engineered one, HumanEval+ and MBPP+ (§3; Tab. 2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/iscan2026falsification \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16999) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16999)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:jiang2026ruleevolve": "- **[RuleEvolve](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.00650 \"Self-Evolving Coding Rules for AI Coding Agents (2026)\")**: Self-Evolving Coding Rules for AI Coding Agents (NeurIPS 2026). Evolves a pool of coding-agent rules: an LLM mutates candidates and a judge keeps the best (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/jiang2026ruleevolve \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.00650) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00650)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:kan2026aria": "- **[Harnessing Code Agents for Automatic Software Verification](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.06341 \"Harnessing Code Agents for Automatic Software Verification (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Hands each Coq lemma to a general code agent (e.g. Claude Code) inside a verification harness, with no fixed proof strategy (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/kan2026aria \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.06341) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06341) · [Code](https://anonymous.4open.science/r/CoqProver-Code-57BB)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/prove\">prove</a></span>", "paper:kiele2026mutants": "- **[Beyond Rule-Based Mutation Testing: Test-Aware Mutant Generation Using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.35841 \"Beyond Rule-Based Mutation Testing: Test-Aware Mutant Generation Using Large Language Models (2026)\")** (ESEM 2026 (LIPIcs, per the PDF's title page)). Mutation testing with an LLM that sees the tests: given the problem statement, the reference solution and its few base tests in one prompt, the model must write a mutant (a wrong version of the program) that passes the base tests; the larger EvalPlus test suites then decide whether it is a real fault (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/kiele2026mutants \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.35841) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35841) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.ESEM.2026.49) · [Code](https://github.com/codingWhale13/test-aware-mutants)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:kim2026memorytransfer": "- **[Memory Transfer Learning (MTL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.14004 \"Memory Transfer Learning: How Memories are Transferred Across Domains in Coding Agents (2026)\")**: Memory Transfer Learning: How Memories are Transferred Across Domains in Coding Agents (\"How Memories are Transferred Across Domains in Coding Agents\"), preprint 2026). A coding agent gets memories built from its own runs on other coding benchmarks (the target benchmark left out) in its system prompt; four memory formats, from raw command traces to task-agnostic insights, on six benchmarks with GPT-5-mini, and Insight memories only with DeepSeek V3.2 and Qwen3-Coder (abstract; §3.1; Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/kim2026memorytransfer \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.14004) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14004) · [Code](https://github.com/KangsanKim07/MemoryTransferLearning)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:kozyrev2026skillissue": "- **[Skill Issue](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.12742 \"Skill Issue: Lessons from Optimizing Repository SKILLs for Coding Agents (2026)\")**: Lessons from Optimizing Repository SKILLs for Coding Agents (preprint 2026). Optimizes repository SKILL files for coding agents on tasks mined from real pull requests. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/kozyrev2026skillissue \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.12742) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12742)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:li2025codeio": "- **[CodeI/O](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.07316 \"CodeI/O: Condensing Reasoning Patterns via Code Input-Output Prediction (2025)\")**: Condensing Reasoning Patterns via Code Input-Output Prediction (ICML 2025). A first training stage before instruction tuning in which a model predicts, in a natural-language chain of thought, a function's output for a given input or a feasible input for a given output; the authors report gains over a single-stage baseline and over other first-stage datasets across symbolic, scientific, logic, math and commonsense benchmarks on four base models of 7B–27B parameters (abstract; §3.2, Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2025codeio \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.07316) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07316) · [Code](https://github.com/hkust-nlp/CodeIO)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:li2026privatelibrary": "- **[Learning from Execution: Self-Evolving Memory for Private-Library Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.24222 \"Learning from Execution: Self-Evolving Memory for Private-Library Code Generation (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Code generation against recently released Python libraries that stand in for an enterprise's private libraries, chosen to reduce \"the chance that models have memorized their latest APIs\" (§V-B); the authors report that even with every API document a task needs in the prompt, models still misuse single APIs, chain APIs wrongly and pick wrong solution strategies (§III, Tab. I, Fig. 1), and add to a RAG pipeline a memory of usage guidelines learned from running the generated code, with no weight updates (abstract; §IV). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2026privatelibrary \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.24222) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.24222) · [Code](https://github.com/THU-Agent/MEMCoder)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rarelang\">rarelang</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:liao2026codemonitor": "- **[Code Monitor Red Teaming](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.20852 \"Code Monitor Red Teaming for Public-Test-Passing Code (2026)\")**: Code Monitor Red Teaming for Public-Test-Passing Code (preprint 2026). Can a weaker LLM verifier find hidden bugs in code that already passes public tests? (abstract) <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liao2026codemonitor \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.20852) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20852)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:liu2025prorl": "- **[ProRL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.24864 \"ProRL: Prolonged Reinforcement Learning Expands Reasoning Boundaries in Large Language Models (2025)\")**: Prolonged Reinforcement Learning Expands Reasoning Boundaries in Large Language Models (NeurIPS 2025). Prolonged RL training with KL control, reference-policy resets and a diverse task suite (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liu2025prorl \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.24864) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24864) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.52202/085713-0608)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:liu2026budgetapo": "- **[BudgetAPO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05671 \"How Should a Prompt Optimizer Spend a Tight Budget? BudgetAPO with Noise-Adaptive Evaluation (2026)\")**: How Should a Prompt Optimizer Spend a Tight Budget? BudgetAPO with Noise-Adaptive Evaluation (preprint 2026). A single-stage prompt optimizer for a tight budget of subject-model calls: a 16-call probe measures the task's per-row score spread, a closed-form rule sizes a fixed evaluation slice to a target standard error, every candidate is scored on that same slice so each accept/reject is a paired comparison, and an optimizer LLM rewrites the prompt's reasoning strategy and output format from up to three failing rows (abstract; §3.1–3.3). The authors call it the first APO method to size its evaluation to each task's measured noise before search (§1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liu2026budgetapo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05671) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05671)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:liu2026infogain": "- **[Self-Play Only Evolves When Self-Synthetic Pipeline Ensures Learnable Information Gain](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.02218 \"Self-Play Only Evolves When Self-Synthetic Pipeline Ensures Learnable Information Gain (2026)\")** ((position paper), ICML 2026 (position track)). Argues that self-evolving loops in which one LLM plays proposer, solver and verifier plateau because the loop \"synthesises more data without increasing learnable information\" (abstract), and proposes three designs: asymmetric co-evolution, capacity growth and proactive information seeking (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liu2026infogain \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.02218) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02218)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/survey\">survey</a></span>", "paper:ma2026sepo": "- **[SEPO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.28067 \"SEPO: Evidence-Grounded Prompt Optimization via Structural Editing (2026)\")**: Evidence-Grounded Prompt Optimization via Structural Editing (accepted at Findings of EMNLP 2026). Edits typed units of a structured prompt and links each edit to the examples it fixes or breaks. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/ma2026sepo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.28067) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28067)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:moon2025codejudge": "- **[Don't Judge Code by Its Cover](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.16222 \"Don't Judge Code by Its Cover: Exploring Biases in LLM Judges for Code Evaluation (2025)\")**: Exploring Biases in LLM Judges for Code Evaluation (EACL 2026 Findings, 2025). Tests whether LLM judges grade code consistently under six injected, semantics-preserving biases: authority, self-declared correctness, reverse-authority and misleading-task comments, variable renaming, and unused dummy functions (§1, §3); the abstract also names formatting, which isn't tested. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/moon2025codejudge \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.16222) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16222)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:novikov2025alphaevolve": "- **[AlphaEvolve](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.13131 \"AlphaEvolve: A coding agent for scientific and algorithmic discovery (2025)\")**: A coding agent for scientific and algorithmic discovery (preprint 2025 (Google DeepMind white paper)). An evolutionary coding agent: LLMs propose program changes, automated evaluators score them. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/novikov2025alphaevolve \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.13131) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13131) · [Code](https://github.com/google-deepmind/alphaevolve_results)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:olausson2023selfrepair": "- **[Is Self-Repair a Silver Bullet for Code Generation?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.09896 \"Is Self-Repair a Silver Bullet for Code Generation? (2024)\")** (ICLR 2024). Studies self-repair (the model debugs and repairs its own code from test feedback) with Code Llama, GPT-3.5 and GPT-4 on HumanEval and APPS (abstract; §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/olausson2023selfrepair \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.09896) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.09896) · [Code](https://github.com/theoxo/self-repair)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:padhi2019overfitting": "- **[Overfitting in Synthesis: Theory and Practice](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1905.07457 \"Overfitting in Synthesis: Theory and Practice (2019)\")** (CAV 2019). Observes that syntax-guided synthesis tools built on counterexample-guided inductive synthesis (CEGIS) fail on problems they solve with a less expressive grammar once the grammar grows, and attributes part of it to overfitting: candidates that fit the examples so far but violate the specification (abstract, §2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/padhi2019overfitting \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1905.07457) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1905.07457) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-25540-4_17) · [Code](https://github.com/SaswatPadhi/LoopInvGen)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:plesner2026imperfect": "- **[An Imperfect Verifier is Good Enough](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.07666 \"An Imperfect Verifier is Good Enough: Learning with Noisy Rewards (2026)\")**: Learning with Noisy Rewards (preprint 2026). RLVR on code (MBPP; Qwen3 8B and GLM4 9B, with Llama 3.1 8B and Qwen3 4B ablations; most configurations single-seed, §4) with injected reward noise of controlled rate and structure, and model-based verifiers as rewards (abstract; §3.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/plesner2026imperfect \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.07666) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07666)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:rad2026rlver": "- **[RLVεR (\"Rate or Fate?\")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.04411 \"Rate or Fate? RLV$^\\varepsilon$R: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Noisy Rewards (2026)\")**: Rate or Fate? RLV$^\\varepsilon$R: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Noisy Rewards (preprint 2026). Models GRPO under a verifier with false positives and false negatives as a multi-armed bandit over recurring \"reasoning modes\", which gives a replicator-style flow on the probability simplex (abstract; §2.1; §4; §5). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/rad2026rlver \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.04411) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04411) · [Code](https://github.com/cognichip/Noisy-RL)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/theory\">theory</a></span>", "paper:rajan2026hackability": "- **[Auditing Reward Hackability in Code RL Training Environments](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16062 \"Auditing Reward Hackability in Code RL Training Environments (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Measures how often a code task's test suite accepts a wrong solution: Claude Sonnet 4 writes incorrect patches meant to pass the existing tests, in up to three rounds that read the failure logs, and each is run with the project's own test runner in the task's Docker container; a sample of SWE-bench Verified tasks from two repositories (astropy, django) and of R2E-Gym tasks (abstract; §3.1; §4; §7). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/rajan2026hackability \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16062) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16062)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:ramadan2026apl": "- **[Neural Code Translation of Legacy Code: APL to C\\#](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.13896 \"Neural Code Translation of Legacy Code: APL to C\\# (2026)\")** (preprint 2026 (Technical University of Denmark and SimCorp)). Measures LLM translation of APL, an array language written in its own glyphs, into C#, scored by compiling the C# and running it on input–output test cases, on a 49-function test split of utility functions from production code (§4; §4.2); direct translation (fine-tuned Qwen3-32B and Gemma-4-31b-it, prompted GPT-5 and Claude Opus 4) against three guided routes: a two-stage route through an English description of the program, retrieval of APL idiom documentation, and a loop of up to 5 tries fed compiler errors and failed tests (§4.1; §5.2; Tab. 2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/ramadan2026apl \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.13896) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13896)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rarelang\">rarelang</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:reitich2026correction": "- **[Correction and Corruption](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.18245 \"Correction and Corruption: A Two-Rate View of Error Flow in LLM Protocols (2026)\")**: A Two-Rate View of Error Flow in LLM Protocols (preprint 2026). A paired audit of an added model call: success before and after on the same instances, under one binary rule (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/reitich2026correction \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.18245) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18245)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:salsabil2026survives": "- **[What Survives the Next Model?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.00468 \"What Survives the Next Model? Benchmarking LLM-Based Techniques Against Single-Prompts (2026)\")**: What Survives the Next Model? Benchmarking LLM-Based Techniques Against Single-Prompts (preprint 2026). Tests whether one automatically generated prompt on a newer model beats 35 LLM-based software-engineering techniques from ICSE 2026 (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/salsabil2026survives \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.00468) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00468) · [Code](https://anonymous.4open.science/r/ICSE2027-What_Survives_The_Next_Model)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:sharma2026metaprogramming": "- **[Frontier Coding Agents Use Metaprogramming to Adapt to Unfamiliar Programming Languages](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.10933 \"Frontier Coding Agents Use Metaprogramming to Adapt to Unfamiliar Programming Languages (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Evaluates six deployed coding agents (Claude Opus 4.6, Sonnet 4.6 and Haiku 4.5 in Claude Code, GPT-5.4 xhigh and GPT-5.4 mini in Codex, Kimi K2.5 in OpenCode) on the EsoLang-Bench problems in four esoteric languages, each agent working through a language's problems in one session with file editing, local interpreter runs and hidden-test grading (abstract; §2); the authors report that the strongest agents often avoid writing the target language and instead write Python programs that generate it (abstract; §3.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sharma2026metaprogramming \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.10933) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10933)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rarelang\">rarelang</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:srikanth2026aide2": "- **[Recursive self-improvement of AI research agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.26457 \"Recursive self-improvement of AI research agents (2026)\")** (preprint 2026 (Weco AI)). An outer-loop research agent rewrites the code of an inner-loop tree-search agent (a pared-down AIDE), grades each rewrite on ML-engineering, heuristic-algorithm and harness-engineering tasks under a fixed dollar budget, and keeps the best-graded agent as the next one to edit (abstract; §2.1–2.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/srikanth2026aide2 \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.26457) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26457)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:stroebl2024resampling": "- **[The Limits of Inference Scaling Through Resampling](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.17501 \"The Limits of Inference Scaling Through Resampling (2024)\")** ((v1–v2: \"Inference Scaling fLaws\"), ICLR 2026 (per its code README), 2024). Argues that resampling until a verifier passes is bounded when the verifier has false positives, since resampling can't lower their probability (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/stroebl2024resampling \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.17501) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.17501) · [Code](https://github.com/benediktstroebl/inference-scaling-limits)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:strozzi2026selftraining": "- **[Teacher-Free Self-Training Amplifies but Does Not Compound: A Pass@$K$ Crossover on a Free-Verifier Domain](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.07856 \"Teacher-Free Self-Training Amplifies but Does Not Compound: A Pass@$K$ Crossover on a Free-Verifier Domain (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A generator and a learned critic (LoRA adapters on one 4-bit Qwen3-4B) with the DSL interpreter as a free exact verifier, on a FlashFill-style \"trapdoor\" DSL (abstract; §4.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/strozzi2026selftraining \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.07856) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07856) · [Code](https://github.com/izzortsi/trapdoor-loom)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:tank2026regressiontax": "- **[The Regression Tax](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.22520 \"The Regression Tax: Decomposing Why Skills Help -- and Hurt -- LLM Agents (2026)\")**: Decomposing Why Skills Help -- and Hurt -- LLM Agents (preprint 2026). Splits the pass-rate change from adding a skill library into gains (tasks newly solved) and regressions (tasks a no-skill agent solved and now fails), with exact McNemar tests (abstract; §3.5–3.6; §4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/tank2026regressiontax \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.22520) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22520) · [Code](https://github.com/sentient-agi/meta-skill-creator)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:thakur2023copra": "- **[COPRA](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.04353 \"An In-Context Learning Agent for Formal Theorem-Proving (2024)\")**: An In-Context Learning Agent for Formal Theorem-Proving (COLM 2024). GPT-4 proposes tactics from inside a stateful backtracking search, in Lean and Coq (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/thakur2023copra \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.04353) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.04353) · [Code](https://github.com/trishullab/copra)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:wang2023grammar": "- **[Grammar Prompting](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.19234 \"Grammar Prompting for Domain-Specific Language Generation with Large Language Models (2023)\")**: Grammar Prompting for Domain-Specific Language Generation with Large Language Models (NeurIPS 2023). Few-shot generation of programs in domain-specific languages (DSLs): each demonstration carries the minimal subset of the DSL's BNF grammar needed for its output, and the LLM first predicts such a grammar for the test input, then the program (abstract; §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2023grammar \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.19234) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.19234) · [Code](https://github.com/berlino/grammar-prompting)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a></span>", "paper:wang2025evalnoise": "- **[Measuring all the noises of LLM Evals](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.21326 \"Measuring all the noises of LLM Evals (2025)\")** (preprint 2025 (v3)). Splits the noise of an LLM eval into prediction noise (different answers to the same question), data noise (which questions were sampled) and their total, by the law of total variance, and proposes \"all-pairs comparison\": paired comparison on every pair of models, measuring all three components (abstract; §1; §3.1; §4.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2025evalnoise \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.21326) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21326) · [Code](https://github.com/all-the-noises/eval-arena)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:wang2025qlcoder": "- **[QLCoder](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.08462 \"QLCoder: A Query Synthesizer For Static Analysis of Security Vulnerabilities (2026)\")**: A Query Synthesizer For Static Analysis of Security Vulnerabilities (ICLR 2026). An agent built on Claude Code that writes a CodeQL static-analysis query from a CVE's metadata; a query counts as successful only if it compiles, reports a dataflow path through the patched code in the vulnerable version, and reports none through it in the fixed version (§3.1); the authors report success for 53.4% of 176 Java CVEs (abstract; Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2025qlcoder \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.08462) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08462) · [Code](https://github.com/neuralprogram/qlcoder)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl\">nl2dsl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-datalog\">dsl-datalog</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:wang2026valve": "- **[Certified Long-Horizon Code Agent Evolution via Validation-Gated Skill Optimization](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.32990 \"Certified Long-Horizon Code Agent Evolution via Validation-Gated Skill Optimization (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Long-horizon self-evolution of a code agent with frozen weights: after a task with mixed outcomes, the same LLM contrasts successful and failed trajectories to propose skills and principles, which join a persistent bank only if a paired rerun on a fixed 50-task validation panel gains at least 0.02 in pass rate; a retriever that scores embedding similarity, word overlap and an importance prior (App. B.5) picks items for later tasks (§1; §4.1; App. B.6). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026valve \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.32990) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32990)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:wen2025rlvr": "- **[Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards Implicitly Incentivizes Correct Reasoning in Base LLMs](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.14245 \"Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards Implicitly Incentivizes Correct Reasoning in Base LLMs (2025)\")** (preprint 2025). Revisits pass@k for math and code, and introduces CoT-Pass@K, which also requires correct reasoning steps, judged by an LLM (DeepSeek-R1-0528-Qwen3-8B, §1, §3.1) (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wen2025rlvr \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.14245) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14245)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:wilf2025psv": "- **[PSV (Propose](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.18160 \"Propose, Solve, Verify: Self-Play Through Formal Verification (2025)\")**: Propose, Solve, Verify: Self-Play Through Formal Verification (Solve, Verify), preprint 2025). Self-play where a formal verifier (Verus) checks generated programs, and a proposer writes new specifications at difficulty levels set from the solver's pass rates, easy ones included (§3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wilf2025psv \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.18160) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.18160) · [Code](https://github.com/abwilf/psv)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:woo2025tritonrl": "- **[TritonRL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.17891 \"TritonRL: Training LLMs to Think and Code Triton Without Cheating (2025)\")**: Training LLMs to Think and Code Triton Without Cheating (preprint 2025). RL for Triton kernel generation with multi-layered verification against reward hacking (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/woo2025tritonrl \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.17891) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.17891)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:wu2023lemur": "- **[Lemur](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.04870 \"Lemur: Integrating Large Language Models in Automated Program Verification (2024)\")**: Integrating Large Language Models in Automated Program Verification (ICLR 2024). Combines LLMs and automated reasoners for program verification: LLM oracle calls propose and repair properties, which the verifier checks (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wu2023lemur \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.04870) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.04870) · [Code](https://github.com/wu-haoze/Lemur-program-verification)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/prove\">prove</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:wu2025optimas": "- **[Optimas](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.03041 \"Optimas: Optimizing Compound AI Systems with Globally Aligned Local Rewards (2026)\")**: Optimizing Compound AI Systems with Globally Aligned Local Rewards (ICLR 2026). Optimizes compound AI systems whose components have different configuration types: prompts, hyperparameters, model weights (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wu2025optimas \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.03041) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.03041) · [Code](https://github.com/snap-stanford/optimas)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a></span>", "paper:yang2024sweagent": "- **[SWE-agent](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.15793 \"SWE-agent: Agent-Computer Interfaces Enable Automated Software Engineering (2024)\")**: Agent-Computer Interfaces Enable Automated Software Engineering (NeurIPS 2024). An agent-computer interface (commands, file viewer, linting) designed for LLM agents. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yang2024sweagent \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.15793) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15793) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.52202/079017-1601) · [Code](https://github.com/SWE-agent/SWE-agent)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a></span>", "paper:yue2025rlreasoning": "- **[Does Reinforcement Learning Really Incentivize Reasoning Capacity in LLMs Beyond the Base Model?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.13837 \"Does Reinforcement Learning Really Incentivize Reasoning Capacity in LLMs Beyond the Base Model? (2025)\")** (NeurIPS 2025 (oral)). At large k, base models' pass@k matches or beats their RLVR-trained versions in most runs (abstract; exceptions in its own Table 3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yue2025rlreasoning \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.13837) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13837) · [Code](https://github.com/LeapLabTHU/limit-of-RLVR)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:yuksekgonul2024textgrad": "- **[TextGrad](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.07496 \"TextGrad: Automatic 'Differentiation' via Text (2024)\")**: Automatic \"Differentiation\" via Text (preprint 2024). Backpropagates natural-language feedback through a computation graph of LLM calls and tools. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yuksekgonul2024textgrad \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.07496) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07496) · [Code](https://github.com/zou-group/textgrad)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:zhang2023lpr": "- **[LPR: Large Language Models-Aided Program Reduction](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.13064 \"LPR: Large Language Models-Aided Program Reduction (2024)\")** (ISSTA 2024). Shrinks programs that trigger compiler bugs, in C, Rust and JavaScript, to smaller ones that still trigger the bug, by having an LLM make the language-specific transformations that a language-generic reducer can't (abstract, §1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2023lpr \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.13064) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.13064) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3650212.3652126)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/reduce\">reduce</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:zhang2024aflow": "- **[AFlow](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.10762 \"AFlow: Automating Agentic Workflow Generation (2025)\")**: Automating Agentic Workflow Generation (ICLR 2025). Searches the workflow itself: code-represented graphs of LLM-calling nodes (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2024aflow \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.10762) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10762) · [Code](https://github.com/FoundationAgents/AFlow)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:zhang2025dgm": "- **[Darwin Gödel Machine (DGM)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.22954 \"Darwin G\\'odel Machine: Open-Ended Evolution of Self-Improving Agents (2026)\")**: Darwin G\\\"odel Machine: Open-Ended Evolution of Self-Improving Agents (ICLR 2026). A coding agent edits its own code; each new agent is scored on coding benchmarks and archived if it still compiles and can edit code, so the Gödel machine's proof that a change helps is replaced by empirical validation (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2025dgm \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.22954) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22954) · [Code](https://github.com/jennyzzt/dgm)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:zhang2026coinflip": "- **[Prompt Optimization Is a Coin Flip](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.14585 \"Prompt Optimization Is a Coin Flip: Diagnosing When It Helps in Compound AI Systems (2026)\")**: Diagnosing When It Helps in Compound AI Systems (CTB@ICML 2026 (workshop)). Runs six optimizers on four single-agent tasks, 3 repeats, two executor models (§4, §4.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026coinflip \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.14585) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14585)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:zhang2026leaky": "- **[When the Reward Suite Is Leaky](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.11022 \"When the Reward Suite Is Leaky: A Preregistered Causal Contrast of Natural Verifier False Positives in RLVR (2026)\")**: A Preregistered Causal Contrast of Natural Verifier False Positives in RLVR (preprint 2026). A preregistered two-arm contrast: GRPO on MBPP rewarded by the original tests (leaky) or by the MBPP+ extra tests alone (hardened; not the official base-and-extra metric, §3.1), with the same tasks, seeds and compute (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026leaky \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.11022) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11022) · [Code](https://github.com/toffee-desuwa/rlvr-leaky-suite)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:zhang2026ratchet": "- **[Ratchet: How Reliable Must an LLM Judge Be to Retire a Skill?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.22148 \"Ratchet: How Reliable Must an LLM Judge Be to Retire a Skill? (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A skill library kept by one frozen LLM in five roles over an append-only log: a router picks at most one skill per task, a critic labels failures, a synthesizer writes skills from clustered failure patterns, and a curator retires a skill once its measured contribution (passes minus failures over its routed trials) falls to −τ after enough trials and caps the active library at a fixed width (§3); the authors state a floor on how far below the no-skill control a governed library can fall, finite because of the width and τ (§4, Prop. 1; as printed it fails for skills with fewer than Nmin trials, and their own statistic doesn't meet its unbiasedness assumption even under unit tests). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026ratchet \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.22148) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22148) · [Code](https://github.com/amazon-science/Self-Evolving-Agents-Ratchet)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:zhang2026whoverifies": "- **[Who Verifies the Verifier? Co-Evolving Inspectable Graders with Self-Improving Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.11464 \"Who Verifies the Verifier? Co-Evolving Inspectable Graders with Self-Improving Agents (2026)\")** (NeurIPS 2026 Workshop \"Who Verifies the Agents? Toward Reliable Agent Development\" (PDF p. 1 footer)). Evolves the grader of a self-improving agent rather than the agent: a readable expression (any-of, all-of, votes) over small, mostly deterministic drawback detectors (static checks, execution in a sandbox or a live warehouse, narrow LLM-judge questions), selected for agreement with a ten-item anchored reference set and with detector consensus on unlabeled outputs, \"never for the agent's score\"; paired with the Ratchet skill loop ([Ratchet](#/papers/zhang2026ratchet \"Ratchet: How Reliable Must an LLM Judge Be to Retire a Skill? (2026)\")) as Double Ratchet (abstract; §3.1–3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026whoverifies \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.11464) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11464)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:zhao2025absolutezero": "- **[Absolute Zero](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.03335 \"Absolute Zero: Reinforced Self-play Reasoning with Zero Data (2025)\")**: Reinforced Self-play Reasoning with Zero Data (preprint 2025). One model proposes code-reasoning tasks and solves them; a code executor checks both. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhao2025absolutezero \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.03335) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03335) · [Code](https://github.com/LeapLabTHU/Absolute-Zero-Reasoner)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "paper:zhou2026contraction": "- **[Many Steps into One: Reasoning Contraction and the Limits of Safe Memory Reuse for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17544 \"Many Steps into One: Reasoning Contraction and the Limits of Safe Memory Reuse for LLM Agents (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Treats thought templates, skill libraries, agent memories and caches as one-step memories of multi-step derivations (each a \"contracted memory\") and trusting and retiring them as sequential tests; proves that clipped counters are change detectors testing a change nobody chose and that unclipped ones may never retire a partially stale memory (\"vote counters (ExpeL, ACE) are change detectors tuned to the wrong change\", §8), and gives a limit, in the worst case and as δ → 0, on how much reasoning any safe memory can skip when facts change and each memory is judged only by its own verified outcomes (abstract; §1; §3–4, Thm. 1, Thm. 3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2026contraction \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17544) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17544)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/code\">code</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:zhou2026curriculum": "- **[The Verifier is the Curriculum](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.09709 \"The Verifier is the Curriculum: Precision Sets the Return on Search in Code Self-Distillation (2026)\")**: Precision Sets the Return on Search in Code Self-Distillation (preprint 2026). Rejection-sampling self-distillation of a code generator, gated by whether the generated game project launches cleanly, with no judge (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2026curriculum
