Benchmarking LLM-Based Techniques Against Single-Prompts (2026)\")", "paper:alkiek2025instructionretrieval": "- **[Instruction Retrieval at Inference Time for Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.13935 \"Instruction Retrieval at Inference Time for Small Language Models (2025)\")** (preprint 2025 (v3, 2026)). A teacher (GPT-5) writes one instruction per cluster of a domain's training questions, with background knowledge, a procedure and the common mistakes; a frozen small model retrieves the five instructions nearest its question and follows them, with no fine-tuning and no teacher call at inference (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/alkiek2025instructionretrieval \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.13935) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.13935)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:bozoglan2026slmdbagent": "- **[What Stops a Small Language Model From Driving a Database Agent](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21341 \"What Stops a Small Language Model From Driving a Database Agent (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Drives the agent mode of LibreDB Studio, an open-source SQL client, with open-weight models served locally and one hosted control, across six task surfaces (abstract; §3; Tab. 1); the authors are contributors to the software evaluated (p. 1; § Conflict of interest). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/bozoglan2026slmdbagent \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21341) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21341) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.57967/hf/10485)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:chen2022pot": "- **[PoT (Program of Thoughts prompting)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.12588 \"Program of Thoughts Prompting: Disentangling Computation from Reasoning for Numerical Reasoning Tasks (2023)\")**: Program of Thoughts Prompting: Disentangling Computation from Reasoning for Numerical Reasoning Tasks (TMLR 2023). Prompts an LLM (mainly Codex) to write its reasoning as a Python program with meaningful variable names; a Python interpreter, with SymPy for equations, runs it and its result is the answer, so the computation is taken out of the model's text (abstract, §2.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2022pot \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.12588) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.12588) · [Code](https://github.com/wenhuchen/Program-of-Thoughts)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:chen2024steering": "- **[Steering Large Language Models between Code Execution and Textual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.03524 \"Steering Large Language Models between Code Execution and Textual Reasoning (2025)\")** (ICLR 2025). Measures when LLMs answer by writing and running code rather than in text, and how well 7 existing ways of steering that choice work (prompts asking for code or for text, AutoGen's system prompt, OpenAI's Code Interpreter), on 14 math, logic, robot-planning and symbolic tasks with 6 LLMs (abstract; §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2024steering \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.03524) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03524) · [Code](https://github.com/yongchao98/CodeSteer-v1.0)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:chen2026multiinstance": "- **[Understanding LLM Performance Degradation in Multi-Instance Processing: The Roles of Instance Count and Context Length](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.22608 \"Understanding LLM Performance Degradation in Multi-Instance Processing: The Roles of Instance Count and Context Length (2026)\")** (ACL 2026). Measures 16 LLMs on prompts that hold from 2 to 2,000 items, each easy alone, and ask for one aggregated answer (a count of labels or a sum): eight tasks such as sentiment, language identification, named entities and parity, using only items that every comparison model solves when given them one at a time (abstract; §3.2, §4, Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026multiinstance \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.22608) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.22608) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.acl-long.1470) · [Code](https://github.com/jingxuanchen916/mip-understanding)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/longctx\">longctx</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:chen2026popper": "- **[Reason Popper-ly: Patching In-Context Reasoning with Inductive Logic Programming](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.23019 \"Reason Popper-ly: Patching In-Context Reasoning with Inductive Logic Programming (2026)\")** (accepted at the 20th Conference on Neurosymbolic Learning and Reasoning, 2026). Checks each inferred step of an LLM's chain of thought against a table of kinship-composition rules, classifies each violation into one of four error types, rewrites the wrong step and has the LLM regenerate the rest of the trace from the corrected prefix, then answer from the repaired trace (abstract; §3.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026popper \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.23019) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23019)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a></span>", "paper:huang2024autoscraper": "- **[AutoScraper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.12753 \"AutoScraper: A Progressive Understanding Web Agent for Web Scraper Generation (2024)\")**: A Progressive Understanding Web Agent for Web Scraper Generation (EMNLP 2024 (main)). Has an LLM write a web scraper for one website, a sequence of XPath expressions built from a few seed pages, which a parser then runs on the site's other pages without the LLM (abstract; §3.1; §4.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/huang2024autoscraper \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.12753) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.12753) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.emnlp-main.141) · [Code](https://github.com/EZ-hwh/AutoScraper)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-path\">dsl-path</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-select\">nl2dsl-select</a></span>", "paper:koplow2026depthcollapse": "- **[Tool Use Reduces Depth-Induced Collapse in OOD Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.21061 \"Tool Use Reduces Depth-Induced Collapse in OOD Reasoning (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A synthetic benchmark of out-of-distribution reasoning: the model rebuilds a random Boolean polynomial over GF(2) one term at a time; each step's labels are the next term XORed with a mask made of the earlier terms, which a solver given the earlier terms can strip and which, for one without them, hides each row more strongly as the number of earlier terms (the depth) grows; it measures next-step accuracy against depth, with and without code execution (abstract; §1; §3.1–3.2, Eq. 3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/koplow2026depthcollapse \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.21061) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.21061)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:lin2026procedures": "- **[Can Large Language Models Generalize Procedures Across Representations?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.03542 \"Can Large Language Models Generalize Procedures Across Representations? (2026)\")** (ICML 2026 (PMLR 306)). Trains models on one of three isomorphic forms of the same planning task (an English how-to task with step durations and order constraints, a graph's adjacency list, a Python longest-path function to trace) and tests on all three; the authors report that \"naive training on a single representation fails to reliably generalize across representations\" across Qwen, Llama and Olmo models and four post-training methods (§3.2, Fig. 2; App. A.2), and propose a two-stage RL curriculum, symbolic data first (graph in the planning runs, code in math and physics), then English (abstract, §4, §4.4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lin2026procedures \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.03542) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03542) · [Code](https://github.com/fangru-lin/procedure_generalization_llm)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "paper:meyerson2025maker": "- **[Solving a Million-Step LLM Task with Zero Errors](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.09030 \"Solving a Million-Step LLM Task with Zero Errors (2025)\")** (preprint 2025). Solves a Towers of Hanoi instance whose solution takes over one million LLM steps, with zero errors (abstract; §4.4); the solution strategy is given in every agent's prompt, so only execution is tested (§4.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/meyerson2025maker \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.09030) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09030) · [Code](https://github.com/cognizant-ai-lab/neuro-san-benchmarking)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:prokopiou2026lastcq": "- **[What Drives Recovery in Agentic Text-to-Cypher? LAST-CQ: An LLM Agent Self-Refinement Framework](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.12746 \"What Drives Recovery in Agentic Text-to-Cypher? LAST-CQ: An LLM Agent Self-Refinement Framework (2026)\")** (accepted at REALM, the 2nd Workshop for Research on Agent Language Models at EMNLP 2026). Ablates a training-free five-agent loop that writes Cypher (a query language for graph databases) from English questions: removing correction, replacing its LLM-written, schema-grounded hints with the raw database error string (\"naive retry\"), and spending the same call budget on three parallel samples, over the 2,471-question executable subset of the Neo4j Text2Cypher benchmark with six API models (abstract; §4; §6). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/prokopiou2026lastcq \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.12746) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12746)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-graph\">dsl-graph</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-eval\">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>", "paper:roy2026ycombinator": "- **[λ-RLM (\"The Y-Combinator for LLMs\")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.20105 \"The Y-Combinator for LLMs: Solving Long-Context Rot with $\\lambda$-Calculus (2026)\")**: The Y-Combinator for LLMs: Solving Long-Context Rot with $\\lambda$-Calculus (preprint 2026). Replaces the open-ended loop of Recursive Language Models, in which the model writes Python in a REPL over a prompt stored as a variable, with a fixed library of typed combinators (Split, Peek, Map, Filter, Reduce, Concat, Cross) run by a deterministic executor; the LLM picks a task type from a menu once and is otherwise called on leaf chunks small enough for its context window, plus a final synthesis call for some task types (abstract; §3.1, Tab. 1; §3.2, Alg. 1–2; App. D). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/roy2026ycombinator \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.20105) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20105) · [Code](https://github.com/lambda-calculus-LLM/lambda-RLM)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a></span>", "paper:singh2024tokenization": "- **[Tokenization counts: the impact of tokenization on arithmetic in frontier LLMs](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.14903 \"Tokenization counts: the impact of tokenization on arithmetic in frontier LLMs (2024)\")** (preprint 2024). Measures how the direction in which digits are grouped into tokens changes GPT-3.5's and GPT-4's accuracy on few-shot addition of 7- to 9-digit numbers: the tokenizer's default 3-digit chunks from the left (L2R) against chunks from the right (R2L), forced by writing a comma, or another single-token separator, every three digits from the right (§2; §3.1–3.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/singh2024tokenization \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.14903) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14903) · [Code](https://github.com/aadityasingh/TokenizationCounts)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:suau2026bottleneck": "- **[The Communication Bottleneck](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21509 \"The Communication Bottleneck: A Round-Trip Study of Tree-Structured Expression Serialization in Language Models (2026)\")**: A Round-Trip Study of Tree-Structured Expression Serialization in Language Models ((round-trip protocol, communication matrix), preprint 2026). Measures how much of a tree-shaped expression survives English between two models: a generator model writes an arithmetic expression as a word problem with no math symbols, a separate extractor model (another model or the same one) recovers the expression from the text alone, and SymPy symbolic equivalence decides success; all pairings of 16 models (12 open-weight from 0.6B to 32B, 4 frontier) give a generator × extractor accuracy matrix (abstract; §2.2–2.3; §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/suau2026bottleneck \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21509) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21509)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:sung2025lrr": "- **[LRR (Localized Regex Repair)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.09037 \"Repairing Regex Vulnerabilities via Localization-Guided Instructions (2026)\")**: Repairing Regex Vulnerabilities via Localization-Guided Instructions (EACL 2026 (Volume 1: Long Papers)). Repairs regular expressions vulnerable to ReDoS (crafted inputs that make the regex engine's matching super-linear): a symbolic module localizes the vulnerable sub-pattern, then an LLM, prompted with that sub-pattern and five chain-of-thought repair examples, rewrites the whole regex (abstract; §4; App. B). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sung2025lrr \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.09037) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09037) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.eacl-long.379) · [Code](https://github.com/cdltlehf/LRR)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rewrite\">rewrite</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-regex\">dsl-regex</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:wang2026graphgym": "- **[Unified Multi-Dimensional Benchmark for Complex Graph Reasoning in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.12391 \"Unified Multi-Dimensional Benchmark for Complex Graph Reasoning in Large Language Models (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A semi-automatic framework in which an LLM writes composite graph tasks, their graph generators, reference solvers, graph-loading and evaluation scripts, with human-written tests for the solvers and an LLM quality filter, and a benchmark built with it that varies graph size (10 to 10,000 nodes), the number of composed seed tasks, explicit or story-like descriptions, graph in the prompt or in a file, and textbook or LeetCode-style task sources (abstract; §1; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026graphgym \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.12391) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12391)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/gym\">gym</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:xie2025logicrl": "- **[Logic-RL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.14768 \"Logic-RL: Unleashing LLM Reasoning with Rule-Based Reinforcement Learning (2025)\")**: Unleashing LLM Reasoning with Rule-Based Reinforcement Learning (preprint 2025). RL with a rule-based reward on procedurally generated Knights and Knaves logic puzzles trains Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-1M; the authors report that it develops reasoning skills such as reflection, verification and summarization that are absent from the logic corpus, and generalizes to the math benchmarks AIME and AMC (abstract; §4 \"RQ 4\"). Seven research questions cover, among others, RL algorithms, SFT against RL, curricula and response length (§4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/xie2025logicrl \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.14768) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14768) · [Code](https://github.com/Unakar/Logic-RL)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "paper:xu2025adaptive": "- **[Adaptive LLM-Symbolic Reasoning via Dynamic Logical Solver Composition](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.06774 \"Adaptive LLM-Symbolic Reasoning via Dynamic Logical Solver Composition (2026)\")** (EACL 2026 (Volume 1: Long Papers)). An LLM reads a natural-language input, splits it into its problems and labels each with the kind of formal reasoning it needs (logic programming, first-order logic, constraint satisfaction or SMT); a router sends each problem to the matching solver, whose LLM front end translates it into that solver's language (abstract; §2, Fig. 1, Alg. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/xu2025adaptive \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.06774) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.06774) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.eacl-long.54) · [Code](https://github.com/idiap/adaptive_symbolic_reasoning)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a></span>", "paper:zhang2025rlm": "- **[RLM (Recursive Language Models)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.24601 \"Recursive Language Models (2025)\")**: Recursive Language Models (preprint 2025). An inference scaffold that stores a long prompt as a variable in a Python REPL and lets the model inspect it with code and call itself, or a smaller model, on slices it builds in loops; only short previews of each step's output return to the model (abstract; §2, Alg. 1; smaller sub-call model: §3.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2025rlm \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.24601) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24601) · [Code](https://github.com/alexzhang13/rlm)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/compact\">compact</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "project:adaptive_symbolic_reasoning": "- **[adaptive_symbolic_reasoning](https://github.com/idiap/adaptive_symbolic_reasoning)**: implements [Adaptive LLM-Symbolic Reasoning via…](#/papers/xu2025adaptive \"Adaptive LLM-Symbolic Reasoning via Dynamic Logical Solver Composition (2026)\")", "project:AutoScraper": "- **[AutoScraper](https://github.com/EZ-hwh/AutoScraper)**: implements [AutoScraper](#/papers/huang2024autoscraper \"AutoScraper: A Progressive Understanding Web Agent for Web Scraper Generation (2024)\")", "project:CodeSteer-v1.0": "- **[CodeSteer-v1.0](https://github.com/yongchao98/CodeSteer-v1.0)**: implements [Steering Large Language Models…](#/papers/chen2024steering \"Steering Large Language Models between Code Execution and Textual Reasoning (2025)\")", "project:lambda-RLM": "- **[lambda-RLM](https://github.com/lambda-calculus-LLM/lambda-RLM)**: implements [λ-RLM (\"The Y-Combinator for LLMs\")](#/papers/roy2026ycombinator \"The Y-Combinator for LLMs: Solving Long-Context Rot with $\\lambda$-Calculus (2026)\")", "project:Logic-RL": "- "- **[LRR](https://github.com/cdltlehf/LRR)**: implements [LRR (Localized Regex Repair)](#/papers/sung2025lrr \"Repairing Regex Vulnerabilities via Localization-Guided Instructions (2026)\")", "project:mip-understanding": "- **[mip-understanding](https://github.com/jingxuanchen916/mip-understanding)**: implements [Understanding LLM Performance Degradation…](#/papers/chen2026multiinstance \"Understanding LLM Performance Degradation in Multi-Instance Processing: The Roles of Instance Count and Context Length (2026)\")", "project:neuro-san-benchmarking": "- **[neuro-san-benchmarking](https://github.com/cognizant-ai-lab/neuro-san-benchmarking)**: implements [Solving a Million-Step LLM…](#/papers/meyerson2025maker \"Solving a Million-Step LLM Task with Tests LLMs on syntax error detection, missing-token identification, query performance prediction, query equivalence and query explanation, with labelled datasets built from known workloads (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/rahaman2024sqlunderstanding \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.10680) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10680) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.48786/EDBT.2025.74) · [Code](https://github.com/AnanyaRahaman/LLMs_SQL_Understading)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/pairs-check\">pairs-check</a></span>", "paper:stoisser2025sparks": "- **[Sparks of Tabular Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00016 \"Sparks of Tabular Reasoning via Text2SQL Reinforcement Learning (2025)\")**: Sparks of Tabular Reasoning via Text2SQL Reinforcement Learning (Table Representation Learning Workshop (ACL) 2025). Trains two 7–8B models on text-to-SQL only (§4.1), then tests whether the gain carries over to answering questions over tables (CRT-QA) with no SQL output required (§1, Fig. 1, §4.5). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/stoisser2025sparks \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00016) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00016) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.trl-1.20) · [Code](https://github.com/josefastoisser/sparks_of_tabular_reasoning)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-data\">nl2dsl-data</a></span>", "paper:stoisser2025structllm": "- **[STRuCT-LLM](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.21575 \"STRuCT-LLM: Unifying Tabular and Graph Reasoning with Reinforcement Learning for Semantic Parsing (2025)\")**: Unifying Tabular and Graph Reasoning with Reinforcement Learning for Semantic Parsing (preprint 2025). Trains Qwen models (1.5B to 32B) on text-to-SQL and text-to-Cypher together, then measures transfer: SQL-only, Cypher-only and joint runs compared on Spider, BIRD Mini-Dev, EHRSQL and Text2Cypher (§4.4, Tab. 1), zero-shot on table and knowledge-graph QA (CRT-QA, TableBench, CR-LT-KGQA; §4.5, Tab. 2) and on SM3, whose questions come with equivalent SQL, Cypher and MongoDB queries (App. C, Tab. C1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/stoisser2025structllm \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.21575) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21575) · [Code](https://github.com/bouv/STRuCT-LLM)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-graph\">dsl-graph</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/nl2dsl-data\">nl2dsl-data</a></span>", "paper:wang2026indextuning": "- **[LLM-driven index tuning on SQL Server](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.09181 \"Evaluating the Practical Effectiveness of LLM-Driven Index Tuning on Microsoft SQL Server (2026)\")**: Evaluating the Practical Effectiveness of LLM-Driven Index Tuning on Microsoft SQL Server (PVLDB 19(12) 2026). Compares LLM index recommendations with Microsoft's Database Tuning Advisor (DTA) on industrial benchmarks and real enterprise workloads (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026indextuning \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.09181) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09181)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dbtask\">dbtask</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "project:LLMs_SQL_Understading": "- **[LLMs_SQL_Understading](https://github.com/AnanyaRahaman/LLMs_SQL_Understading)**: implements [Evaluating SQL Understanding in…](#/papers/rahaman2024sqlunderstanding \"Evaluating SQL Understanding in Large Language Models (2025)\")", "project:sparks_of_tabular_reasoning": "- **[sparks_of_tabular_reasoning](https://github.com/josefastoisser/sparks_of_tabular_reasoning)**: implements [Sparks of Tabular Reasoning](#/papers/stoisser2025sparks \"Sparks of Tabular Reasoning via Text2SQL Reinforcement Learning (2025)\")", "project:STRuCT-LLM": "- **[STRuCT-LLM](https://github.com/bouv/STRuCT-LLM)**: implements [STRuCT-LLM](#/papers/stoisser2025structllm \"STRuCT-LLM: Unifying Tabular and Graph Reasoning with Reinforcement Learning for Semantic Parsing (2025)\")", "project:Table-Pretraining": "- **[Table-Pretraining](https://github.com/microsoft/Table-Pretraining)**: implements [TAPEX](#/papers/liu2021tapex \"TAPEX: Table Pre-training via Learning a Neural SQL Executor (2022)\")", "paper:an2026parsing": "- **[SQLFlex](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.16155 \"Dialect-Agnostic SQL Parsing via LLM-Based Segmentation (2026)\")**: Dialect-Agnostic SQL Parsing via LLM-Based Segmentation (PACMMOD 4(3) / SIGMOD 2026). Dialect-agnostic parsing: grammar-based parsing plus LLM segmentation with validation checks. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/an2026parsing \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.16155) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16155) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3802038)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/reduce\">reduce</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-parse\">dialect-parse</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:davis2021lingua": "- **[Why Aren't Regular Expressions a Lingua Franca? An Empirical Study on the Re-use and Portability of Regular Expressions](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2105.04397 \"Why Aren't Regular Expressions a Lingua Franca? An Empirical Study on the Re-use and Portability of Regular Expressions (2019)\")** (ESEC/FSE 2019). A developer survey on regex re-use (§4), a corpus of regexes extracted from the module registries of eight programming languages (§5, Tab. 1), and measurements of how often the same regex is re-used (§6) and behaves differently across those languages' regex engines (§7). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/davis2021lingua \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2105.04397) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.04397) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3338906.3338909) · [Code](https://github.com/SBULeeLab/LinguaFranca-FSE19)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dialect\">dialect</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/difftest\">difftest</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-regex\">dsl-regex</a></span>", "paper:emani2025horizon": "- **[Horizon](#/papers/emani2025horizon \"Horizon: Robust Checks for SQL Migration Using LLMs (2025)\")**: Robust Checks for SQL Migration Using LLMs (PVLDB 18(12) 2025 (demo)). Microsoft's LLM-based schema and code migration, with checks for syntactic completeness and functional equivalence. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/emani2025horizon \"Summary\") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3750601.3750646)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/difftest\">difftest</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:jiang2026acme": "- **[ACME](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3797134 \"ACME: Automated Clause Mapping Engine for Testing Emerging Database Systems (2026)\")**: Automated Clause Mapping Engine for Testing Emerging Database Systems (FSE 2026 (PACMSE)). An LLM maps emerging-DBMS clauses to equivalent relational SQL so the two engines can be tested differentially (abstract, §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/jiang2026acme \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3797134) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3797134) · [Code](https://github.com/YuanchengJiang/ACME)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/difftest\">difftest</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/rules-discover\">rules-discover</a></span>", "paper:lin2024sqless": "- **[SQLess](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3650212.3680317 \"SQLess: Dialect-Agnostic SQL Query Simplification (2024)\")**: Dialect-Agnostic SQL Query Simplification (ISSTA 2024). Shrinks SQL queries that trigger DBMS bugs while keeping the bug, across dialects (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lin2024sqless \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3650212.3680317) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3650212.3680317) · [Code](https://github.com/SQLess/AdaptSQLess)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/reduce\">reduce</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-parse\">dialect-parse</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:lin2025qtran": "- **[QTRAN](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3728908 \"QTRAN: Extending Metamorphic-Oracle Based Logical Bug Detection Techniques for Multiple-DBMS Dialect Support (2025)\")**: Extending Metamorphic-Oracle Based Logical Bug Detection Techniques for Multiple-DBMS Dialect Support (ISSTA 2025 (PACMSE)). An LLM translates metamorphic test-statement pairs into other DBMS dialects, so logic-bug oracles carry across engines (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lin2025qtran \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3728908) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3728908) · [Code](https://github.com/QTRANll/QTRAN)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:oh2026queryhouse": "- **[QueryHouse](https://hacking.kaist.ac.kr/pubs/2026/oh:queryhouse.pdf \"QueryHouse: Cross-DBMS Differential Testing with LLM and Query Transpilation (2026)\")**: Cross-DBMS Differential Testing with LLM and Query Transpilation (ESORICS 2026 (Springer LNCS, pp. 701–721)). Cross-DBMS differential testing for logic bugs: each generated test case is transpiled into the dialects of Oracle, MySQL, PostgreSQL, SQLite and MariaDB, run on all of them, and any difference goes to triage, as a bug if it contradicts official documentation, else as an undocumented inconsistency (§1, PDF p. 2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/oh2026queryhouse \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://hacking.kaist.ac.kr/pubs/2026/oh:queryhouse.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-032-38692-2_35) · [Code: QueryHouse](https://github.com/queryhouse-2037/QueryHouse) · [Code: QueryHouse-sqlglot](https://github.com/queryhouse-2037/QueryHouse-sqlglot)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/difftest\">difftest</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/workload\">workload</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:slutz1998rags": "- **[Massive Stochastic Testing of SQL](https://www.vldb.org/conf/1998/p618.pdf \"Massive Stochastic Testing of SQL (1998)\")** (VLDB 1998). Generates random SQL statements and runs each one on several vendors' DBMSs, comparing row counts and a checksum of the results (§2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/slutz1998rags \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://www.vldb.org/conf/1998/p618.pdf)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dialect\">dialect</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/difftest\">difftest</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/reduce\">reduce</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/workload\">workload</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:xie2026rise": "- **[RISE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.05579 \"RISE: Rule-Driven SQL Dialect Translation via Query Reduction (2026)\")**: Rule-Driven SQL Dialect Translation via Query Reduction (ICSE 2026). A formal typing and cast semantics (TRAF) for SQL engines, instantiated for PostgreSQL, SQL Server, MySQL, SQLite and Oracle. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/ye2026traf \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3834861) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3834861) · [Code](https://github.com/YeWenjia/Elucidating-Type-Conversions-in-SQL-Engines)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/dialect\">dialect</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a></span>", "paper:yu2026agentmigrate": "- **[AgentMigrate](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3810991.3811630 \"Bridging the Procedural Code Gap: Agentic Database Migration with Execution-Grounded Validation (2026)\")**: Bridging the Procedural Code Gap: Agentic Database Migration with Execution-Grounded Validation (DBTest@SIGMOD 2026). Agentic migration of Oracle stored procedures to PostgreSQL: translate, compile, execute, diff results, repair. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yu2026agentmigrate \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3810991.3811630) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3810991.3811630)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/difftest\">difftest</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dialect-translate\">dialect-translate</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/dsl-sql\">dsl-sql</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-gen\">llm-gen</a></span>", "paper:zhou2025cracksql": "- **[CrackSQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.00882 \"Cracking SQL Barriers: An LLM-based Dialect Translation System (2025)\")**: Cracking SQL Barriers: An LLM-based Dialect Translation System (PACMMOD / SIGMOD 2025).
