Execution Labels Set the Reported Risk of Conformal Abstention for Text-to-SQL (2026)\")", "project:WeTune": "- **[WeTune](https://github.com/WeTune/WeTune-code)**: implements [WeTune](#/papers/wang2022wetune \"WeTune: Automatic Discovery and Verification of Query Rewrite Rules (2022)\")", "paper:sileo2026reasoningcore": "- **[Reasoning Core](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.05148 \"Reasoning Core: Designing Broad Procedural Data for Completion-Supervised Reasoning Training (2026)\")**: Designing Broad Procedural Data for Completion-Supervised Reasoning Training (preprint 2026). A library of procedural generators of checkable reasoning tasks (e.g. mathematics, logic, planning, formal languages, structured data, code), each with a semantic scorer and difficulty controls, compared under matched supervised fine-tuning with Procedural Warmup, Reasoning Gym and SynLogic on held-out benchmarks (abstract; §3, Tab. 1; §4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sileo2026reasoningcore \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.05148) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05148) · [Code](https://github.com/sileod/reasoning-core)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/gym\">gym</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "project:reasoning-core": "- **[reasoning-core](https://github.com/sileod/reasoning-core)**: implements [Reasoning Core](#/papers/sileo2026reasoningcore \"Reasoning Core: Designing Broad Procedural Data for Completion-Supervised Reasoning Training (2026)\")", "paper:ammanamanchi2026leandefects": "- **[Faults in Our Formal Benchmarking](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.29493 \"Faults in Our Formal Benchmarking: Dataset Defects and Evaluation Failures in Lean Theorem Proving (2026)\")**: Dataset Defects and Evaluation Failures in Lean Theorem Proving (ICML 2026). Audits five Lean theorem-proving benchmarks (miniF2F, ProofNet, FormalMath, CombiBench, ProverBench) and their forks, with a fault taxonomy: fidelity failures in formalization, evaluation-time loopholes, and maintenance decay (abstract; §1; Tab. 1; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/ammanamanchi2026leandefects \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.29493) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29493) · [Code](https://github.com/Shashi456/atp-checkers)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:bhaskar2025rlmt": "- **[RLMT (\"Language Models that Think](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.20357 \"Language Models that Think, Chat Better (2026)\")**: Language Models that Think, Chat Better (Chat Better\"), COLM 2026). Trains 7–8B models to write a long chain of thought before each chat response, rewarded by a learned preference reward model as in RLHF instead of a verifier, and pairs each run with a matched non-thinking RLHF run (abstract; §2.2, §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/bhaskar2025rlmt \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.20357) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20357) · [Code](https://github.com/princeton-pli/RLMT)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "paper:egashira2026systematic": "- **[Delay, Plateau, or Collapse](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.02909 \"Delay, Plateau, or Collapse: Evaluating the Impact of Systematic Verification Error on RLVR (2026)\")**: Evaluating the Impact of Systematic Verification Error on RLVR ((systematic verification error in RLVR), COLM 2026 (the PDF's running header, p. 1)). Separates random verifier noise, which flips rewards independently of the output, from systematic errors, which depend on the query and the output, so a model can learn to trigger or avoid them; it names three outcomes against clean training: delay, plateau and collapse (§3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/egashira2026systematic \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.02909) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.02909) · [Code](https://github.com/eth-sri/llm-verifier-noise)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:gao2022overoptimization": "- **[Scaling Laws for Reward Model Overoptimization](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2210.10760 \"Scaling Laws for Reward Model Overoptimization (2023)\")** (ICML 2023). A fixed 6B \"gold\" reward model stands in for human labellers and labels comparisons that train proxy reward models of 3M to 3B parameters; a GPT-3-series policy in the InstructGPT setting is optimized against the proxy by best-of-n or by PPO, and the gold score is tracked as optimization grows (abstract; §2, §2.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/gao2022overoptimization \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2210.10760) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.10760)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:hou2026grpo": "- **[Prompt Dominance and Asymmetric Verifier Costs: Empirical Ablations of GRPO at 1B Scale on GSM8K](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.04928 \"Prompt Dominance and Asymmetric Verifier Costs: Empirical Ablations of GRPO at 1B Scale on GSM8K (2026)\")** ((GRPO at 1B on GSM8K), preprint 2026). A from-scratch GRPO implementation trains OLMo-2-0425-1B on GSM8K with a symbolic-equality grader as the reward, with prompt and learning-rate ablations, one-change estimator variants (Dr. GRPO among them) and off-policy corrections (clipped, unclipped, GSPO); two seeds per configuration except the learning-rate sweep and the verifier study's exact arm (abstract; §2–5; Fig. 2; §6.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/hou2026grpo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.04928) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04928) · [Code](https://github.com/Helios-YQH/llm-from-scratch)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:hu2026winnerscurse": "- **[The Winner's Curse in LLM Self-Improvement Loops: Selection Noise, Lock-in, and Acceptance Rules](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.09239 \"The Winner's Curse in LLM Self-Improvement Loops: Selection Noise, Lock-in, and Acceptance Rules (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Models the keep-if-better step of self-improvement loops as selection under measurement noise, with candidates of a generation sharing the incumbent's error, and studies what reusing the selection set does (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/hu2026winnerscurse \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.09239) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09239)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:kiyani2026weakstrong": "- **[When to Trust the Cheap Check](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.17633 \"When to Trust the Cheap Check: Weak and Strong Verification for Reasoning (2026)\")**: Weak and Strong Verification for Reasoning ((weak and strong verification, SSV), preprint 2026). A policy decides for each output whether to accept or reject it on a cheap \"weak\" verifier's score (self-consistency, proxy rewards) or to defer to a costly \"strong\" verifier (a user, a check against ground truth); its errors are incorrect acceptance (type I) and incorrect rejection (type II) relative to the strong verdict, plus how often the strong verifier is called (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/kiyani2026weakstrong \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.17633) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17633) · [Code](https://github.com/nooranisima/weak-and-strong-verification)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:kong2025rtrl": "- **[RTRL (Round-Trip Reinforcement Learning)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.01527 \"Round-trip Reinforcement Learning: Self-Consistent Training for Better Chemical LLMs (2026)\")**: Round-trip Reinforcement Learning: Self-Consistent Training for Better Chemical LLMs (COLM 2026). Trains a chemical LLM for round-trip consistency on paired tasks (molecule ↔ caption, reactants ↔ product): the model's output for an input is rewarded by how likely a frozen copy of the model finds the original input when mapping back, so the training data can be unlabelled inputs from one side only (abstract; §4, §4.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/kong2025rtrl \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.01527) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01527) · [Code](https://github.com/LechengKong/RTRL)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:lightman2023verify": "- **[Let's Verify Step by Step](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.20050 \"Let's Verify Step by Step (2023)\")** (preprint 2023). Process supervision (each step labeled) beats outcome supervision for reward models, on MATH (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lightman2023verify \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.20050) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.20050) · [Code](https://github.com/openai/prm800k)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:moya2026verifiererrors": "- **[Verifier Errors in RLVR](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.35677 \"Verifier Errors in RLVR: Reward Hacking, Limits of Feedback, and Selective Control (2026)\")**: Reward Hacking, Limits of Feedback, and Selective Control (preprint 2026). When does reward rise while correctness falls under an imperfect verifier? (abstract) <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/moya2026verifiererrors \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.35677) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35677) · [Code](https://github.com/cmoyacal/verifier-errors)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/theory\">theory</a></span>", "paper:saadfalcon2025weaver": "- **[Weaver](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.18203 \"Shrinking the Generation-Verification Gap with Weak Verifiers (2025)\")**: Shrinking the Generation-Verification Gap with Weak Verifiers (NeurIPS 2025). Builds a strong verifier from many weak ones (LM judges, reward models) to rank sampled answers (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/saadfalcon2025weaver \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.18203) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.18203)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:shao2025spurious": "- **[Spurious Rewards](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.10947 \"Spurious Rewards: Rethinking Training Signals in RLVR (2025)\")**: Rethinking Training Signals in RLVR (preprint 2025). RLVR improves some models even with random or wrong rewards. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/shao2025spurious \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.10947) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10947) · [Code](https://github.com/ruixin31/Spurious_Rewards)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:shawn2026pace": "- **[PACE: Anytime-Valid Acceptance Tests for Self-Evolving Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.08106 \"PACE: Anytime-Valid Acceptance Tests for Self-Evolving Agents (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Treats the keep-it-if-the-dev-score-went-up step of self-evolving agents as uncontrolled adaptive multiple testing on a reused dev set, and replaces it with a commit gate: candidate and incumbent are scored on the same items, and a testing-by-betting e-process on the discordant pairs commits once its wealth reaches 1/α (abstract; §3; §4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/shawn2026pace \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.08106) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08106)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a></span>", "paper:si2026ctx2skill": "- **[From Context to Skills: Can Language Models Learn from Context Skillfully?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.27660 \"From Context to Skills: Can Language Models Learn from Context Skillfully? (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Builds a natural-language skill set for one long, unseen context (a short Markdown file prepended to the system prompt) so that an LLM answers new tasks over that context better, with no human annotation, no external feedback and no weight updates (abstract; §3.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/si2026ctx2skill \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.27660) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27660) · [Code](https://github.com/S1s-Z/Ctx2Skill)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:snell2024scaling": "- **[compute-optimal test-time scaling](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.03314 \"Scaling LLM Test-Time Compute Optimally can be More Effective than Scaling Model Parameters (2024)\")**: Scaling LLM Test-Time Compute Optimally can be More Effective than Scaling Model Parameters (preprint 2024). Compares search against verifiers with sequential revisions, by problem difficulty. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/snell2024scaling \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.03314) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03314)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:sun2026reuse": "- **[Which Self-Improvements Should We Trust? Reliable Self-Improvement When Agents Reuse Their Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.33180 \"Which Self-Improvements Should We Trust? Reliable Self-Improvement When Agents Reuse Their Benchmarks (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A certified evaluation and promotion rule for self-improvement loops that reuse one fixed evaluation set: with probability at least 1−α every promoted change is a genuine improvement on the task distribution (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sun2026reuse \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.33180) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33180)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a></span>", "paper:uesato2022process": "- **[process- vs outcome-based feedback](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.14275 \"Solving math word problems with process- and outcome-based feedback (2022)\")**: Solving math word problems with process- and outcome-based feedback (preprint 2022). Compares outcome-based and process-based supervision on GSM8K (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/uesato2022process \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.14275) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.14275)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:wang2025falsepositives": "- **[False Positives under Inference Scaling](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.06217 \"Examining False Positives under Inference Scaling for Mathematical Reasoning (2025)\")**: Examining False Positives under Inference Scaling for Mathematical Reasoning (preprint 2025). Measures correct final answers reached through flawed reasoning in math, across models and datasets (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2025falsepositives \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.06217) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06217) · [Code](https://github.com/Wloner0809/False-Positives-in-Math)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:wu2026vrrstop": "- **[VRR-Stop (\"Verify](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.17641 \"Verify, Repair, Repeat, or Stop? Robust Stopping for Noisy Verify-Repair Loops in LLM Agents (2026)\")**: Verify, Repair, Repeat, or Stop? Robust Stopping for Noisy Verify-Repair Loops in LLM Agents (Repair, Repeat, or Stop?\"), preprint 2026). When to stop an LLM's verify-repair loop when both the verifier and the repairer err: a four-parameter model (false accepts, false rejects, repairs, damage) turns each round's repeated verifier votes into a belief that the current plan is valid, and the loop repairs only while the expected gain of one more round is positive; VRR-Guard, a fallback for verifiers that barely separate valid from invalid plans, keeps the best candidate and replaces it only on a margin of votes (abstract; §3.2; §4.1–4.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wu2026vrrstop \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.17641) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17641) · [Code](https://anonymous.4open.science/r/vrr-artifact-2583)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:xu2026harnessrepair": "- **[What Does a Harness Repair? A Preregistered Study of Visibility, Baseline Adequacy and Evaluation Defects](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05533 \"What Does a Harness Repair? A Preregistered Study of Visibility, Baseline Adequacy and Evaluation Defects (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Two preregistered studies of where the gains of harness search come from, a search that keeps a change to the prompts, reasoning switches, token budgets or parsers around a frozen model if it raises a score: three models of 1.7B to 8B parameters (a longevity-tuned Qwen3-1.7B, SmolLM3-3B, Intern-S1-mini), then Qwen3-14B, on LongevityBench, MMLU-Pro and GSM8K pools with replication and test partitions, a GEPA arm in each study that searches prompt text only, and a descriptive comparison of the tuned and base Qwen3-1.7B (abstract; §1; §3.1; §3.6; Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/xu2026harnessrepair \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05533) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05533)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:yang2026hackprobe": "- **[HackProbe](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.04665 \"Harness-agnostic detection and immunization of reward hacking in self-evolving language models (2026)\")**: Harness-agnostic detection and immunization of reward hacking in self-evolving language models (preprint 2026). A monitor for reward hacking in self-evolving loops that attaches through two black-box hooks (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026hackprobe \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.04665) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04665)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a></span>", "paper:yoon2026calif": "- **[CaLIF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19525 \"Diagnosing and Correcting Reward Hacking in Verifiable Instruction Following (2026)\")**: Diagnosing and Correcting Reward Hacking in Verifiable Instruction Following (preprint 2026). Verifiable instruction-following benchmarks such as IFBench check only the output constraints appended to a request, by program; the authors show that frontier models can receive full credit for responses with little answer content, and that RL with the verifier as reward raises constraint satisfaction while lowering LLM-judged response quality: in all three released OLMo 3 DPO–RLVR checkpoint pairs, whose RL includes this task among others, and in their own IF-RLVR runs (abstract; §1; §3; §5.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yoon2026calif \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19525) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19525)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "paper:zhou2026convincing": "- **[More Convincing, Not More Correct](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.05904 \"More Convincing, Not More Correct: Self-Play Reward Hacking of Reference-Free LLM Judges (2026)\")**: Self-Play Reward Hacking of Reference-Free LLM Judges (preprint 2026). Self-play against a reference-free LLM judge: one model answers GSM8K questions, judges its own answers without a reference, and is trained by DPO on the judge's accept/reject pairs, while a hidden anchor, an exact-match check on the final answer that the judge never sees, audits true accuracy (abstract; §3; §5). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2026convincing \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.05904) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05904)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/hacking\">hacking</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "project:atp-checkers": "- **[atp-checkers](https://github.com/Shashi456/atp-checkers)**: implements [Faults in Our Formal Benchmarking](#/papers/ammanamanchi2026leandefects \"Faults in Our Formal Benchmarking: Dataset Defects and Evaluation Failures in Lean Theorem Proving (2026)\")", "project:Ctx2Skill": "- **[Ctx2Skill](https://github.com/S1s-Z/Ctx2Skill)**: implements [From Context to Skills](#/papers/si2026ctx2skill \"From Context to Skills: Can Language Models Learn from Context Skillfully? (2026)\")", "project:False-Positives-in-Math": "- **[False-Positives-in-Math](https://github.com/Wloner0809/False-Positives-in-Math)**: implements [False Positives under Inference Scaling](#/papers/wang2025falsepositives \"Examining False Positives under Inference Scaling for Mathematical Reasoning (2025)\")", "project:llm-from-scratch": "- Ablations of GRPO at 1B Scale on GSM8K (2026)\")", "project:llm-verifier-noise": "- **[llm-verifier-noise](https://github.com/eth-sri/llm-verifier-noise)**: implements [Delay, Plateau, or Collapse](#/papers/egashira2026systematic \"Delay, Plateau, or Collapse: Evaluating the Impact of Systematic Verification Error on RLVR (2026)\")", "project:prm800k": "- **[prm800k](https://github.com/openai/prm800k)**: implements [Let's Verify Step by Step](#/papers/lightman2023verify \"Let's Verify Step by Step (2023)\")", "project:RLMT": "- **[RLMT](https://github.com/princeton-pli/RLMT)**: implements [RLMT (\"Language Models that Think](#/papers/bhaskar2025rlmt \"Language Models that Think, Chat Better (2026)\")", "project:RTRL": "- Chemical LLMs (2026)\")", "project:Spurious_Rewards": "- **[Spurious_Rewards](https://github.com/ruixin31/Spurious_Rewards)**: implements [Spurious Rewards](#/papers/shao2025spurious \"Spurious **[vrr-artifact](https://anonymous.4open.science/r/vrr-artifact-2583)**: implements [VRR-Stop (\"Verify](#/papers/wu2026vrrstop \"Verify, Repair, Repeat, or Stop? Robust Stopping for Noisy Verify-Repair Loops in LLM Agents (2026)\")", "project:weak-and-strong-verification": "- **[weak-and-strong-verification](https://github.com/nooranisima/weak-and-strong-verification)**: implements [When to Trust the Cheap Check](#/papers/kiyani2026weakstrong \"When to Trust the Cheap Check: Weak and Strong Verification for Reasoning (2026)\")", "paper:alzubi2026evoskill": "- **[EvoSkill](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.02766 \"EvoSkill: Automated Skill Discovery for Multi-Agent Systems (2026)\")**: Automated Skill Discovery for Multi-Agent Systems (preprint 2026). Grows a coding agent's library of skill folders (instructions, trigger metadata, helper scripts) from the agent's failures on labeled training questions, with the model frozen (abstract; §2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/alzubi2026evoskill \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.02766) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02766) · [Code](https://github.com/sentient-agi/EvoSkill)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:amonkar2025realitycheck": "- **[A Reality Check of Language Models as Formalizers on Constraint Satisfaction Problems](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.13252 \"A Reality Check of Language Models as Formalizers on Constraint Satisfaction Problems (2026)\")** (COLM 2026 Workshop on Efficient Reasoning (v6 PDF header)). Compares LLMs that translate a constraint satisfaction problem into a program an external solver or interpreter runs (LLM-as-formalizer: free-form Python, or Python code for the Z3 SMT solver) with the same LLMs answering directly (LLM-as-solver), with 4 reasoning and 2 non-reasoning models on three NaturalPlan planning domains (calendar, trip and meeting planning) and ZebraLogic logic grid puzzles (abstract; §1; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/amonkar2025realitycheck \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.13252) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13252) · [Code](https://github.com/rikhil-amonkar/llm-csp)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:amrollahi2026roundtrip": "- **[Faithful Autoformalization via Roundtrip Verification and Repair](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.25031 \"Faithful Autoformalization via Roundtrip Verification and Repair (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Checks an LLM's translation of a natural-language rule into logic with no reference translation: formalize, translate back to English, formalize again, and ask an SMT solver whether the two formalizations are equivalent; when they are not, an LLM judge names the first translation step that lost meaning and only that step is repaired (abstract; §3–§4, Alg. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/amrollahi2026roundtrip \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.25031) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25031) · [Code](https://github.com/daneshvar-amrollahi/roundtrip-autoformalization)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/smt\">smt</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:anchan2026handofftax": "- **[Attention Tax, Handoff Tax](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.06069 \"Attention Tax, Handoff Tax: A Stylised Model of When Multi-Agent LLM Systems Help (2026)\")**: A Stylised Model of When Multi-Agent LLM Systems Help (preprint 2026). A stylised reliability model of single- against multi-agent LLM systems: per-step failure rises with the context an agent attends over, handing work to a fresh agent costs a \"handoff tax\" per boundary, and parallel samples share part of their failures (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/anchan2026handofftax \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.06069) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06069) · [Code](https://github.com/akshitanchan/attention-handoff-tax)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/theory\">theory</a></span>", "paper:berlotattwell2025libraryfails": "- **[Is This LLM Library Learning? Evaluation Must Account For Compute and Behaviour](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.03048 \"Is This LLM Library Learning? Evaluation Must Account For Compute and Behaviour (2026)\")** (EACL 2026). Re-runs three in-context library-learning systems, LEGO-Prover (Isabelle lemmas, miniF2F), TroVE and AgentOptimizer (Python functions and tools, MATH and table QA), and measures whether what they learn is reused directly (all three, §3.1–3.2) or by editing (LEGO-Prover only, §3.2) (abstract; §1; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/berlotattwell2025libraryfails \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.03048) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03048) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.eacl-long.163) · [Code](https://github.com/ikb-a/llm_lib_learning_fails)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:cai2023toolmakers": "- **[Large Language Models as Tool Makers](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.17126 \"Large Language Models as Tool Makers (2024)\")** (ICLR 2024). A strong model writes a reusable Python function (a tool) for a class of tasks; a lighter model solves each instance by calling it (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/cai2023toolmakers \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.17126) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.17126) · [Code](https://github.com/ctlllll/LLM-ToolMaker)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:chen2025seedprover": "- **[Seed-Prover](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.23726 \"Seed-Prover: Deep and Broad Reasoning for Automated Theorem Proving (2025)\")**: Deep and Broad Reasoning for Automated Theorem Proving (preprint 2025). A lemma-style Lean whole-proof model that refines its proof from Lean feedback, proved lemmas and self-summaries (abstract), trained with RL (§2.2.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2025seedprover \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.23726) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23726) · [Code](https://github.com/ByteDance-Seed/Seed-Prover)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:chen2026adaptivegepa": "- **[Adaptive-GEPA](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.38762 \"Adaptive-GEPA: Make Your Harness Fit Heterogeneous Requests (2026)\")**: Make Your Harness Fit Heterogeneous Requests (preprint 2026). Evolves a router and a library of specialist programs under one search budget. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026adaptivegepa \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.38762) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38762)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:feng2025retool": "- **[ReTool](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.11536 \"ReTool: Reinforcement Learning for Strategic Tool Use in LLMs (2026)\")**: Reinforcement Learning for Strategic Tool Use in LLMs (ICLR 2026). Trains an LLM with reinforcement learning to interleave Python code, run in a sandbox, with its text reasoning on competition math, rewarded only on whether the final answer matches (abstract; §2.3.1, Eq. 2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/feng2025retool \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.11536) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11536) · [Code](https://github.com/ReTool-RL/ReTool)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:hu2024adas": "- **[ADAS](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.08435 \"Automated Design of Agentic Systems (2024)\")**: Automated Design of Agentic Systems (ICLR 2025 (per the paper's running header, PDF p. 1), 2024). Meta Agent Search: a meta agent programs new agents in code (abstract), building on an archive of discovered agents (App. B). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/hu2024adas \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.08435) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08435) · [Code](https://github.com/ShengranHu/ADAS)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:huang2023selfcorrect": "- **[Large Language Models Cannot Self-Correct Reasoning Yet](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.01798 \"Large Language Models Cannot Self-Correct Reasoning Yet (2024)\")** (ICLR 2024). Tests intrinsic self-correction, where a model reviews its own answer and answers again with no external feedback, on GSM8K and CommonSenseQA with GPT-3.5, GPT-4, GPT-4-Turbo and Llama-2, and on HotpotQA with GPT-3.5 and GPT-4 (§2; §3.1, Tabs. 3–4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/huang2023selfcorrect \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.01798) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.01798)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:kambhampati2024llmmodulo": "- **[LLM-Modulo (\"LLMs Can't Plan](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.01817 \"Position: LLMs Can't Plan, But Can Help Planning in LLM-Modulo Frameworks (2024)\")**: Position: LLMs Can't Plan, But Can Help Planning in LLM-Modulo Frameworks (But Can Help Planning in LLM-Modulo Frameworks\"), ICML 2024 position paper). Argues that autoregressive LLMs cannot plan or verify plans by themselves, so cannot improve by critiquing their own output, drawing mainly on the authors' earlier studies (abstract; §2.1–2.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/kambhampati2024llmmodulo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.01817) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01817)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/survey\">survey</a></span>", "paper:kassianik2026fapo": "- **[FAPO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.19605 \"FAPO: Fully Automated Prompt Optimization of Multi-Step LLM Pipelines (2026)\")**: Fully Automated Prompt Optimization of Multi-Step LLM Pipelines (preprint 2026). Claude Code optimizes a multi-step LLM pipeline inside a standard codebase. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/kassianik2026fapo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.19605) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19605) · [Code](https://github.com/cisco-foundation-ai/fully-automated-prompt-optimization)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:li2026selfmodifying": "- **[Self-Modifying Lean Proof Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.17352 \"Self-Modifying Lean Proof Agents with Verifier-Grounded Benchmark Coevolution (2026)\")**: Self-Modifying Lean Proof Agents with Verifier-Grounded Benchmark Coevolution (preprint 2026). A fixed, trusted runtime wraps a mutable workspace (proof workflow, prompts, tools) that the agent rewrites between generations, around one fixed LLM backend (abstract; App. A). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2026selfmodifying \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.17352) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17352)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:lu2026spear": "- **[SPEAR](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.26275 \"SPEAR: Code-Augmented Agentic Prompt Optimization (2026)\")**: Code-Augmented Agentic Prompt Optimization (preprint (EMNLP 2026 submission)). A free-form agentic optimizer with tools: evaluate, a Python sandbox, set_prompt, finish. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lu2026spear \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.26275) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26275)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:mirzaei2026samplemore": "- **[Sample More, Reflect Less](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.28576 \"Sample More, Reflect Less: Self-Refine and Reflexion Lose to Repeated Sampling at Equal Token Cost, from 1.5B to 7B (2026)\")**: Self-Refine and Reflexion Lose to Repeated Sampling at Equal Token Cost, from 1.5B to 7B (preprint 2026). Compares seven reasoning methods (single-sample, self-inspection, best-of-N self-verification, debate) with repeated sampling at equal measured token cost, on 1.5B–7B open models (four of the seven at 7B) and two math benchmarks (abstract; methods in §4.3 Tab. 1; 7B rows in Tab. 2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/mirzaei2026samplemore \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.28576) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28576)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:nasvytis2026core": "- **[CORE (Contrastive Reflection)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.28742 \"CORE: Contrastive Reflection Enables Rapid Improvements in Reasoning (2026)\")**: CORE: Contrastive Reflection Enables Rapid Improvements in Reasoning (preprint 2026). Compares successful and unsuccessful reasoning traces and distills the differences into short natural-language insights (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/nasvytis2026core \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.28742) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28742) · [Code](https://github.com/LinasNas/core-reasoning)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:pan2023logiclm": "- **[Logic-LM](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.12295 \"Logic-LM: Empowering Large Language Models with Symbolic Solvers for Faithful Logical Reasoning (2023)\")**: Empowering Large Language Models with Symbolic Solvers for Faithful Logical Reasoning (Findings of EMNLP 2023). An LLM translates a logic problem stated in natural language into a symbolic formulation, and a deterministic solver infers the answer from it; when the solver returns an error, the LLM is prompted with the error message to revise the formulation (abstract; §3, §3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/pan2023logiclm \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.12295) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.12295) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.findings-emnlp.248) · [Code](https://github.com/teacherpeterpan/Logic-LLM)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a></span>", "paper:rishav2026contraprompt": "- **[ContraPrompt](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.17937 \"ContraPrompt: Contrastive Prompt Optimization via Dyadic Reasoning Trace Analysis (2026)\")**: Contrastive Prompt Optimization via Dyadic Reasoning Trace Analysis (ICLR 2026 LLM Reasoning Workshop (PDF running head)). Contrastive optimization from paired reasoning traces (a failed and a successful attempt on the same input; abstract, §1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/rishav2026contraprompt \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.17937) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17937) · [Code](https://github.com/rishvv/contraprompt_artefacts)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:singh2026snowball": "- **[The Hallucination Snowball](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.14588 \"The Hallucination Snowball: Modeling Error Propagation as State Transitions in Multi-Agent LLM Pipelines (2026)\")**: Modeling Error Propagation as State Transitions in Multi-Agent LLM Pipelines (ICML 2026 FAGEN workshop). Injects wrong numbers into the first agent's output of a four-agent LangGraph pipeline (Researcher → Analyst → Writer → Reviewer) on FinanceBench questions and tracks at each stage whether a gpt-4o judge without ground truth and a numeric matcher against reference values still detect them (§3.1–3.2); a second experiment compares five LLMs as skeptics at the first stage (§3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/singh2026snowball \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.14588) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14588) · [Code](https://github.com/prabhjotschugh/hallucination-snowball)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:stechly2024selfverification": "- **[On the Self-Verification Limitations of Large Language Models on Reasoning and Planning Tasks](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.08115 \"On the Self-Verification Limitations of Large Language Models on Reasoning and Planning Tasks (2024)\")** (preprint 2024). GPT-4 critiquing its own answers vs a sound external verifier, on Game of 24, graph coloring and STRIPS planning (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/stechly2024selfverification \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.08115) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08115)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:suzgun2025cheatsheet": "- **[Dynamic Cheatsheet](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.07952 \"Dynamic Cheatsheet: Test-Time Learning with Adaptive Memory (2025)\")**: Test-Time Learning with Adaptive Memory (preprint 2025). A persistent, evolving memory of strategies and code snippets reused across problems at test time. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/suzgun2025cheatsheet \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.07952) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07952) · [Code](https://github.com/suzgunmirac/dynamic-cheatsheet)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:wang2023legoprover": "- **[LEGO-Prover](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.00656 \"LEGO-Prover: Neural Theorem Proving with Growing Libraries (2024)\")**: Neural Theorem Proving with Growing Libraries (ICLR 2024). Proves Isabelle theorems with ChatGPT block by block: a decomposer proposes sub-goal lemmas, a formalizer writes the proof using lemmas retrieved from a growing skill library, and an evolver generalizes library lemmas and proves the requested sub-goals (abstract; §3; ChatGPT, §4.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2023legoprover \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.00656) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.00656) · [Code](https://github.com/wiio12/LEGO-Prover)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:wang2026limits": "- **[Know Your Limits](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16118 \"Know Your Limits: On the Faithfulness of LLMs as Solvers and Autoformalizers in Legal Reasoning (2026)\")**: On the Faithfulness of LLMs as Solvers and Autoformalizers in Legal Reasoning ((LLMs as solvers and autoformalizers in legal reasoning), preprint 2026 (accepted by the AI4Law and AI4Math workshops at ICML)). Compares three ways of deciding whether a contract clause entails, contradicts or is neutral to a hypothesis: the LLM classifies the text directly, the LLM classifies after autoformalizing both into first-order logic, and the Z3 SMT solver decides over that formalization; on a hand re-annotated subset of ContractNLI, with five LLMs (abstract; §1; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026limits \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16118) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16118)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/smt\">smt</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/llm-assist\">llm-assist</a></span>", "paper:xu2026replaylens": "- **[ReplayLens: Auditing Agents' Use of Outcomes](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.34177 \"ReplayLens: Auditing Agents' Use of Outcomes (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A black-box audit of what an agent takes from a log of past actions and their scores: it rebuilds the memory from the log with one relationship changed (scores swapped between actions, intact action–score pairs moved to other record slots, actions renamed in both log and menu, or names moved over unchanged scores) and measures how many later decisions change and what that does to externally scored utility (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/xu2026replaylens \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.34177) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34177)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/harness\">harness</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/placebo\">placebo</a></span>", "project:AdalFlow": "- **[AdalFlow](https://github.com/SylphAI-Inc/AdalFlow)**: implements [LLM-AutoDiff (AdalFlow)](#/papers/yin2025llmautodiff \"LLM-AutoDiff: Auto-Differentiate Any LLM Workflow (2025)\")", "project:ADAS": "- **[ADAS](https://github.com/ShengranHu/ADAS)**: implements [ADAS](#/papers/hu2024adas \"Automated Design of Agentic Systems (2024)\")", "project:attention-handoff-tax": "- **[attention-handoff-tax](https://github.com/akshitanchan/attention-handoff-tax)**: Systems (2026)\")", "project:fapo": "- **[fapo](https://github.com/cisco-foundation-ai/fully-automated-prompt-optimization)**: implements [FAPO](#/papers/kassianik2026fapo \"FAPO: Fully Automated Evolution Can Outperform Reinforcement Learning (2026)\")", "project:hallucination-snowball": "- **[hallucination-snowball](https://github.com/prabhjotschugh/hallucination-snowball)**: implements [The **[LEGO-Prover](https://github.com/wiio12/LEGO-Prover)**: implements [LEGO-Prover](#/papers/wang2023legoprover \"LEGO-Prover: Neural Theorem Proving with Growing Libraries (2024)\")", "project:llm-csp": "- **[llm-csp](https://github.com/rikhil-amonkar/llm-csp)**: implements [A Reality Check of…](#/papers/amonkar2025realitycheck \"A Reality Check of Language Models as Formalizers on Constraint Satisfaction Problems (2026)\")", "project:LLM-ToolMaker": "- **[LLM-ToolMaker](https://github.com/ctlllll/LLM-ToolMaker)**: implements [Large Language Models as Tool
