**[llm_lib_learning_fails](https://github.com/ikb-a/llm_lib_learning_fails)**: implements [Is This LLM Library…](#/papers/berlotattwell2025libraryfails \"Is This LLM Library Learning? Evaluation Must Account For Compute and Behaviour (2026)\")", "project:Logic-LLM": "- **[Logic-LLM](https://github.com/teacherpeterpan/Logic-LLM)**: implements [Logic-LM](#/papers/pan2023logiclm \"Logic-LM: Empowering Large Language Models with Symbolic Solvers for Faithful Logical Reasoning (2023)\")", "project:ReTool": "- **[ReTool](https://github.com/ReTool-RL/ReTool)**: implements [ReTool](#/papers/feng2025retool \"ReTool: Reinforcement Learning for Strategic Tool Use in LLMs (2026)\")", "project:roundtrip-autoformalization": "- **[roundtrip-autoformalization](https://github.com/daneshvar-amrollahi/roundtrip-autoformalization)**: implements [Faithful Autoformalization via Roundtrip…](#/papers/amrollahi2026roundtrip \"Faithful Autoformalization via Roundtrip Verification and Repair (2026)\")", "project:Seed-Prover": "- **[Seed-Prover](https://github.com/ByteDance-Seed/Seed-Prover)**: implements [Seed-Prover](#/papers/chen2025seedprover \"Seed-Prover: Deep and Broad Reasoning for Automated Theorem Proving (2025)\")", "paper:dong2025stp": "- **[STP](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.00212 \"STP: Self-play LLM Theorem Provers with Iterative Conjecturing and Proving (2025)\")**: Self-play LLM Theorem Provers with Iterative Conjecturing and Proving (ICML 2025). One model is both conjecturer and prover: conjectures barely provable by the current prover train the conjecturer, and the prover uses expert iteration (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/dong2025stp \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.00212) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.00212) · [Code](https://github.com/kfdong/STP)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:pandey2026beyondanswer": "- **[Beyond the Answer: Decoding the Behavior of LLMs as Scientific Reasoners](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.28038 \"Beyond the Answer: Decoding the Behavior of LLMs as Scientific Reasoners (2026)\")** (Post-AGI Science and Society Workshop at ICLR 2026). Optimizes prompts with a simplified GEPA variant on DeepSeek-V3.2 for Lean proofs of MiniF2F problems (\"Algebra\") and GPQA Diamond multiple-choice science questions, then runs the same prompts on GPT-5.4-mini, GLM 5 and Claude Sonnet 4.6 (abstract; §3, Alg. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/pandey2026beyondanswer \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.28038) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28038)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/itp\">itp</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "project:STP": "- **[STP](https://github.com/kfdong/STP)**: implements [STP](#/papers/dong2025stp \"STP: Self-play LLM Theorem Provers with Iterative Conjecturing and Proving (2025)\")", "paper:huang2024sharpening": "- **[Self-Improvement in Language Models: The Sharpening Mechanism](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.01951 \"Self-Improvement in Language Models: The Sharpening Mechanism (2025)\")** (ICLR 2025). Formalizes self-improvement as \"sharpening\": the model acts as its own verifier (a self-reward such as its own likelihood) to shift probability onto high-quality responses, amortizing inference-time search into post-training (abstract; §1.1, §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/huang2024sharpening \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.01951) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01951)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/theory\">theory</a></span>", "paper:kim2025correlated": "- **[Correlated Errors in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.07962 \"Correlated Errors in Large Language Models (2025)\")** (ICML 2025). Measures how often different LLMs make the same mistake: the rate at which two models give the same wrong answer when both are wrong, over hundreds of models on MMLU questions with labelled answers from the HuggingFace Open LLM Leaderboard and HELM, plus the correlation of residuals against hand labels on LLM resume ratings (abstract; §1; §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/kim2025correlated \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.07962) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07962) · [Code](https://github.com/nikhgarg/llm_correlated_errors_public)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:liu2026jury": "- **[LLMs as a Jury](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.10139 \"LLMs as a Jury: Cross-Model Consensus Can Outperform Process Reward Models for LLM Reasoning (2026)\")**: Cross-Model Consensus Can Outperform Process Reward Models for LLM Reasoning (preprint 2026). Agreement across independently trained models, each solving once, picks the answer from a Best-of-N pool; the authors report it beats self-consistency and self-scoring on seven benchmarks (Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liu2026jury \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.10139) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10139)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:song2024mindgap": "- **[Mind the Gap](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.02674 \"Mind the Gap: Examining the Self-Improvement Capabilities of Large Language Models (2025)\")**: Examining the Self-Improvement Capabilities of Large Language Models (ICLR 2025). A controlled study of self-improvement where a model verifies its own outputs, filters or reweights them, and distills (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/song2024mindgap \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.02674) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.02674)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:sun2024easytohard": "- **[Easy-to-Hard Generalization](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.09472 \"Easy-to-Hard Generalization: Scalable Alignment Beyond Human Supervision (2024)\")**: Scalable Alignment Beyond Human Supervision (NeurIPS 2024). Reward models trained only on MATH levels 1–3 (on final-answer labels, step labels from PRM800K or Math-Shepherd, or both) score solutions to levels 4–5; the authors report that these evaluators generalize from easy to hard better than the generators trained on the same easy data (§4.2.1), and that an easy-trained outcome reward model also helps on hard APPS coding problems (§4.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sun2024easytohard \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.09472) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09472) · [Code](https://github.com/Edward-Sun/easy-to-hard)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:zhang2024genrm": "- **[Generative Verifiers (GenRM)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.15240 \"Generative Verifiers: Reward Modeling as Next-Token Prediction (2025)\")**: Generative Verifiers: Reward Modeling as Next-Token Prediction (ICLR 2025). Reward modeling as next-token prediction: the verifier answers \"Yes\"/\"No\" (§3.1), in its CoT variant after writing a rationale (§3.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2024genrm \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.15240) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15240)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/judge\">judge</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "project:easy-to-hard": "- **[easy-to-hard](https://github.com/Edward-Sun/easy-to-hard)**: implements [Easy-to-Hard Generalization](#/papers/sun2024easytohard \"Easy-to-Hard Generalization: Scalable Alignment Beyond Human Supervision (2024)\")", "project:llm_correlated_errors": "- **[llm_correlated_errors](https://github.com/nikhgarg/llm_correlated_errors_public)**: implements [Correlated Errors in Large Language Models](#/papers/kim2025correlated \"Correlated Errors in Large Language Models (2025)\")", "paper:belcamino2026generalizationgap": "- **[On the Generalization Gap in LLM Planning: Tests and Verifier-Reward RL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.14456 \"On the Generalization Gap in LLM Planning: Tests and Verifier-Reward RL (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Fine-tunes Qwen-3-1.7B to write PDDL plans from a domain and a problem, on generated problems from 10 IPC 2023 domains, and scores it by the share of plans the validator VAL accepts on held-out problems of those domains and on two domains absent from training, Rover and Briefcase (abstract; §3, §5); three diagnostic variants follow: per-tuple symbol anonymization, a compact plan format, and GRPO with a VAL-based reward (§1, §4.1–4.3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/belcamino2026generalizationgap \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.14456) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.14456)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "paper:lyu2026sameanswer": "- **[Same Answer, New Box: How Much of RLVR's Out-of-Domain Gain Is Answer Format?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20639 \"Same Answer, New Box: How Much of RLVR's Out-of-Domain Gain Is Answer Format? (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Measures how much of the out-of-domain gain that answer extractors copied from public code report for released RLVR checkpoints over their base models is a change in where the answer is written rather than in the answers; the author reports that on four released 7B SimpleRL base–RL pairs, \"82% of the out-of-domain multiple-choice gain that these extractors report disappears\" under a fixed extractor of the author's own (abstract; §4.1, Fig. 2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lyu2026sameanswer \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20639) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20639)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/labels\">labels</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "paper:agrawal2025gepa": "- **[GEPA](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.19457 \"GEPA: Reflective Prompt Evolution Can Outperform Reinforcement Learning (2026)\")**: Reflective Prompt Evolution Can Outperform Reinforcement Learning (ICLR 2026). Reflective prompt evolution with Pareto selection. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/agrawal2025gepa \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.19457) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.19457) · [Code: gepa](https://github.com/gepa-ai/gepa) · [Code: gepa-ai](https://github.com/gepa-ai/dspy) · [Code: gepa-artifact](https://github.com/gepa-ai/gepa-artifact)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:balachandran2026discern": "- **[DISCERN (\"Pay Only for Disagreement\")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.17560 \"Pay Only for Disagreement: Certified No-Regression Verdicts for Model Updates with Matching Label-Complexity Bounds (2026)\")**: Pay Only for Disagreement: Certified No-Regression Verdicts for Model Updates with Matching Label-Complexity Bounds (preprint 2026). Certifies whether a model update (retrain, fine-tune, quantization, vendor swap) is a regression: a sequential audit of the paired risk difference between old and new model, with anytime-valid confidence sequences, valid under any predictable label-routing rule (abstract; §4; §5.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/balachandran2026discern \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.17560) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17560)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a></span>", "paper:bowyer2025clt": "- **[Position: Don't Use the CLT in LLM Evals With Fewer Than a Few Hundred Datapoints](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.01747 \"Position: Don't Use the CLT in LLM Evals With Fewer Than a Few Hundred Datapoints (2025)\")** (ICML 2025 (spotlight position paper)). Argues that error bars from the central limit theorem fail for LLM evals with few questions, with clustered questions, when comparing two models, and for metrics that are not averages such as F1, and recommends Wilson score or Bayesian Beta–Bernoulli intervals for one model on independent questions and Bayesian models for clustered and paired data (abstract; §3.1–3.5). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/bowyer2025clt \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.01747) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01747) · [Code: bayes_evals](https://github.com/sambowyer/bayes_evals) · [Code: no_clt_paper](https://github.com/sambowyer/no_clt_paper)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/survey\">survey</a></span>", "paper:chang2024promptsurvey": "- **[Efficient Prompting Methods for Large Language Models: A Survey](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.01077 \"Efficient Prompting Methods for Large Language Models: A Survey (2024)\")** (preprint 2024). Surveys prompt compression and automatic prompt optimization. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chang2024promptsurvey \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.01077) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01077)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/survey\">survey</a></span>", "paper:chen2026attribution": "- **[Learning from Prompt itself: the Hierarchical Attribution Prompt Optimization](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.02683 \"Learning from Prompt itself: the Hierarchical Attribution Prompt Optimization (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A prompt optimizer that targets \"prompt drift\", where new prompts fix earlier failures but break earlier successes (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026attribution \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.02683) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.02683)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:cheng2023bpo": "- **[BPO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.04155 \"Black-Box Prompt Optimization: Aligning Large Language Models without Model Training (2024)\")**: Black-Box Prompt Optimization: Aligning Large Language Models without Model Training (ACL 2024). Trains a small model that rewrites user prompts so a black-box LLM answers better. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/cheng2023bpo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.04155) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.04155) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.acl-long.176) · [Code](https://github.com/thu-coai/BPO)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a></span>", "paper:fernando2023promptbreeder": "- **[Promptbreeder](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.16797 \"Promptbreeder: Self-Referential Self-Improvement Via Prompt Evolution (2023)\")**: Self-Referential Self-Improvement Via Prompt Evolution (preprint, 2023). Evolves task prompts and the mutation prompts that change them. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/fernando2023promptbreeder \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.16797) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16797)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:gao2022pal": "- **[PAL (Program-aided Language Models)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.10435 \"PAL: Program-aided Language Models (2022)\")**: PAL: Program-aided Language Models (preprint 2022). Few-shot prompts make the LLM write its reasoning steps as a Python program; a Python interpreter runs it and its result is the answer (abstract, §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/gao2022pal \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.10435) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.10435) · [Code](https://github.com/reasoning-machines/pal)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:gong2026whypo": "- **[Why Prompt Optimization Works, and Why It Sometimes Doesn't: A Causal-Inspired Edit-Level Analysis](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.26655 \"Why Prompt Optimization Works, and Why It Sometimes Doesn't: A Causal-Inspired Edit-Level Analysis (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Asks which kinds of edits prompt optimizers (DSPy with MIPROv2, TextGrad, GEPA) make go with gains or losses on which task groups (commonsense, math, logical, sequential, multi-hop), from the optimization logs of five LLM backbones on 11 benchmarks (abstract; §1; §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/gong2026whypo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.26655) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26655)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:guo2023evoprompt": "- **[EvoPrompt](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.08532 \"EvoPrompt: Connecting LLMs with Evolutionary Algorithms Yields Powerful Prompt Optimizers (2024)\")**: Connecting LLMs with Evolutionary Algorithms Yields Powerful Prompt Optimizers (ICLR 2024). LLMs act as crossover and mutation operators (GA and DE) over a prompt population. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/guo2023evoprompt \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.08532) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.08532) · [Code](https://github.com/beeevita/EvoPrompt)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:hase2024easydata": "- **[The Unreasonable Effectiveness of Easy Training Data for Hard Tasks](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2401.06751 \"The Unreasonable Effectiveness of Easy Training Data for Hard Tasks (2024)\")** (ACL 2024). Fits language models to easy questions only and tests them on hard ones, against a zero-shot lower bound and a hard-data upper bound; easy and hard are set by six human measures (grade level, expert rating, Bloom skill, question and answer length, reasoning steps) and a model-based MDL score, on ARC, MMLU-STEM-5, StrategyQA and GSM8k (§1, §3, Tab. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/hase2024easydata \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2401.06751) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06751) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.acl-long.378) · [Code](https://github.com/allenai/easy-to-hard-generalization)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:hochlehnert2025sober": "- **[A Sober Look at Progress in Language Model Reasoning: Pitfalls and Paths to Reproducibility](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.07086 \"A Sober Look at Progress in Language Model Reasoning: Pitfalls and Paths to Reproducibility (2025)\")** (COLM 2025). Measures how far math-reasoning scores of open 1.5B and 7B reasoning models move with evaluation choices alone: random seed, temperature and top-p, output-token limit, prompt template, hardware and evaluation framework, on AIME'24, AMC'23 and MATH500 (§3.1–3.4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/hochlehnert2025sober \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.07086) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07086) · [Code](https://github.com/bethgelab/sober-reasoning)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:hong2025contextrot": "- **[Context Rot](#/papers/hong2025contextrot \"Context Rot: How Increasing Input Tokens Impacts LLM Performance (2025)\")**: How Increasing Input Tokens Impacts LLM Performance (Chroma technical report 2025). Measures 18 LLMs as input length grows on tasks whose difficulty is held fixed: needle-in-a-haystack variants (needle–question similarity, distractors, needle–haystack similarity, shuffled against coherent haystacks), LongMemEval chat-history questions with only the relevant parts or the full history, and copying a run of repeated words (PDF pp. 3–5, 9–10, 24). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/hong2025contextrot \"Summary\") · [Paper](https://www.trychroma.com/research/context-rot) · [Code](https://github.com/chroma-core/context-rot)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/longctx\">longctx</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:huan2025mathtransfer": "- **[Does Math Reasoning Improve General LLM Capabilities? Understanding Transferability of LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.00432 \"Does Math Reasoning Improve General LLM Capabilities? Understanding Transferability of LLM Reasoning (2025)\")** (preprint 2025). Asks whether math-reasoning gains carry over to other reasoning tasks (science, coding, planning, medical questions) and to non-reasoning ones (conversational QA, instruction following, hallucination detection, temporal common sense); the authors report that \"most models that succeed in math fail to transfer their gains to other domains\" (abstract) and name the fine-tuning paradigm, RL or SFT, as the factor that \"consistently predicts transferability\" (§1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/huan2025mathtransfer \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.00432) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00432) · [Code](https://github.com/ReasoningTransfer/Transferability-of-LLM-Reasoning)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "paper:jones2023syntra": "- **[SynTra (Synthetic Transfer)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.06827 \"Teaching Language Models to Hallucinate Less with Synthetic Tasks (2023)\")**: Teaching Language Models to Hallucinate Less with Synthetic Tasks (preprint 2023). Lowers hallucination on tasks that answer or summarize from a given context by optimizing on a synthetic task where a program checks every answer (names retrieval: list the first five names, from a list of 100 random names, whose first name starts with a given letter; a name not on the list is a hallucination), then reusing the result on realistic tasks (abstract; §2.2, §3.1.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/jones2023syntra \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.06827) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.06827)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/transfer\">transfer</a></span>", "paper:khattab2023dspy": "- **[DSPy](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.03714 \"DSPy: Compiling Declarative Language Model Calls into Self-Improving Pipelines (2024)\")**: Compiling Declarative Language Model Calls into Self-Improving Pipelines (ICLR 2024). Declarative LM pipelines with a compiler that bootstraps and selects few-shot demonstrations (or finetunes) to maximize a metric; instructions are named as parameters, but this version focuses on demonstrations (§4). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/khattab2023dspy \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.03714) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.03714) · [Code](https://github.com/stanfordnlp/dspy)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:lee2025feedbackdescent": "- **[Feedback Descent](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.07919 \"Feedback Descent: Open-Ended Text Optimization via Pairwise Comparison (2025)\")**: Open-Ended Text Optimization via Pairwise Comparison (preprint 2025). Optimizes text artifacts (prompts, code, molecules) with pairwise comparisons that carry textual critiques. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/lee2025feedbackdescent \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.07919) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.07919)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:li2025cevolve": "- **[C-Evolve](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.23331 \"C-Evolve: Consensus-based Evolution for Prompt Groups (2025)\")**: Consensus-based Evolution for Prompt Groups (preprint 2025). Evolves a group of prompts whose majority vote is best, with island-based diversity. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2025cevolve \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.23331) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23331)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:li2026dynamicskills": "- **[Dynamic Agent Skills: A Lifecycle Survey and Taxonomy of Evolving Skill Libraries](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.10113 \"Dynamic Agent Skills: A Lifecycle Survey and Taxonomy of Evolving Skill Libraries (2026)\")** (TMLR 2026). Surveys how LLM agents' skill libraries (code functions, natural-language instructions, SKILL.md packages, workflow graphs, learned adapters) change over time, across what it calls a 124-paper 2023–2026 audit set (abstract; §2.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/li2026dynamicskills \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.10113) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10113)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/survey\">survey</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a></span>", "paper:liu2023lostmiddle": "- **[Lost in the Middle](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.03172 \"Lost in the Middle: How Language Models Use Long Contexts (2024)\")**: How Language Models Use Long Contexts (TACL 2024). Measures how six LLMs (§2.2) use long inputs by moving the one relevant item through the context at fixed lengths: the answer passage among 10, 20 or 30 retrieved Wikipedia passages (multi-document QA on NaturalQuestions-Open), and the queried key among 75, 140 or 300 JSON key-value pairs of random UUIDs (§2.1, §2.3, §3.1–3.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liu2023lostmiddle \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.03172) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.03172) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1162/tacl_a_00638) · [Code](https://github.com/nelson-liu/lost-in-the-middle)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/longctx\">longctx</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:liu2026espo": "- **[ESPO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.04197 \"ESPO: Error-Structured Prompt Optimization via Diagnose, Diversify, and Stabilize (2026)\")**: Error-Structured Prompt Optimization via Diagnose, Diversify, and Stabilize (EMNLP 2026). Error-structured prompt optimization: diagnose all errors at once, propose with four strategies, select by bootstrap stability. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/liu2026espo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.04197) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04197)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:muennighoff2025simple": "- **[s1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.19393 \"s1: Simple test-time scaling (2025)\")**: Simple test-time scaling (EMNLP 2025). Budget forcing: end or extend the model's thinking to control compute. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/muennighoff2025simple \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.19393) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.19393) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.emnlp-main.1025) · [Code](https://github.com/simplescaling/s1)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:nath2026codebooks": "- **[Prompt Codebooks (PCO)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.28360 \"Prompt Codebooks: Discrete Compositional Optimization for Language Model Instruction Refinement (2026)\")**: Prompt Codebooks: Discrete Compositional Optimization for Language Model Instruction Refinement (preprint 2026). A codebook of reusable instruction units; an encoder routes each input to a few, a generator composes the prompt. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/nath2026codebooks \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.28360) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28360)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:oved2026crosstier": "- **[Optimize Cheap](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.10694 \"Optimize Cheap, Deploy Strong: Cost-Aware Cross-Tier Transfer for Evolutionary Optimization (2026)\")**: Optimize Cheap, Deploy Strong: Cost-Aware Cross-Tier Transfer for Evolutionary Optimization (Deploy Strong, preprint 2026). Runs evolutionary prompt search with a cheap answering model, a strong reflector, and deploys on a stronger model. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/oved2026crosstier \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.10694) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10694)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:pryzant2023protegi": "- **[ProTeGi](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.03495 \"Automatic Prompt Optimization with 'Gradient Descent' and Beam Search (2023)\")**: Automatic Prompt Optimization with \"Gradient Descent\" and Beam Search (EMNLP 2023). Natural-language \"gradients\": an LLM criticizes a prompt on errors and edits it. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/pryzant2023protegi \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.03495) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.03495) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.emnlp-main.494) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/LMOps)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:ramirez2026vfr": "- **[Resource-Aware Neuro-Symbolic Reasoning for Local Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.27281 \"Resource-Aware Neuro-Symbolic Reasoning for Local Small Language Models (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A small model run locally translates an ordering problem, in one call, into a typed finite-domain program of entities, safe Horn-style rules and pairwise or fixed-position constraints, each with the source text it comes from; a check that every constraint is grounded in that text, deterministic local repairs, and an exact solver then produce the answer, in place of repeated sampling (abstract; §5; §3 \"Operational semantics of the typed fragment\"). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/ramirez2026vfr \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.27281) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27281)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a></span>", "paper:schnabel2024sammo": "- **[SAMMO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.02319 \"Symbolic Prompt Program Search: A Structure-Aware Approach to Efficient Compile-Time Prompt Optimization (2024)\")**: Symbolic Prompt Program Search: A Structure-Aware Approach to Efficient Compile-Time Prompt Optimization (Findings of EMNLP 2024). Compile-time search over prompt programs represented symbolically (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/schnabel2024sammo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.02319) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02319) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.findings-emnlp.37) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/sammo)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:shao2024deepseekmath": "- **[DeepSeekMath](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.03300 \"DeepSeekMath: Pushing the Limits of Mathematical Reasoning in Open Language Models (2024)\")**: Pushing the Limits of Mathematical Reasoning in Open Language Models (preprint 2024). Math pretraining on web data, then RL. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/shao2024deepseekmath \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.03300) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03300) · [Code](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-Math)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:shin2020autoprompt": "- **[AutoPrompt](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2010.15980 \"AutoPrompt: Eliciting Knowledge from Language Models with Automatically Generated Prompts (2020)\")**: Eliciting Knowledge from Language Models with Automatically Generated Prompts (EMNLP 2020). Searches discrete trigger tokens that elicit knowledge from masked language models. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/shin2020autoprompt \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2010.15980) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.15980) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2020.emnlp-main.346) · [Code](https://github.com/ucinlp/autoprompt)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a></span>", "paper:singh2026mage": "- **[MAGE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.11944 \"MAGE: Understanding Stability-Performance Trade-offs in Multi-component Prompt Optimization (2026)\")**: Understanding Stability-Performance Trade-offs in Multi-component Prompt Optimization (preprint 2026). A controlled platform for how memory, Pareto selection and adaptive evaluation interact in reflective optimization. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/singh2026mage \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.11944) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11944)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:song2026sketchops": "- **[SketchOps](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.26448 \"Mergeable Model-Side Aggregation States for Long-Context Language Models (2026)\")**: Mergeable Model-Side Aggregation States for Long-Context Language Models (preprint 2026). Keeps fixed-size HyperLogLog sketch states beside a frozen LLM for set-based aggregation over long inputs (distinct counts, union size, Jaccard similarity, containment, grouped distinct counts; a Count–Min state for point frequencies) and appends their readouts to the model's input as evidence for its final decision, without \"an additional generate–execute–return cycle\" (abstract; §4; App. D). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/song2026sketchops \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.26448) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26448) · [Code](https://github.com/songdc98/sketchops)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a></span>", "paper:sordoni2023dln": "- **[Deep Language Networks](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.12509 \"Joint Prompt Optimization of Stacked LLMs using Variational Inference (2023)\")**: Joint Prompt Optimization of Stacked LLMs using Variational Inference (NeurIPS 2023). Two stacked LLM calls treated as layers whose prompts are learned jointly. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/sordoni2023dln \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.12509) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.12509) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/deep-language-networks)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:tong2026codevslanguage": "- **[Is Code Better Than Language for Algorithmic Reasoning?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.15589 \"Is Code Better Than Language for Algorithmic Reasoning? (2026)\")** ((three-route framework), ICML 2026 (PMLR 306)). Separates the two things that change when an LLM switches from reasoning in text to writing code that Python runs: the form of the reasoning trace and who executes it. A middle route has the model write code and then simulate running it in text, so text reasoning (Route 1) and code simulated by the model (Route 2) differ only in the trace, and Route 2 and the same code run in Python (Route 3) only in the executor (abstract; §1, Fig. 1; §2.3; §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/tong2026codevslanguage \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.15589) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15589) · [Code](https://github.com/TerryTong-Git/ToolProj)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:wang2022selfconsistency": "- **[Self-Consistency](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.11171 \"Self-Consistency Improves Chain of Thought Reasoning in Language Models (2023)\")**: Self-Consistency Improves Chain of Thought Reasoning in Language Models (ICLR 2023). Sample several reasoning paths and take the majority answer. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2022selfconsistency \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.11171) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.11171)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:wang2025maestro": "- **[Maestro](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.04642 \"Maestro: Joint Graph & Config Optimization for Reliable AI Agents (2025)\")**: Joint Graph & Config Optimization for Reliable AI Agents (preprint 2025 (RELAI technical report)). Jointly searches an agent's graph (modules, flow) and each node's configuration. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/wang2025maestro \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.04642) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04642)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:xiong2026dive": "- **[DIVE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.12486 \"DIVE: Unlocking Self-Improvement in Frozen Language Models Through Diversity-Driven Skill Evolution (2026)\")**: Unlocking Self-Improvement in Frozen Language Models Through Diversity-Driven Skill Evolution (preprint 2026). Evolves several populations of natural-language skills from experience and verifier feedback. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/xiong2026dive \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.12486) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12486)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-context\">promptopt-context</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:xu2026phantomgains": "- **[Phantom Gains](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.20290 \"Phantom Gains: Auditing Self-Improvement Against a Measured Null (2026)\")**: Auditing Self-Improvement Against a Measured Null (preprint 2026 (under review, venue not named)). Audits per-problem claims about self-improvement (which problems a model learned, lost or newly reached) by passing a frozen, untrained model through the same pipeline as every trained arm, so that any transition the frozen model shows is a measurement artifact (abstract; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/xu2026phantomgains \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.20290) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20290) · [Code](https://github.com/chengxuphd/phantom-gains)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:xu2026siren": "- **[Towards Reliable LLM Evaluation: Correcting the Winner's Curse in Adaptive Benchmarking](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.05973 \"Towards Reliable LLM Evaluation: Correcting the Winner's Curse in Adaptive Benchmarking (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). A reporting protocol for scores after prompt or program search. Its target is the fresh-data performance of the whole tune-then-deploy procedure at each tuning budget, not the winning artifact's score (§2). It freezes the shortlist the search returned and, on a separate evaluation pool, repeatedly splits the items into a scoring part that sets smooth selection weights (softmax by default, §3.3, §4) and a held-out part that scores the result, with an item-level Gaussian multiplier bootstrap for intervals and simultaneous bands over budgets (abstract; §3.1–3.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/xu2026siren \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.05973) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05973) · [Code](https://github.com/jefferyz001/siren)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a></span>", "paper:yang2023opro": "- **[OPRO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.03409 \"Large Language Models as Optimizers (2024)\")**: Large Language Models as Optimizers (ICLR 2024). An LLM is the optimizer: it sees past prompts with their scores and proposes better ones. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yang2023opro \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.03409) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.03409) · [Code](https://github.com/google-deepmind/opro)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:yang2026leaderboard": "- **[How Sensitive Are LLM Leaderboard Claims to Hidden Model Selection?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.28177 \"How Sensitive Are LLM Leaderboard Claims to Hidden Model Selection? (2026)\")** (preprint 2026 (v2)). Asks how many privately scored sibling variants a published leaderboard margin over a fixed comparator can survive: under a Gaussian margin model it derives the least-favorable null of the selected margin and inverts it into a per-claim sensitivity curve, the largest hidden count k̄ as a function of a lower bound on the siblings' correlation (abstract; §1; §3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026leaderboard \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.28177) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28177)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:yang2026tallymem": "- **[When Agent Memory Needs to Compute: Evidence Access and Executable Aggregation](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19474 \"When Agent Memory Needs to Compute: Evidence Access and Executable Aggregation (2026)\")** (preprint 2026). Answers questions about an agent's logged history that combine many events (e.g. how often an action was taken): Trajectory Memory (TM) keeps the trajectory verbatim and shows a budgeted, order-preserving view chosen at question time, and TallyMem adds an event ledger of every step, over which a model-written event specification (scope, event predicate, operation) is run by a deterministic executor whose result and supporting step IDs join TM's view (abstract; §4; §6). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026tallymem \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19474) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19474)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/longctx\">longctx</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/offload\">offload</a></span>", "paper:yao2023tot": "- **[Tree of Thoughts](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.10601 \"Tree of Thoughts: Deliberate Problem Solving with Large Language Models (2023)\")**: Deliberate Problem Solving with Large Language Models (NeurIPS 2023). Search over intermediate thoughts with self-evaluation, BFS or DFS. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yao2023tot \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.10601) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.10601) · [Code](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/tree-of-thought-llm)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/scaling\">scaling</a></span>", "paper:yin2025llmautodiff": "- **[LLM-AutoDiff (AdalFlow)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.16673 \"LLM-AutoDiff: Auto-Differentiate Any LLM Workflow (2025)\")**: LLM-AutoDiff: Auto-Differentiate Any LLM Workflow (preprint 2025). Extends textual gradients to pipelines of several LLM calls and functional nodes, including cyclic ones (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yin2025llmautodiff \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.16673) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.16673) · [Code](https://github.com/SylphAI-Inc/AdalFlow)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:yu2025sipdo": "- **[SIPDO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.19514 \"SIPDO: Closed-Loop Prompt Optimization via Synthetic Data Feedback (2025)\")**: Closed-Loop Prompt Optimization via Synthetic Data Feedback (preprint 2025). A closed loop in which a synthetic data generator writes examples that expose the current prompt's weaknesses and a prompt optimizer revises the prompt (abstract). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/yu2025sipdo \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.19514) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19514)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-feedback\">promptopt-feedback</a></span>", "paper:zelikman2022star": "- **[STaR (Self-Taught Reasoner)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.14465 \"STaR: Bootstrapping Reasoning With Reasoning (2022)\")**: STaR: Bootstrapping Reasoning With Reasoning (NeurIPS 2022). A few-shot-prompted LLM writes rationales for many questions; only those whose final answer matches the dataset's answer are kept, the model is fine-tuned on them, and the loop repeats (abstract; §3.1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zelikman2022star \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.14465) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.14465) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.52202/068431-1126) · [Code](https://github.com/ezelikman/STaR)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/rlvr\">rlvr</a></span>", "paper:zhang2024opro": "- **[Revisiting OPRO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.10276 \"Revisiting OPRO: The Limitations of Small-Scale LLMs as Optimizers (2024)\")**: The Limitations of Small-Scale LLMs as Optimizers (Findings of ACL 2024). Reruns OPRO with small LLMs (the LLaMA-2 family, Mistral 7B) as both optimizer and scorer (abstract; §3.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2024opro \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.10276) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10276) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.findings-acl.100)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:zhang2026positional": "- **[Positional Failures in Long-Context LLMs](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23170 \"Positional Failures in Long-Context LLMs: A Blind Spot in Reasoning Benchmarks (2026)\")**: A Blind Spot in Reasoning Benchmarks ((CRE), preprint 2026). Audits 11 long-context benchmarks for whether they control where the task sits in a long input (App. F, Tab. 22), and the official sources of four recent flagship releases for whether position-controlled benchmarks appear in their main result tables (App. G, Tab. 23); then moves a GSM8K question from the end to the middle of an 8K, 32K or 64K-token context of filler of three kinds, for nine API models, with ARC-Challenge questions at 8K only (§3, §4.4, Tab. 3). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026positional \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23170) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23170)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/longctx\">longctx</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a></span>", "paper:zhou2022ape": "- **[APE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.01910 \"Large Language Models Are Human-Level Prompt Engineers (2023)\")**: Large Language Models Are Human-Level Prompt Engineers (ICLR 2023). An LLM proposes instructions from input–output examples; the best-scoring one is kept. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2022ape \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.01910) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.01910) · [Code](https://github.com/keirp/automatic_prompt_engineer)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a></span>", "paper:zhou2024selfdiscover": "- **[Self-Discover](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.03620 \"SELF-DISCOVER: Large Language Models Self-Compose Reasoning Structures (2024)\")**: SELF-DISCOVER: Large Language Models Self-Compose Reasoning Structures (NeurIPS 2024). The model selects, adapts and composes reasoning modules into an explicit reasoning structure once per task, then follows it on every instance of the task (abstract; §2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2024selfdiscover \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.03620) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03620) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.52202/079017-4004)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt\">promptopt</a></span>", "paper:zhou2026reflector": "- **[What Should the Reflector See?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.32452 \"What Should the Reflector See? An Empirical Study of Evidence in Reflective Prompt Optimization (2026)\")**: What Should the Reflector See? An Empirical Study of Evidence in Reflective Prompt Optimization (preprint 2026). Compares nine reflection strategies (evidence composition, example visibility, candidate selection, knowledge policy; abstract) in a GEPA-inspired search. <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2026reflector \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.32452) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32452)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/math\">math</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/nl\">nl</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/study\">study</a><a class=\"tag sub\" href=\"#/tags/promptopt-evolve\">promptopt-evolve</a></span>", "paper:ziems2025mmgrpo": "- **[mmGRPO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.04660 \"Composing Policy Gradients and Prompt Optimization for Language Model Programs (2026)\")**: Composing Policy Gradients and Prompt Optimization for Language Model Programs (ACM CAIS 2026). [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04660) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3786335.3813164)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/llm\">llm</a><a class=\"tag\" Gives algorithms that let an analyst validate many adaptively chosen hypotheses against one holdout set without overfitting to it: Thresholdout answers with the training-set value unless it differs from the holdout value by more than a noisy threshold, so a hypothesis that does not overfit reveals little about the holdout set; SparseValidate answers arbitrary Boolean queries and is analysed by description length (abstract; §1.1; §4.1–4.2). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/dwork2015holdout \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1506.02629) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1506.02629)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a></span>", "paper:smith2006optimizerscurse": "- **[The Optimizer's Curse](https://jimsmith.host.dartmouth.edu/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/The_Optimizers_Curse.pdf \"The Optimizer's Curse: Skepticism and Postdecision Surprise in Decision Analysis (2006)\")**: Skepticism and Postdecision Surprise in Decision Analysis (Management Science 2006). Shows that choosing the alternative with the highest estimated value makes that estimate biased high even when every estimate is unbiased, so a decision maker should expect to be disappointed on average (abstract; §2.3, Prop. 1). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/smith2006optimizerscurse \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://jimsmith.host.dartmouth.edu/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/The_Optimizers_Curse.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.1050.0451)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/method\">method</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/stats\">stats</a><a class=\"tag\" href=\"#/tags/theory\">theory</a></span>", "project:nltk_data": "- **nltk_data**", "paper:hopcroft1971linear": "- **[A Linear Algorithm for Testing Equivalence of Finite Automata](https://ecommons.cornell.edu/handle/1813/5958 \"A Linear Algorithm for Testing Equivalence of Finite Automata (1971)\")** (Cornell University technical report TR 71-114, December 1971). Decides whether two finite automata with start states (deterministic in later terms: δ maps each state and symbol to one state, §II, PDF p. 4) accept the same strings, in time the authors bound by a constant times the size of the input alphabet times the number of states of the larger automaton (abstract, PDF p. 2; Thm. 1, PDF p. 6, gives the sum of both automata's states), on a random access computer (§IV, PDF p. 6). <span class=\"links\">[🔎](#/papers/hopcroft1971linear \"Summary\") · [PDF](https://ecommons.cornell.edu/handle/1813/5958)</span> <span class=\"tags\"><a class=\"tag\"
